8. Domain Model

8.1 Core Entity Taxonomy

Entity Type Cardinality Owns
Repo Root aggregate 1..N repos per index N docs
Doc Content unit 1..M per repo trigrams, symbols, metas
Trigram Index key ≤16,777,216 distinct posting list per shard
Posting DocId in a trigram's list O(docs × avg_trigrams) encoded bytes in shard
Shard Immutable on-disk unit grows via compaction trigram table + postings + doc-meta
Manifest Atomic shard registry exactly one active ordered shard set + deletion markers
SymbolEntry Named definition 1..K per doc name, kind, line
QueryAst Parsed search intent 1 per query literals, regex Hir, filters
TrigramQuery Boolean over trigrams derived from QueryAst AND/OR/Trigram/All/Any
MatchSpan Verified match location 0..R per candidate line, col, len, line_text_ref
SearchResult Final ranked hit top-k per query DocMeta + spans + RankScore

8.2 Trigram Packing — Bijection and Representation

Pack:  T = (b0 << 16) | (b1 << 8) | b2    where b0,b1,b2 ∈ [0,255]
Unpack: b0 = (T >> 16) & 0xFF
        b1 = (T >>  8) & 0xFF
        b2 =  T        & 0xFF
Domain: T ∈ [0, 16_777_215]  (24-bit unsigned)
  • I8: pack/unpack is bijective; no two 3-byte sequences map to same u32.
  • Trigram(u32) stores raw bytes, never code-points; indexing is byte-oriented.
  • Case-fold variant: for case-insensitive mode, emit both original and to_ascii_lowercase trigrams; no separate fold field; soundness preserved because superset generated.
  • Boundary handling: file shorter than 3 bytes → zero trigrams extracted; file of exactly 3 bytes → exactly one trigram.

8.3 Posting List: Storage Layout and Encoding

On-disk layout (per trigram, within shard):
  [varint: n_postings]
  [varint: first DocId]
  [varint: delta_1]  (DocId[1] - DocId[0])
  [varint: delta_2]  (DocId[2] - DocId[1])
  ...
  • Lists are strictly ascending (I2); delta is always ≥ 1.
  • Varint encoding: little-endian base-128 (continuation bit = bit 7).
  • PostingReader iterates by decoding running sum; no random access within list.
  • PostingWriter accumulates sorted DocIds, encodes on flush.
  • Galloping intersection (Phase 8): skip-pointer advances when gap between iterators is large; O(n log(max/min)) vs O(n) for dense lists.

Worked Example — 4-doc posting list for trigram "int":

DocIds: [3, 7, 12, 20]
Deltas: [3, 4, 5, 8]
Varint bytes: 04 03 04 05 08   (first byte = count=4)

Failure path — corrupt varint (continuation bit set on final byte):

  • Decoder returns Err(PostingDecodeError::UnterminatedVarint).
  • Shard reader closes the list; candidate doc is skipped (safe: may miss a match from corrupt data, flagged via metrics).
  • CRC mismatch on shard open → Err(ShardError::CrcMismatch) → shard excluded from fan-out; error logged.

8.4 Shard On-Disk Layout

Byte offset  Field
0            magic: [u8;8] = b"CSEARCH1"
8            version: u32
12           n_docs: u32
16           n_trigrams: u32
20           trigram_table_off: u64   -- absolute offset to trigram index
28           doc_table_off: u64       -- absolute offset to doc-meta table
36           postings_off: u64        -- absolute offset to posting-list blob
44           crc32: u32               -- CRC of bytes [0..44)
48           [trigram table]          -- sorted array of (Trigram: u24, list_offset: u40) pairs, 8 bytes each
             [posting-list blob]      -- concatenated encoded lists
             [doc-meta table]         -- length-prefixed CBOR records, one per DocId (ascending)
  • Trigram table: binary-searched by Trigram value; O(log n_trigrams) lookup.
  • Doc-meta table offset indexed by DocId (variable-length → an auxiliary offset array at doc_table_off).
  • Shard is written once; sealed with CRC; never mutated (I3).
  • mmap opens read-only; PostingList<'a> holds &'a [u8] slice into the mapped region (P5 zero-copy).

8.5 Manifest Format

{
  "version": 1,
  "shards": [
    { "shard_id": 1, "path": "shard-0001.csi", "repo_ids": [1,2], "generation": 3 },
    ...
  ],
  "deleted_docs": [
    { "shard_id": 1, "doc_id": 42 }
  ],
  "created_at": "<unix-ms>"
}
  • Written atomically: new file → fsync → rename over old (I7).
  • deleted_docs list filters superseded docs at query time until compaction (I9).
  • generation counter increments on each rebuild or merge; monotonically increasing.
  • Hot-reload in csearch-server: inotify/kqueue watch on manifest path; swap active shard registry under a read-write lock.

8.6 QueryAst and Filter Grammar (EBNF)

query       ::= clause (WS clause)*
clause      ::= filter_expr | term
filter_expr ::= field ':' value
field       ::= 'file' | 'lang' | 'repo' | 'case' | 'sym'
value       ::= bare_word | quoted_string | '/' regex_body '/'
term        ::= quoted_string | bare_word | '/' regex_body '/'
bare_word   ::= [^ \t\n:/]+
quoted_string ::= '"' ([^"\\] | '\\' .)* '"'
regex_body  ::= ([^/\\] | '\\' .)*
  • Implicit AND across space-separated clauses.
  • case:yes / case:no toggles ASCII case-folding globally for the query.
  • sym:Name routes to symbol posting index (Phase 10), bypasses trigram scan.
  • file: and lang: applied as post-candidate DocMeta filters (no trigram overhead).
  • Malformed query (unclosed /, unrecognized field) → QueryParseError with byte offset; HTTP 400.

8.7 TrigramQuery Lowering State Machine

Regex Hir node         TrigramQuery produced
-----------------      --------------------------------
Literal(s), |s|≥3      And(trigrams(s))        -- all overlapping trigrams required
Literal(s), |s|<3      All                     -- no constraint extractable; sound (P2)
Concat(a, b)           And(lower(a), lower(b))
Alternation(a,b,..)    Or(lower(a), lower(b), ..)
Repetition(min=0,..)   All                     -- may match zero chars; no guarantee
Repetition(min≥1, e)   lower(e)
Dot / AnyByte          All
Class(ranges)          All  (conservative; could enumerate if small)
  • And(All, x) simplifies to x; Or(All, x) simplifies to All.
  • And([]) = All; Or([]) = Any (vacuously false — no doc matches).
  • Soundness obligation (I1): lower(R) must be satisfied by every doc D where R matches D. Verified by fuzz corpus (Phase 14).

Worked Example — literal "struct Foo":

trigrams: "str","tru","ruc","uct"," Fo","Foo"
TrigramQuery: And(["str","tru","ruc","uct"," Fo","Foo"])  (encoded as Trigram(u32) values)

Worked Example — regex /err|warn/:

lower("err") = And(["err"])
lower("warn") = And(["war","arn"])
TrigramQuery: Or(And(["err"]), And(["war","arn"]))

Edge case — regex /.*/ : lowers to All; every shard is scanned (full-scan path); result cap + deadline apply (P6).

Edge case — regex /a{0,}/: min=0 → All; same as .*.

Failure path — trigram query returns empty candidate set: valid result; zero SearchResults; not an error.

8.8 Search Execution Sequence

Client               csearch-server          csearch-search          csearch-shard (×N)
  |--POST /search-------->|                       |                         |
  |                       |--parse QueryAst------->|                         |
  |                       |                       |--lower TrigramQuery----->|
  |                       |                       |<--PostingList iterators--|
  |                       |                       |--gallop intersect------->|
  |                       |                       |<--candidate DocIds-------|
  |                       |                       |--apply DocMeta filters-->|
  |                       |                       |--verify with PikeVm----->|
  |                       |                       |<--MatchSpans-------------|
  |                       |                       |--rank + top-k----------->|
  |                       |<--SearchResults--------|                         |
  |<--JSON response--------|                       |                         |
  • Fan-out across shards is concurrent (tokio tasks per shard); first shard to fill result cap cancels others (P6).
  • Deadline carried as Instant; each stage checks before proceeding.
  • Metrics emitted per query: shard_count, candidate_count, verified_count, false_positive_ratio, latency_ms per stage (P10).

8.9 Thompson NFA / Pike VM Execution Model

NFA state set: S ⊆ {0..num_states}
At each byte b of input text:
  next(S, b) = ε-closure( { t | ∃s∈S, (s,b)→t ∈ transitions } )
  S ← next(S, b)
Match: accept_state ∈ S after processing input prefix
Submatch capture: Pike VM threads carry capture vectors; priority = leftmost-longest
  • Compile once per query; reuse NFA across all candidate docs (P3).
  • NFA size bounded by regex AST node count; no exponential blowup.
  • I4: O(len(text) × |NFA|) per doc; |NFA| fixed at compile time.
  • Anchors: ^ = start from position 0 only; $ = accept only at newline/EOF.
  • Line/col extraction: scan backward from match start offset to prior newline.

8.10 Incremental Indexing State Transitions

State: {CLEAN, DIRTY, BUILDING, MERGING}

CLEAN  --file_changed(repo)--> DIRTY
DIRTY  --trigger_build-------> BUILDING
BUILDING --shard_written-----> CLEAN  (manifest updated; old shard superseded)
CLEAN  --merge_threshold met-> MERGING
MERGING --compacted_shard-----> CLEAN  (merged shard replaces N old shards; manifest atomic swap)
  • Dirty detection: compare file mtime + sha256 against DocMeta.sha256 in existing shard.
  • Superseded shard: added to manifest.deleted_shards; excluded from search; GC after merge.
  • Merge trigger: shard count > threshold (default 16); merge smallest N shards into one.
  • BUILDING state is per-repo; multiple repos can build concurrently; manifest updates are serialized.

8.11 DocMeta Record Layout (CBOR)

{
  doc_id:     u32,
  path:       text,       -- repo-relative UTF-8 path
  repo_id:    u32,
  lang:       text,       -- "rust", "go", "python", etc. or "unknown"
  size:       u64,        -- bytes
  sha256:     bytes[32],
  line_count: u32,
  mtime:      u64         -- unix seconds
}
  • Stored in shard doc-meta table in DocId-ascending order.
  • lang field populated by csearch-ingest (extension → shebang → content sniff priority order).
  • Binary files: lang = "binary"; never indexed for trigrams; not returned for text queries.

8.12 Symbol Index Model

SymbolEntry:
  name:    text          -- qualified or bare identifier
  kind:    Def|Class|Func|Struct|Enum|Const|Macro|..
  doc_id:  (ShardId, DocId)
  line:    u32
  col:     u16
  • Symbol posting index mirrors trigram posting index: name → sorted (ShardId, DocId) list.
  • Key: 4-byte FNV hash of lowercased name (collision tolerated; false positives filtered by exact match at verify step).
  • sym:Foo query: lookup hash(Foo) → candidate docs → verify symbol name at returned line.
  • Rank bonus: SymbolEntry.kind ∈ {Def, Class, Func} → +0.15 RankScore addend.

8.13 Ranking Model

RankScore = w_density  × match_density(doc, spans)
           + w_path    × path_importance(doc_meta.path)
           + w_symbol  × symbol_bonus(spans)
           + w_repo    × repo_weight(doc_meta.repo_id)
Factor Computation Default weight
match_density spans count / line_count, capped at 1.0 0.40
path_importance 1/(depth+1) × non_test × non_vendor 0.30
symbol_bonus 1 if any span on a SymbolEntry line else 0 0.20
repo_weight configurable per-repo float [0,1] 0.10
  • Top-k via bounded max-heap (k = result_cap, default 20).
  • Tie-break: (path lexicographic ASC, line ASC) — deterministic (I10).
  • BM25 not used for code search (token boundaries ill-defined in code); match_density replaces IDF.

8.14 Edge Cases and Failure Catalog

Scenario System Behavior Invariant
File < 3 bytes Zero trigrams; doc stored but never a candidate I2
All-binary repo No docs ingested; zero-doc shard not written —
Trigram → empty posting list Candidate set = ∅; zero results; not an error I1
Shard CRC mismatch on open Shard excluded; error metric incremented I3
Regex catastrophic backtrack attempt Rejected by Thompson NFA; linear time guaranteed I4
Regex .* (no trigram constraint) TrigramQuery = All; full scan; deadline enforced P6
Duplicate sha256 across repos DocMeta stored per repo; no dedup at index level (distinct paths) I5
Manifest swap mid-query Query sees either old or new manifest; never partial (atomic rename) I7
sym: on doc with no symbols Symbol index empty; zero results; no error —
Very long line (> 64KB) line_text_ref truncated to 512 bytes for response; full match coords preserved I6
Deleted doc queried before compaction Filtered via manifest.deleted_docs at fan-out start I9
Result cap reached mid-fan-out Remaining shard tasks cancelled; partial result set returned P6

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (stable toolchain) Go, C++, Zig Memory safety without GC; zero-cost mmap abstractions; cargo ecosystem; no GC pauses on search path Go wins for a small team prioritizing developer velocity over p99 latency; Google Code Search itself is Go. C++ wins when the team already owns a large C++ codebase with mature allocators. Zig wins for embedded targets where the Rust std is too heavy.
ADR-02 Build system Cargo workspace (single workspace, per-crate) Bazel, Buck2, CMake Native Rust; reproducible lockfile; no cross-tool friction; Bazel adds complexity without benefit at this scale Bazel/Buck2 win when the project is a polyglot monorepo (Rust + C++ + Python + Go) requiring hermetic cross-language builds and remote caching at >100-engineer scale. CMake wins when Rust must link into a pre-existing C++ CMake project.
ADR-03 Async runtime Tokio (multi-thread scheduler) async-std, smol, sync threads Mature ecosystem; structured concurrency; per-query task fan-out; timer/deadline support; wide crate compatibility Sync threads win if all I/O is mmap-only (no network) and the workload is embarrassingly CPU-parallel — e.g., a pure offline index builder. smol wins in embedded or WASM contexts where Tokio's syscall surface is too large.
ADR-04 Shard serialization (posting lists) Custom varint+delta binary (hand-rolled) protobuf, flatbuffers, bincode Zero-copy mmap access; no deserialization step; exact control over encoding for galloping; external formats add indirection FlatBuffers wins when the shard format must be read by many languages (C++, Python, JS) without per-language decoders, accepting a small overhead for the table accessor layer. Protobuf wins if the shard needs to evolve frequently with backward-compat guarantees enforced by a schema registry.
ADR-05 Doc-meta serialization CBOR (ciborium crate) JSON, MessagePack, protobuf Compact binary; self-describing; ciborium is no_std-friendly; easier tooling than raw binary; faster than JSON JSON wins when doc-meta records must be streamed to external tools (Elasticsearch, Splunk) that speak JSON natively; the size overhead is acceptable for metadata (not posting lists). MessagePack wins when size matters more than self-description and JSON tooling compatibility is not required.
ADR-06 Manifest format JSON (serde_json) TOML, CBOR, SQLite Human-readable for debugging; atomic rename semantics need no DB; small manifest size makes JSON parse cost negligible SQLite wins when the manifest must support transactional multi-writer updates (e.g., multiple concurrent ingest workers), ACID semantics, or when the manifest grows large enough (>10 MB) that JSON parse cost is measurable. CBOR wins for embedded deployments where JSON parsing is too heavy and human-readability is not needed.
ADR-07 Memory-mapped I/O memmap2 crate (read-only) pread syscalls, io_uring, read-ahead P5 zero-copy; OS page cache shared across processes; no userspace buffer; memmap2 is safe-wrapped POSIX mmap io_uring wins when the workload involves large numbers of random small reads from cold shards (paging in individual posting lists for rare trigrams), where async I/O submission batching reduces latency over synchronous page faults. pread wins on Windows where mmap semantics differ significantly from POSIX and file locking conflicts arise.
ADR-08 Regex engine (verification) Custom Thompson NFA / Pike VM (csearch-regex) regex crate, PCRE2, Hyperscan Mandatory linear-time guarantee (I4, P3); regex crate's backtracking engine risks super-linear on adversarial input; custom lets us reuse NFA for trigram lowering Hyperscan wins when the corpus contains fixed-pattern threat signatures (YARA-style) and Intel hardware SIMD throughput matters more than linear-time guarantees per pattern. The regex crate (meta-regex) wins when developer velocity matters more than auditable linear-time, and the threat model accepts no adversarial patterns.
ADR-09 Trigram query lowering Custom HIR visitor (csearch-trigquery) regex-syntax transform, DFA minimization Direct HIR traversal is simpler; DFA minimization over-complex for soundness; result is a boolean algebra, not a DFA DFA minimization wins if the goal is to maximize precision (minimize false positives) by extracting the exact set of required byte sequences via DFA reachability analysis — at the cost of exponential worst-case construction time for complex alternations.
ADR-10 Regex parsing frontend regex-syntax crate (Hir) hand-written parser, pest Battle-tested; produces typed HIR; avoids re-implementing Unicode character classes; Hir is stable and documented A hand-written parser wins when the accepted regex dialect must diverge substantially from POSIX/RE2 (e.g., a query DSL that overloads / and * for path/glob semantics) or when compilation size constraints forbid the regex-syntax transitive closure.
ADR-11 HTTP server axum (tokio-native) actix-web, warp, hyper-raw axum integrates with Tower middleware; typed extractors; simple streaming response; tokio-native avoids runtime bridging actix-web wins when raw single-core throughput benchmarks (TechEmpower) matter more than ergonomics and the project uses Actix actor semantics elsewhere. hyper-raw wins for an embedded HTTP server that must minimize binary size and has no need for routing or middleware.
ADR-12 Posting list intersection algorithm Galloping (exponential search) linear merge, SIMD INTERSECT, Roaring Bitmaps Galloping optimal when lists are of unequal size (common for rare vs common trigrams); Roaring adds memory overhead and complexity for posting lists that are already delta-encoded Roaring Bitmaps win when DocIds are dense (close to contiguous), posting lists are materialized entirely in memory, and bitwise AND over 64-bit words is cheaper than delta-varint decode + gallop. SIMD INTERSECT wins for bulk set-intersection of equal-length, uncompressed integer arrays on AVX-512 hardware.
ADR-13 Integer compression LEB128 varint (delta-coded) PForDelta, Simple-8b, stream-vbyte Sufficient compression for DocId gaps; simpler decoder; avoids alignment constraints for mmap slices; can upgrade to SIMD VByte in Phase 14 if benchmarks warrant PForDelta / PFOR wins when posting lists are very long (>10k entries) and SIMD vectorized bulk decode (4-bit frames) is the throughput bottleneck; the compression ratio improvement justifies the decoder complexity. stream-vbyte wins on ARM NEON where SIMD width differs from x86.
ADR-14 Language detection Extension table + shebang line + content sniff (csearch-ingest) linguist FFI, tokei No FFI dependency; shebang covers scripts; content sniff catches extensionless files; accuracy sufficient for lang: filter linguist (via FFI) wins when the deployment requires GitHub-accurate language statistics and the FFI overhead is acceptable; e.g., a product feature that surfaces per-repo language breakdowns. tokei wins when line-count statistics per language are a first-class product feature.
ADR-15 Symbol extraction Regex/heuristic per language (csearch-symbol, ctags-style) tree-sitter, LSP, ctags subprocess Tree-sitter accurate but adds large dep + per-language grammar; ctags subprocess adds process overhead; regex heuristics cover 80% of definition patterns with zero deps tree-sitter wins when symbol extraction must handle macro-generated code, nested definitions, and attribute macros in Rust; the 10–20 MB grammar dependency is acceptable and recall must exceed 99%. LSP wins for a persistent IDE-backed server where semantic accuracy (including type-checked rename-aware symbols) is a product requirement.
ADR-16 Logging tracing crate (structured, per-span context) log crate, slog tracing integrates with tokio; span context carries query_id, shard_id; machine-parseable JSON output via tracing-subscriber The log crate wins for a lightweight CLI tool or library crate where the tokio async context propagation overhead is unnecessary and binary size is a constraint. slog wins when structured logging is needed but Tokio integration is not, and the team prefers explicit drain composition.
ADR-17 Metrics metrics crate + metrics-exporter-prometheus Prometheus client direct, statsd, OpenTelemetry metrics facade decouples instrumentation from exporter; Prometheus scrape endpoint for P10 observability OpenTelemetry wins when the deployment requires traces, metrics, and logs in a unified pipeline (OTLP) to a vendor like Datadog, Honeycomb, or Grafana Cloud. Prometheus client direct wins when the codebase is Prometheus-only and the facade abstraction adds no value.
ADR-18 Testing framework Rust built-in #[test] + criterion for benchmarks nextest (optional), googletest-rust Built-in is zero-dep; criterion provides statistically rigorous latency benchmarks; nextest compatible if added nextest wins for large workspaces where test-binary startup time dominates and per-test parallelism at the harness level speeds up CI; it is fully backward-compatible with #[test]. googletest-rust wins when the team migrates from a C++ codebase and wants assertion parity with GoogleTest matchers.
ADR-19 Fuzz testing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) AFL++, honggfuzz cargo-fuzz first-class Rust support; structured inputs via arbitrary crate; Phase 14 fuzz targets for shard reader and trigram soundness AFL++ wins when coverage-guided fuzzing must cover multiple languages (C + Rust) in a shared process and AFL++'s CMPLOG and persistent mode outperform libFuzzer on the specific target. honggfuzz wins for kernel fuzzing or targets that require hardware performance counters as coverage signals.
ADR-20 Property testing proptest quickcheck proptest shrinking superior for complex types (QueryAst, byte sequences); arbitrary strategies composable quickcheck wins for simple scalar properties where the shrinking overhead of proptest's state machine is unnecessary and the team prefers the lighter API.
ADR-21 Differential testing (correctness) Compare output vs grep -rE on test corpus (Phase 14) regex crate baseline, hand-crafted oracles grep -rE is universally available, POSIX-compliant; differential test catches false negatives definitively The regex crate baseline wins when the search engine itself is being benchmarked for precision against a Rust-native reference, or when the corpus contains non-UTF-8 files that grep handles differently across platforms. Hand-crafted oracles win when the query semantics intentionally differ from grep (e.g., symbol-semantic search).
ADR-22 CI/CD GitHub Actions (cargo test, cargo clippy, cargo fmt, cargo fuzz in nightly) CircleCI, Buildkite Standard for open-source Rust; cache cargo registry and target dir; matrix for stable + nightly Buildkite wins for large orgs that need self-hosted runners with hardware-specific test machines (e.g., NUMA benchmarking rigs, specific NVMe models) and fine-grained secrets management outside GitHub's trust boundary. CircleCI wins for legacy corporate environments already standardized on it.
ADR-23 Dependency policy Minimal, audited; cargo audit in CI; no proc-macro-heavy deps on hot path permissive / add freely Proc macros inflate compile time; audit gates known CVEs; small dep tree reduces supply-chain risk A permissive policy wins during rapid prototyping where the ecosystem maturity of tokio, axum, serde etc. provides real value fast, and the security posture is not yet production-grade. It also wins when the cost of maintaining audited vendored deps exceeds the CVE risk at a given scale.
ADR-24 Shard checksum CRC32 (crc32fast crate) SHA256, xxHash, Adler32 I3 integrity check on open; CRC32 fast and sufficient for corruption detection (not cryptographic); crc32fast uses SIMD SHA256 wins when shards must be integrity-verified across a network (e.g., downloaded from object storage) where the threat model includes a malicious storage provider, requiring a cryptographic hash. xxHash wins when the priority is maximal speed and the integrity model is accidental corruption only, but CRC32 hardware acceleration is not available (non-x86 targets).
ADR-25 Content addressing SHA-256 (sha2 crate) BLAKE3, MD5, xxHash I5 cryptographic identity for dedup; sha2 is widely audited; BLAKE3 faster but less ubiquitous in tooling BLAKE3 wins when index build throughput is I/O-bound and hashing CPU time is measurable (>5% of build time); BLAKE3 is 3–4× faster than SHA-256 in software on modern CPUs and is cryptographically sound. MD5 wins only in legacy environments where compatibility with existing content-addressable stores requires MD5.
ADR-26 Packaging / distribution Single static binary per crate (musl target optional); Cargo.lock checked in Docker-only, Homebrew formula, Nix flake Static binary simplest deployment; Cargo.lock reproducibility; Docker layer can wrap binary; no runtime deps A Nix flake wins when the team uses NixOS systemwide or needs fully reproducible bit-identical builds across developer machines; the derivation guarantees hermetic closure. Docker-only wins when the deployment target is a Kubernetes cluster and there is no need for developer-local binary execution.
ADR-27 Reproducible builds Pin all deps via Cargo.lock; use rust-toolchain.toml to pin channel+version Nix, Guix rust-toolchain.toml + Cargo.lock sufficient for binary reproducibility on same target triple; avoids Nix complexity Nix wins for multi-language projects (Rust + C + Python) where the C toolchain version must also be pinned and rust-toolchain.toml does not cover system library versions. Guix wins for FSF-compliant fully free software distributions.
ADR-28 Benchmarking corpus Linux kernel source (≥30 M lines) + Rust stdlib + CPython as canonical perf corpus synthetic data, smaller repos Real-world trigram distribution; tests posting list skew and large-shard mmap; reproducible by tag Synthetic data wins when the real-world corpus cannot be redistributed (proprietary code), or when the benchmark requires specific trigram frequency distributions not present in OSS corpora (e.g., testing behavior on all-ASCII vs. all-binary).
ADR-29 Licensing Apache-2.0 OR MIT (dual) GPL, BSL, proprietary Permissive; compatible with google/codesearch (BSD-3) and Sourcegraph zoekt (Apache-2.0) reference reading; standard Rust ecosystem norm GPL wins when the project is a developer tool intended to remain permanently open-source and the copyleft requirement is acceptable for all anticipated deployment scenarios; GPL ensures derivative works remain open. BSL wins for commercial products that need the open-source development ecosystem but want time-limited protection against cloud-provider reselling.
ADR-30 Shard file extension .csi (Code Search Index) .idx, .shard, .bin Unique; avoids collision with other tools; human-recognizable in directory listing .shard wins when multiple storage engines in the same codebase use the term "shard" as a shared abstraction and the project requires a generic file format viewer that keys off the extension. .idx wins for maximum compatibility with Unix tooling that already recognizes .idx as an index file and provides editor/viewer support.

11. Core Schema and Core Types

All types reside in csearch-core unless noted. These are normative: phase implementations must match names, field types, and semantics exactly.

11.1 Identity Types — csearch-core::ids

pub struct DocId(pub u32);
pub struct RepoId(pub u32);
pub struct ShardId(pub u32);
Type Scope Uniqueness Mutation Serialization
DocId(u32) dense, per-shard unique within a shard; (ShardId, DocId) globally unique immutable once assigned u32 little-endian in shard doc table
RepoId(u32) workspace-wide assigned at ingest registration immutable u32 in manifest
ShardId(u32) workspace-wide assigned at shard write immutable u32 in manifest
  • DocId values are dense [0, n_docs) within a shard; gaps forbidden.
  • A deleted doc retains its DocId; filtered at query time via manifest supersession (I3, I9).
  • All three types implement Copy, Eq, Ord, Hash, Debug, serde::{Serialize, Deserialize}.

11.2 Trigram — csearch-core::trigram

pub struct Trigram(pub u32);  // upper 8 bits unused; bits 23-0 = b0<<16 | b1<<8 | b2

impl Trigram {
    pub fn pack(b: [u8; 3]) -> Self;     // bijection: 3 bytes -> 24-bit value (I8)
    pub fn unpack(self) -> [u8; 3];      // inverse of pack
    pub fn from_bytes(s: &[u8]) -> Option<Self>;  // None if len < 3
}
  • I8: pack then unpack is identity over all 2^24 values; property-tested in Phase 1.
  • Trigram is Copy, Eq, Ord, Hash; ordered by numeric value for binary search.
  • Only the low 24 bits are significant; the high 8 bits must be zero (enforced in pack).
  • Case-folded variants produced by csearch-trigram::fold; stored as additional trigrams in index, not as a separate field.

11.3 Posting and PostingList — csearch-core::posting

pub struct Posting(pub DocId);   // a single entry in a trigram's posting list

pub struct PostingList<'a> {
    data: &'a [u8],              // mmap-backed delta+varint encoded bytes
    len: u32,                    // number of DocId entries
}

impl<'a> PostingList<'a> {
    pub fn iter(&self) -> PostingIter<'a>;   // ascending DocId iterator; I2
    pub fn len(&self) -> u32;
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool;
}

pub struct PostingIter<'a> { /* opaque; decodes delta+varint lazily */ }
impl<'a> Iterator for PostingIter<'a> { type Item = DocId; }
Property Value
Ownership 'a borrows from mmap; zero allocation on iteration (P5)
Order strictly ascending DocId; I2; enforced at write time
Encoding delta encoding (successive differences) + unsigned varint (LEB128)
Mutation none; shard is immutable (I3, P4)
Concurrency Send + Sync; multiple readers from same mmap slice safe
Persistence encoded bytes written once by PostingWriter; never rewritten 
pub struct PostingWriter {
    buf: Vec<u8>,
    last: u32,   // last DocId written, for delta computation
}

impl PostingWriter {
    pub fn push(&mut self, id: DocId);   // panics if id <= last (order violation)
    pub fn finish(self) -> Vec<u8>;      // returns encoded bytes
}

11.4 DocMeta — csearch-core::doc_meta

pub struct DocMeta {
    pub path:       String,      // repo-relative file path; UTF-8
    pub repo:       RepoId,
    pub lang:       String,      // MIME-like lang tag, e.g. "rust", "go", ""
    pub size:       u32,         // file byte length at index time
    pub sha256:     [u8; 32],    // content address (I5)
    pub line_count: u32,
}
Aspect Policy
Mutation immutable once written into shard
Content address sha256 is the content identity; duplicate content across repos detected by equality (I5)
Serialization fixed-layout binary record in shard doc-meta table; offsets in ShardHeader
Concurrency Send + Sync; read-only after shard seal
path encoding must be valid UTF-8; non-UTF-8 paths are rejected at ingest

11.5 QueryAst and Filter — csearch-core::query_ast

pub enum QueryAst {
    Literal(String),
    Regex(Hir),                          // csearch-regex Hir
    And(Vec<QueryAst>),
    Or(Vec<QueryAst>),
    Not(Box<QueryAst>),
    Field(Filter),
}

pub enum Filter {
    File(Hir),                           // file: <regex> — match on DocMeta.path
    Lang(String),                        // lang: <tag>
    Repo(String),                        // repo: <name>
    Case(bool),                          // case:yes / case:no
    Sym(String),                         // sym: <name> — symbol search
}
  • QueryAst is produced by csearch-query::parser; consumed by csearch-search::executor.
  • Not applies only to sub-trees, not to the top-level; a top-level Not is a query error.
  • And([]) / Or([]) are illegal; parser must produce non-empty vectors.
  • Hir is regex_syntax::hir::Hir (re-exported); QueryAst owns its Hir values.
  • Filter::Case applies to the nearest enclosing Literal/Regex; default is case-sensitive.

11.6 TrigramQuery — csearch-core::trigram_query

pub enum TrigramQuery {
    All,                              // matches all documents (wildcard / .* lowering)
    Any,                              // matches no documents (impossible; prune branch)
    Trigram(Trigram),                 // single trigram must be present
    And(Vec<TrigramQuery>),           // all children must be satisfied
    Or(Vec<TrigramQuery>),            // at least one child must be satisfied
}
Property Invariant
Soundness if regex matches doc D then lower(regex) is satisfied by D's trigram set (I1, P1, P2)
All semantics every doc satisfies All; used when lowering cannot extract useful trigrams (e.g. .*)
Any semantics no doc satisfies Any; used as zero element for And; executor prunes immediately
Simplification And([x]) -> x; Or([x]) -> x; And containing Any -> Any; Or containing All -> All
Ownership owned tree; produced by csearch-trigquery::lower, consumed by csearch-search::intersect
Concurrency immutable once lowered; Send + Sync

11.7 Nfa and PikeVm — csearch-regex

// csearch-regex::nfa
pub struct Nfa {
    pub states: Vec<NfaState>,
    pub start:  u32,
}

pub enum NfaState {
    Match,
    Byte(u8, u32),               // on byte b, go to state next
    Split(u32, u32),             // epsilon split to two states
    Capture(u8, u32),            // capture group open/close + next
}

// csearch-regex::pike_vm
pub struct PikeVm<'a> {
    nfa: &'a Nfa,
}

impl<'a> PikeVm<'a> {
    pub fn new(nfa: &'a Nfa) -> Self;
    pub fn find_iter<'t>(&self, text: &'t [u8]) -> PikeMatchIter<'t, 'a>;
    // guarantee: O(|text| * |NFA states|); no backtracking
}
  • I4: linear time is a structural guarantee of the NFA simulation; no memoization of states per position is needed beyond the current/next state set.
  • PikeVm is Send + Sync; borrows Nfa, so multiple query threads can share one compiled NFA.
  • Capture groups produce byte-range pairs; only group 0 (full match) is required for Phase 5; named captures deferred to Phase 10.

11.8 MatchSpan and SearchResult — csearch-core::match_span

pub struct MatchSpan {
    pub doc:       (ShardId, DocId),
    pub line:      u32,              // 1-based line number
    pub col:       u32,              // 0-based byte column within line
    pub len:       u32,              // match byte length
    pub line_text: String,           // full text of the matched line (for display)
}

pub struct SearchResult {
    pub doc_meta:  DocMeta,
    pub spans:     Vec<MatchSpan>,   // all matches within this doc, ascending line order
    pub score:     RankScore,
}

pub struct RankScore(pub f32);       // higher = more relevant; NaN forbidden
Field Constraint
line 1-based; 0 is illegal
col byte offset, not char offset; caller converts for display
len must equal actual match byte length (I6)
line_text trimmed to at most 512 bytes to bound memory; truncation indicated by … suffix
RankScore must be finite (f32::is_finite); NaN causes a panic in topk

11.9 SymbolEntry — csearch-core::symbol

pub struct SymbolEntry {
    pub name: String,
    pub kind: SymbolKind,
    pub doc:  (ShardId, DocId),
    pub line: u32,                   // 1-based definition line
}

pub enum SymbolKind {
    Function,
    Struct,
    Enum,
    Trait,
    Class,
    Const,
    TypeAlias,
    Module,
    Other(String),
}
  • Symbol postings are keyed by name trigrams in the symbol sub-index within a shard.
  • sym: queries lower name to trigrams, intersect the symbol index, then verify exact name match.
  • SymbolKind::Other carries the raw ctags kind string for languages not enumerated above.

11.10 ShardHeader — csearch-core::shard_header

pub struct ShardHeader {
    pub magic:              [u8; 8],    // b"CSEARCH1"
    pub version:            u16,        // format version; currently 1
    pub shard_id:           ShardId,
    pub n_docs:             u32,
    pub n_trigrams:         u32,
    pub trigram_table_off:  u64,        // byte offset into shard file
    pub posting_lists_off:  u64,
    pub doc_table_off:      u64,
    pub symbol_index_off:   u64,        // 0 if no symbol index in this shard
    pub crc32:              u32,        // CRC of all bytes following the header
}
Constraint Value
magic must equal b"CSEARCH1"; mismatch → ShardError::BadMagic
version reader rejects version > CURRENT_VERSION with ShardError::UnsupportedVersion
crc32 CRC-32/ISO-HDLC over bytes [sizeof(ShardHeader)..]; checked on open (P7)
Endianness little-endian for all multi-byte fields
Size fixed 64 bytes; padding to alignment if needed
Mutation header written once at shard seal; never overwritten (I3)

11.11 PostingWriter / PostingReader — csearch-shard + csearch-index

// csearch-index::shard_writer
pub struct PostingWriter {
    buf:  Vec<u8>,
    last: u32,
}
impl PostingWriter {
    pub fn push(&mut self, id: DocId);     // contract: id > last, else panic
    pub fn finish(self) -> Vec<u8>;        // encoded posting list bytes
}

// csearch-shard::posting_reader
pub struct PostingReader<'a> {
    data:  &'a [u8],                       // mmap slice for the entire posting lists region
}
impl<'a> PostingReader<'a> {
    pub fn list(&self, offset: u64, len: u32) -> PostingList<'a>;
    // offset + (u64 * len) must be within data; else panic in debug, UB-safe in release
}
  • PostingWriter is single-threaded append-only; used only during shard build.
  • PostingReader is Send + Sync; multiple threads read concurrently from the same mmap.
  • Encoding: unsigned LEB128 of (id - last_id) for each successive DocId; first entry encodes the raw DocId value (delta from 0).

11.12 Cross-Crate Trait Obligations

// All identity/value types must implement:
trait CoreValue: Copy + Clone + Eq + Ord + Hash + Debug + Send + Sync
    + serde::Serialize + for<'de> serde::Deserialize<'de> {}

// Implemented by: DocId, RepoId, ShardId, Trigram, RankScore (except Copy constraint relaxed for RankScore)

// Shard-reading abstraction (csearch-shard)
pub trait ShardRead: Send + Sync {
    fn posting_list(&self, t: Trigram) -> Option<PostingList<'_>>;
    fn doc_meta(&self, id: DocId) -> Option<&DocMeta>;
    fn n_docs(&self) -> u32;
    fn shard_id(&self) -> ShardId;
}
  • ShardRead is the boundary between csearch-shard (mmap impl) and test fakes.
  • All crates depend only on csearch-core; no circular dependencies permitted.
  • csearch-search depends on csearch-core, csearch-shard (via ShardRead), csearch-trigquery, csearch-regex, csearch-rank.
  • Dependency graph is a DAG; csearch-server is the only crate that imports nearly everything.

11.13 Error Types

Crate Error Type Key Variants
csearch-core CoreError InvalidTrigram, DocIdOutOfRange, Utf8Error
csearch-shard ShardError BadMagic, UnsupportedVersion, CrcMismatch, OffsetOutOfBounds
csearch-index BuildError IoError, MemoryBudgetExceeded, PostingOrderViolation
csearch-query ParseError UnterminatedRegex, UnknownFilter, EmptyQuery
csearch-regex RegexError ParseFailed, Unsupported(feature)
csearch-search SearchError DeadlineExceeded, ResultCapReached, ShardUnavailable
csearch-server ServerError BindFailed, ManifestLoadFailed, FanoutError
  • All error types implement std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static.
  • SearchError::DeadlineExceeded is not a server 500; it returns partial results with a truncated: true field in the JSON response.
  • ShardError::CrcMismatch marks the shard as quarantined in the manifest; csearch doctor reports it.

Phase 1 — core types + trigram model

1.1 Objectives

  • Define all foundational newtypes used throughout every crate: DocId, RepoId, ShardId, Trigram, Posting, MatchSpan, QueryAst skeleton, RankScore.
  • Establish the byte-indexing decision: the engine indexes raw bytes, not Unicode codepoints; UTF-8 correctness is a property of callers, not the trigram kernel.
  • Publish csearch-core as a zero-dependency workspace crate depended on by all others.
  • Guarantee trigram pack/unpack bijection by property tests.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: I8 (trigram bijection), B4/B5 (NFA/PikeVm theory prerequisite), ADR-01 (Rust newtype pattern), Phase 1 (foundational types) -->

csearch-core/src/types.rs — all primary newtypes:

Type Representation Invariant
DocId(u32) dense, per-shard valid only within its ShardId scope
RepoId(u32) global, assigned at ingest monotone increasing
ShardId(u32) global, assigned at build stable once written
Trigram(u32) 24-bit value in lower bits bits 24–31 always zero
Posting DocId in list context list is sorted ascending
RankScore(f32) composite; higher = more relevant finite, non-negative

Trigram pack/unpack — b0<<16 | b1<<8 | b2; function signatures only:

fn pack(b: [u8; 3]) -> Trigram;
fn unpack(t: Trigram) -> [u8; 3];

Both are pure, branchless, #[inline(always)].

MatchSpan fields: doc: (ShardId, DocId), line: u32, col: u32, len: u32, line_text_ref: Option<Arc<[u8]>>.

QueryAst skeleton (no parser yet):

enum QueryAst { Literal(String), Regex(String), And(Vec<QueryAst>), Or(Vec<QueryAst>), Not(Box<QueryAst>), Field(Filter) }
enum Filter { File(String), Lang(String), Repo(String), Case(bool), Sym(String) }

TrigramQuery skeleton:

enum TrigramQuery { All, Any, Trigram(Trigram), And(Vec<TrigramQuery>), Or(Vec<TrigramQuery>) }

csearch-core/src/lib.rs — re-exports all types; no std::io, no serde yet. csearch-core/Cargo.toml — [features] serde = ["dep:serde"]; feature-gated only.

Property test harness in csearch-core/tests/trigram_roundtrip.rs using proptest.

1.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p csearch-core passes; property tests cover all 16 777 216 (2²⁴) representable trigrams via proptest sampling.
  • All other crates compile when they add csearch-core as a dependency with zero code yet.
  • Trigram, DocId, ShardId, RepoId implement Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
proptest pack/unpack roundtrip 0 failures across 10 000 cases
Trigram(0xFFFFFF) pack/unpack exact match
All types derive Hash + Ord confirmed by HashSet<Trigram> compile test
csearch-core has zero runtime panics cargo test with RUSTFLAGS="-C panic=abort"
serde gate off by default cargo build -p csearch-core produces no serde import

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected
fixtures/trigram/pack_vectors.csv (b0,b1,b2) -> expected u32 16 manually computed rows
fixtures/trigram/boundary.json [0,0,0], [255,255,255], [0,0,1] packed values 0, 16777215, 1
fixtures/trigram/malformed_high_bits.json Trigram with bit 24 set unpack must mask/panic per policy

1.6 Pitfalls

  • High-bit contamination: storing Trigram(u32) without masking allows bit 24+ to set if arithmetic is careless; pack must always mask to 24 bits before constructing.
  • DocId scope confusion: DocId is local to a shard; a global lookup with a bare DocId without ShardId silently returns wrong metadata. Enforce (ShardId, DocId) pair for any cross-shard reference; add a lint via clippy::disallowed_types on bare DocId in csearch-search.
  • MatchSpan lifetime: line_text_ref holding Arc<[u8]> keeps entire shard mmap alive if callers accumulate large result sets; measure Arc refcount pressure under 10k-result queries.
  • QueryAst Regex as String: downstream crates expect the regex string to be syntactically valid; validate at parse time in Phase 7, not here. Any downstream unwrap on regex compilation is a latent panic.
  • serde feature bleed: if a binary crate enables serde globally, private internal types may leak serialization. Guard with #[cfg_attr(feature = "serde", derive(Serialize, Deserialize))] per type, not crate-wide.

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • Trigram(u32) fits in a register; no heap cost. Hash implementations must not branch on upper bits.
  • MatchSpan with Arc<[u8]> adds an atomic refcount per span; batch results before cloning.
  • QueryAst enum allocates Vec on heap for And/Or arms; typical queries are shallow (depth ≤ 4), so this is acceptable.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • pack([0,0,0]) = 0; pack([255,255,255]) = 16 777 215; both must round-trip.
  • Trigram from user-supplied u32 with high bits set: constructor must either mask silently or return Err; document the policy (mask).
  • MatchSpan::col overflows u32 on a line > 4 GB; contract: col is byte offset from line start, capped at u32::MAX; line length limits in Phase 9 prevent genuine overflow.
  • QueryAst::Not(Box::new(Not(x))) double-negation: Phase 7 normalizes, Phase 6 handles semantically.

1.9 Failure Analysis

  • Blast radius: wrong Trigram packing corrupts every downstream posting list lookup; detectable by property test failures before merge.
  • Detection: differential test in Phase 14 (grep -rE vs engine) will surface any encoding defect that slipped past unit tests.
  • Recovery: csearch-core is a pure-types crate; re-build is a seconds-level cargo recompile.
  • Operator visibility: shard header includes a version field; a pack-format change bumps the version, causing shard readers to reject stale shards cleanly.

1.10 Operational Notes

  • No runtime metrics emitted from csearch-core; it is a pure library.
  • version constant in csearch-core::consts is the single source of truth for wire format versioning; all other crates import it.
  • All Debug impls must emit human-readable values (e.g., Trigram(0x61_62_63) formats as abc).

Phase 2 — trigram extraction

2.1 Objectives

  • Implement csearch-trigram crate: extract the deduplicated set of overlapping 3-byte trigrams from any byte slice.
  • Compute per-trigram raw frequency for later cost estimation in csearch-trigquery.
  • Define case-fold strategy: optional generation of additional lowercase-folded trigrams for case-insensitive indexing.
  • Handle newlines, short files (<3 bytes), empty files, and binary-like byte sequences correctly.
  • No I/O in this crate; input is &[u8].

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: I8 (pack/unpack bijection applied to per-doc trigram set), B1 Ch.3 (inverted files: why overlapping n-grams), P13 (trigram frequency profiles), ADR-04 (raw byte trigrams over Unicode codepoints) -->

csearch-trigram/src/extract.rs:

fn extract_set(bytes: &[u8]) -> TrigramSet;
fn extract_with_freq(bytes: &[u8]) -> TrigramFreqMap;
fn extract_case_folded(bytes: &[u8], fold: CaseFold) -> TrigramSet;
  • TrigramSet = BTreeSet<Trigram> for deterministic iteration order matching sorted posting lists.
  • TrigramFreqMap = HashMap<Trigram, u32> (raw occurrence count, not deduplicated).
  • Sliding window: iterate bytes[i..i+3] for i in 0..bytes.len().saturating_sub(2).
  • Dedup: insert into BTreeSet; ignore duplicates.
  • Frequency: count raw occurrences before dedup for query cost estimation.

Newline handling: newlines are bytes 0x0A / 0x0D; they participate in trigrams as-is. No special stripping. A trigram spanning a line boundary is valid and indexed.

Case-fold strategy:

enum CaseFold { None, AsciiLower }
  • AsciiLower: for each 3-byte window, if any byte in b'A'..=b'Z', emit additional trigram with those bytes replaced by b'a'+b-b'A'. Emit both folded and original.
  • Full Unicode fold is out of scope; ASCII fold is sufficient for case-insensitive code search (identifiers are ASCII).

Short-file handling:

bytes.len() Behavior
0 return empty TrigramSet
1 return empty TrigramSet
2 return empty TrigramSet
≥ 3 normal sliding window

csearch-trigram/src/lib.rs exports: extract_set, extract_with_freq, extract_case_folded, TrigramSet, TrigramFreqMap, CaseFold.

csearch-trigram/tests/extract_tests.rs — unit + property tests:

  • Known strings -> expected trigram sets.
  • proptest: for any Vec<u8> of len ≥ 3, every output trigram's bytes appear consecutively in the input (soundness of extraction).
  • Frequency sum across all trigrams ≥ len-2 (every window counted).

2.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p csearch-trigram passes.
  • extract_set(b"hello") returns {hel, ell, llo} as Trigram values.
  • extract_set(b"ab") returns empty set.
  • extract_case_folded(b"Hello", AsciiLower) returns {Hel, ell, llo, hel} (original + folded).
  • Frequency map for b"aaaa" returns {aaa: 2}.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
extract_set(b"hello") exactly {hel,ell,llo}
proptest soundness: every trigram's bytes in input 0 failures / 10 000 cases
Empty/1-byte/2-byte input empty set, no panic
extract_with_freq sum ≥ len - 2 holds for all inputs len ≥ 3
BTreeSet output is sorted confirmed by iterator assertion
Case-fold: extract_case_folded(b"ABC", AsciiLower) {ABC, abc}

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Content Expected trigrams
fixtures/trigram/hello.txt hello\n hel,ell,llo,lo\n
fixtures/trigram/short.txt hi {}
fixtures/trigram/empty.txt `` {}
fixtures/trigram/binary.bin \x00\xFF\x00\xFF {0x00FF00, 0xFF00FF}
fixtures/trigram/casefold.txt AbC normal + folded set
fixtures/trigram/newline_boundary.txt ab\ncd ab\n, b\nc, \ncd

2.6 Pitfalls

  • Off-by-one on window end: bytes.len() - 2 as usize underflows if len < 2; use saturating_sub(2) consistently.
  • Trigram spanning CR+LF: \r\n produces trigrams crossing line endings; correct and intentional, but test explicitly to prevent future regressions where "clean line" preprocessing is introduced.
  • Case-fold double-emit: emitting both original and folded trigrams doubles the trigram set size for all-alpha files; the index build must account for this in memory estimation. Log fold ratio at ingest.
  • Large binary files: files with random bytes yield nearly uniform trigram sets (~16M unique trigrams at 256³); extract_with_freq on a 100 MB binary file allocates ~64 MB for the HashMap alone. Phase 9 applies binary filter before reaching this crate.
  • BTreeSet iteration performance: BTreeSet insertion is O(log n) per element; for files producing >100k unique trigrams, prefer a sort-then-dedup approach on a Vec<Trigram>. Expose extract_set_fast(bytes: &[u8]) -> Vec<Trigram> as an alternative that sorts + deduplicates in place.
  • Non-ASCII case fold: bytes 0x80–0xFF should NOT be folded; only b'A'..=b'Z' -> b'a'..=b'z'. Guard with explicit range check.

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • Tight loop over bytes with 3-byte pack: instruction-level parallelism-friendly; ensure no bounds-check per iteration (get_unchecked behind unsafe block with slice-len pre-check).
  • BTreeSet<Trigram> has poor cache locality for large files; Vec + sort-dedup is 2–4× faster for files > 64 KB; expose both; indexer uses fast path.
  • HashMap for frequency: pre-size with HashMap::with_capacity(bytes.len()) to avoid rehashing.
  • Avoid per-window heap allocation; all extraction is stack-local until final insert.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • All-null bytes \x00\x00\x00...: trigram set = {Trigram(0)}; valid.
  • Single unique byte repeated: one trigram; frequency = len-2.
  • File of exactly 3 bytes: one trigram; must not panic on any byte values.
  • File with BOM \xEF\xBB\xBF: treated as raw bytes; BOM bytes form trigrams.
  • Very long lines (no newline for 1 MB): correct; no line-splitting in this crate.
  • Input borrowed from mmap; no copy of the &[u8] slice permitted inside the extraction hot path.

2.9 Failure Analysis

  • Wrong trigram set: downstream posting lists built from incorrect trigrams produce false negatives (I1 violated). Detectable by the Phase 14 differential test.
  • Panic on short files: saturating_sub guards prevent; any panic here is a critical bug blocking all indexing.
  • Memory exhaustion on binary files: Phase 9 binary filter is the first line of defense; if it fails, extract_with_freq on a large binary is the fallback risk. Monitor allocated bytes per doc in ingest metrics.
  • Blast radius: wrong extraction contaminates all posting lists in the shard; entire shard must be rebuilt.

2.10 Operational Notes

  • Instrument extract_set_fast with a histogram of trigram-set size per doc; emit as trigram.set_size_p50/p99 per-repo.
  • Log fold-expansion ratio: folded_count / original_count; alert if > 2.0 (unexpected all-uppercase codebase or misconfiguration).
  • No disk I/O in this crate; no metrics sink needed; metrics emitted by csearch-index when it calls this crate.

Phase 3 — posting lists + shard format

3.1 Objectives

  • Define on-disk shard binary format: header, sorted trigram table, packed posting lists, doc-meta table, trailing CRC32.
  • Implement csearch-index: in-memory accumulation of Trigram -> Vec<DocId> posting lists during build.
  • Implement csearch-shard: immutable shard writer (PostingWriter) + mmap-backed reader (PostingReader) with zero-copy PostingList<'a> iterators.
  • Encoding: delta + unsigned varint (LEB128) per posting list.
  • Establish on-disk invariants I2, I3, I7, I8.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: I2 (ascending DocId order), I3 (shard immutability), P4 (immutable shards), P5 (zero-copy mmap), B1 Ch.3-4 (inverted file format + varint compression), S6 (LEB128 spec), ADR-07 (memmap2 zero-copy) -->

On-disk layout (big-endian, mmap-compatible):

[ ShardHeader (64B) ][ TrigramTable: n_trigrams × TrigramEntry (12B) ]
[ PostingListBlob: varint-encoded posting data ][ DocMetaTable: n_docs × DocMetaEntry (variable, len-prefixed) ]
[ CRC32 of all preceding bytes (4B) ]

ShardHeader: magic: [u8;8] (b"CSRCH001") | version: u16 | shard_id: u32 | n_docs: u32 | n_trigrams: u32 | trigram_table_off/posting_blob_off/doc_table_off/crc_off: u64 (byte offsets) | _reserved: [u8;6] (zeroed).

TrigramEntry: trigram: u32 (24-bit) | posting_off: u32 | posting_len: u32 (bytes). TrigramTable sorted ascending by trigram; binary search O(log n_trigrams).

Delta+varint encoding: first entry = raw DocId LEB128; subsequent = DocId - prev_DocId (always positive, I2) LEB128; end-of-list implicit from posting_len.

// csearch-index/src/posting.rs
struct PostingWriter { map: BTreeMap<Trigram, Vec<DocId>> }
fn add(&mut self, trigram: Trigram, doc: DocId);            // accumulates unsorted
fn finish(self) -> SortedPostingMap;                        // sorts + dedups each Vec<DocId>

// csearch-index/src/builder.rs — IndexBuilder
fn add_doc(&mut self, meta: DocMeta, trigrams: TrigramSet) -> DocId;  // dense DocId from 0, in-shard
fn write_shard(&mut self, path: &Path, shard_id: ShardId) -> Result<ShardHeader>;

// csearch-shard/src/reader.rs — ShardReader
struct ShardReader { mmap: Mmap, header: ShardHeader }
fn open(path: &Path) -> Result<ShardReader>;
fn posting_list(&self, t: Trigram) -> Option<PostingList<'_>>;
fn doc_meta(&self, doc: DocId) -> Result<DocMeta>;
fn verify_crc(&self) -> Result<()>;

// PostingList<'a> — zero-copy iterator over mmap-backed encoded slice
struct PostingList<'a> { data: &'a [u8], prev: u32, remaining: usize }
impl Iterator for PostingList<'_> { type Item = DocId; }

DocMetaTable: variable-length entries, u32 length-prefixed, postcard-encoded (no serde in shard format — determinism). Fields: path: String, repo_id: RepoId, lang: String, size: u32, sha256: [u8;32], line_count: u32.

CRC32/IEEE over all bytes preceding the CRC field; computed at write, verified at open.

Spill for bounded-memory build: if PostingWriter map exceeds a configurable budget (default 512 MB), flush a partial shard; merged in Phase 12. Phase 3 defines the format; spill policy lands in Phase 4.

3.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p csearch-index -p csearch-shard passes.
  • Round-trip: build shard from programmatic docs → tmpfile → ShardReader::open → posting_list/doc_meta correct, CRC passes.
  • PostingList iterator yields strictly ascending order (I2).
  • Binary diff of two identical-input builds is byte-identical (determinism).

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Round-trip: 1000-doc shard, all postings match 0 mismatches
CRC failure on single-byte corruption ShardReader::open returns Err
Binary search on TrigramTable for absent trigram None
Delta+varint decode: DocId sequence ascending property test, 10 000 cases
PostingList iterator: no heap allocation per item confirmed by allocator shim in test
ShardHeader magic check on truncated file Err(InvalidMagic)
Deterministic build output byte-identical for identical input sequence

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected
fixtures/shard/small_shard.bin 3-doc, 10-trigram shard CRC valid; all docs readable
fixtures/shard/truncated.bin header cut at byte 32 open returns Err
fixtures/shard/corrupt_crc.bin last 4 bytes flipped verify_crc returns Err
fixtures/shard/empty_posting.bin one trigram has 0 docs posting_list returns None/empty
fixtures/shard/maxdoc_shard.bin n_docs = u32::MAX reader handles; DocId wraps cleanly
fixtures/shard/roundtrip_vectors.json 50 trigram→docid lists expected encoded byte sequences

3.6 Pitfalls

  • Unsigned varint overflow: DocId - prev > u32::MAX (corrupt data only; impossible with dense ids) → decoder returns Err, never panics.
  • TrigramTable unsorted at write: binary search needs sorted order; PostingWriter::finish must sort by Trigram; assert sortedness in write_shard.
  • DocMetaTable offset misalignment: unaligned PostingListBlob can push doc_table_off into padding; use 8-byte alignment between sections.
  • CRC computed before its own field is written: CRC covers [0, crc_off); compute after all sections, before the final 4 bytes — a classic off-by-one.
  • mmap on Windows: memmap2 semantics differ; CI tests Linux only. Phase 14 adds a read-based fallback behind a feature flag.
  • Shard open on empty file: mmap of a 0-byte file panics on some platforms; detect and return Err(EmptyShard) first.
  • Non-contiguous DocId assignment: future batching must still assign dense DocIds from 0 per shard; derive from insertion order in IndexBuilder.

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • BTreeMap<Trigram, Vec<DocId>> in PostingWriter: ~40M entries for 1M trigrams × 10K docs; pre-size with entry().or_insert_with(|| Vec::with_capacity(16)).
  • finish() sort: Vec::sort_unstable (cache-friendly); DocIds are unique so stable sort is unneeded.
  • PostingList iterator: one LEB128 decode per next(); LLVM unrolls short lists; no branch in the hot loop.
  • mmap page faults on first access: warm up by sequential scan during merge (Phase 12); no madvise in Phase 3 (added Phase 13).
  • CRC32 via crc32fast (hardware-accelerated); streaming over a 100 MB shard < 1 ms.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • n_trigrams = 0 / n_docs = 0: both valid; TrigramTable/DocMetaTable empty; posting_list always None.
  • Trigram in every doc: posting-list length = n_docs; varint handles any count up to u32::MAX.
  • Single-doc posting list: delta = raw DocId; decode handles the first-entry special case.
  • doc_meta(DocId >= n_docs): returns Err(DocIdOutOfRange), never an OOB index.
  • Concurrent readers, same file: read-only mmap; POSIX allows concurrent read-mmap on one fd; safe.
  • Shard file deleted while reader open: mmap holds the inode; Linux reads continue correctly (documented as expected).

3.9 Failure Analysis

  • Corrupt shard (disk error): CRC detects on open; open returns Err; executor skips the shard, logs a warning, alerts operator.
  • Partial write (crash mid-build): incomplete shard fails CRC; build writes to temp path + atomic rename, never overwrites a valid shard in place.
  • Wrong posting_off: decoder reads garbage; LEB128 decode may not error immediately — debug-mode invariant checks each decoded DocId > previous.
  • Blast radius: one corrupt shard affects only its doc range; others serve correctly; Phase 12 incremental rebuild regenerates it.
  • Operator visibility: shard.open_error_count, shard.crc_fail_count, shard.doc_count, shard.trigram_count.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • ShardReader::open logs INFO: shard path, version, n_docs, n_trigrams, file size.
  • verify_crc runs at open; also exposed via csearch-cli doctor --shard <path>.
  • Metric shard.posting_list_decode_ns_p99 collected during Phase 13 search fan-out.
  • ShardHeader.version bumped on any breaking layout change; reader rejects mismatches with Err(VersionMismatch { expected, found }).
  • Shards never modified post-write; rollback = swap manifest to the previous shard set (Phase 12 atomicity, I7).

Phase 4 — Index Builder

4.1 Objectives

  • Drive the full index pipeline: csearch-ingest file stream → csearch-trigram extraction → csearch-index postings accumulation → csearch-shard writer.
  • Enforce bounded-memory build via spill-to-disk segmentation and segment merge.
  • Capture DocMeta (path, repo, lang, size, sha256, line_count) per ingested document.
  • Assign deterministic DocId values within each shard; produce a build manifest listing all shard paths and their RepoId coverage.
  • Constraints: peak RSS must not exceed a configurable --mem-limit (default 2 GiB); merge must preserve ascending DocId order and correct posting-list sort.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: B2 Ch.4 (SPIMI bounded-memory build), P14 (MapReduce sort-merge inspiration), I2 (posting order preserved across spill/merge), ADR-04 (custom binary format), §3.2 (shard format contract that builder must produce) -->

Module: csearch-index/src/builder.rs

Struct / fn Responsibility
IndexBuilder::new(cfg) Holds in-memory postings map HashMap<Trigram, Vec<DocId>>, doc-meta table Vec<DocMeta>, current DocId counter, memory watermark.
IndexBuilder::ingest_doc(meta, trigrams) Append DocMeta; for each Trigram in the doc's set, push DocId into the postings map; after each doc, sample RSS via /proc/self/status or jemalloc_stats; trigger spill when watermark exceeds threshold.
IndexBuilder::spill_segment() -> SegmentPath Sort each posting list in the in-memory map; call csearch-shard ShardWriter to flush a temporary segment shard; clear in-memory state; record segment path.
IndexBuilder::merge_segments(segs) -> ShardPath N-way merge of segment shards using a BinaryHeap<(Trigram, SegmentCursor)>; for each Trigram, concatenate sorted DocId runs (already sorted per segment, global sort by merge order); delta+varint encode the merged list; write final shard via ShardWriter.
IndexBuilder::finalize() -> BuildManifest If no segments, write a single shard directly; otherwise merge all segments; compute shard CRC; write BuildManifest (JSON, listing shard paths, per-shard doc range, RepoId set, build timestamp).

DocId assignment: sequential counter, reset to 0 per shard; globally unique identifier is (ShardId, DocId).

Memory watermark: poll after every WATERMARK_POLL_INTERVAL documents (configurable, default 512); use libc::mallinfo2 or jemalloc_ctl epoch + stats; compare against mem_limit * 0.80 (20% headroom for merge overhead).

csearch-index/src/merge.rs: SegmentMerger — opens N PostingReader iterators simultaneously; k-way merge keyed by Trigram; on Trigram tie, concatenate DocId lists (segments processed in ingestion order so DocIds are non-overlapping); emit merged posting to PostingWriter.

BuildManifest schema (JSON):

{ "shards": [{ "path", "shard_id", "doc_range": [lo, hi], "repo_ids": [...], "trigram_count", "doc_count" }], "built_at" }

Observability hooks: emit tracing::info! spans at spill_segment, merge_segments, finalize; counters: docs_ingested, trigrams_total, spills, merge_duration_ms.

4.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p csearch-index passes all unit tests.
  • Binary csearch-cli index <repo-path> writes a valid shard file and manifest.json.
  • csearch-cli stat <shard> reports doc count, trigram count, shard size, CRC status.
  • Ingesting a 10 GiB corpus with --mem-limit 1g produces correct results without OOM.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Round-trip correctness Every DocMeta.sha256 in the shard matches the on-disk file hash
DocId monotonicity shard.doc_ids() is strictly ascending within each posting list
Memory ceiling Peak RSS ≤ mem_limit * 1.05 for any corpus
Spill + merge correctness Output shard trigram coverage identical to a single-pass in-memory build on the same corpus (proptest over random 500-doc sets)
Manifest completeness manifest.json lists all shards; shard_id values are unique
CRC validity csearch-cli doctor reports no CRC failures

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/index/tiny-repo/ (50 files, known trigrams) Baseline build Exact trigram set + DocMeta match golden JSON
fixtures/index/empty-file.txt Zero-byte doc Shard contains DocMeta, posting lists for this doc absent
fixtures/index/unicode-mixed/ UTF-8, Latin-1, binary mix Binary files excluded; UTF-8 trigrams correct
fixtures/index/spill-trigger/ (synthetic 10k doc set) Forced spill at low mem limit Post-merge shard identical to in-memory reference
fixtures/index/duplicate-sha/ Two files with identical content Two DocMeta entries, same sha256; each doc independently queryable
fixtures/index/corrupt-segment.shard Malformed spill segment merge_segments returns Err(CorruptSegment); no partial shard emitted

4.6 Pitfalls

  • Memory watermark lag: mallinfo2 can lag real RSS; allocator arenas hold freed memory. Mitigation: poll both mallinfo2 and /proc/self/status VmRSS; use the larger.
  • Segment DocId collision: if a spill resets the DocId counter, two segments have overlapping DocIds, corrupting the merge. Invariant: DocId counter is never reset; it carries across spills within one shard build.
  • Trigram sort inversion after merge: if segments are merged out of ingestion order, DocId lists within a trigram are not ascending. Enforce strict segment-ordered merge; assert sorted output in debug builds.
  • Large posting list in-memory explosion: a single very-common trigram (e.g., th) can produce millions of DocId entries before a spill. Mitigation: per-trigram in-memory list capped; spill early when any single list exceeds MAX_LIST_ENTRIES (configurable, default 1M).
  • Partial shard on crash: shard file written but manifest not updated. On startup, csearch-cli doctor detects shards absent from manifest; recovery: re-run merge or delete orphaned shards.
  • Determinism break from HashMap ordering: iteration order of HashMap<Trigram, Vec<DocId>> is not deterministic across runs on stable Rust (random seed). Mitigation: sort trigrams before writing the trigram table; DocId order is controlled by ingestion sequence.

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Trigram table write: sorting N trigrams at spill time is O(N log N); for N=16M unique trigrams this is ~0.5s; acceptable per spill, but avoid repeated re-sorts across spills.
  • Merge I/O: k-way merge reads N segment files concurrently; use BufReader with 256 KiB buffers; avoid per-byte I/O.
  • DocMeta serialization: encode with bincode or flatbuffers; avoid JSON per-doc (3–5× size overhead).
  • Hash map memory: HashMap<Trigram, Vec<DocId>> has ~40-byte overhead per entry; for 16M trigrams that is ~640 MiB for the map alone before the DocId vecs; monitor and spill proactively.
  • Spill amplification: many small spills increase merge cost quadratically. Tune WATERMARK_POLL_INTERVAL and mem_limit to minimize spill count; log spill count at INFO.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-document repo: finalize() must still emit a valid (empty) shard and a manifest entry.
  • Single file > MAX_FILE_SIZE: csearch-ingest filters before reaching IndexBuilder; assert in ingest_doc that meta.size <= MAX_FILE_SIZE.
  • Trigram extraction fails (e.g., decompression error mid-file): skip the document; log at WARN with path and reason; increment skip_count metric.
  • Shard file write fails mid-stream (disk full): ShardWriter returns Err; builder cleans up partial file; propagates error; manifest is not updated.
  • Repo walk interrupted: builder holds an Option<BuildManifest>; on Drop without finalize(), log WARN "index build incomplete, no manifest written".

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Segment shard CRC mismatch on merge PostingReader::open returns Err(CrcFail) Shard not written Re-run index build from scratch for that repo
OOM during merge (insufficient headroom) Process killed by OOM killer Partial shard on disk doctor detects orphan; delete + re-index with higher mem_limit
DocMeta table truncated ShardHeader.doc_table_off points past EOF All queries on shard fail doctor reports bad shard; remove from manifest; re-index
Duplicate ShardId in manifest BuildManifest::validate() returns error Queries return duplicate results Fix manifest; assign unique ShardId via atomic counter in ManifestWriter

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: expose index_build_docs_total, index_build_spills_total, index_build_duration_seconds, index_shard_size_bytes (Prometheus-style counters/gauges).
  • Tracing: tracing spans for spill_segment (with doc_count, trigram_count, mem_used_bytes fields) and merge_segments (with segment_count, output_size_bytes).
  • Logging: INFO on each spill and final shard write; WARN on skipped docs; ERROR on unrecoverable I/O.
  • Deployment: csearch-cli index is offline; server does not need to be paused. Manifest swap (Phase 12) makes new shards live atomically.
  • Migration: shard format versioned via ShardHeader.version; builder stamps current FORMAT_VERSION; reader rejects unknown versions with clear error.

Phase 5 — Linear-Time Regex Matcher

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement csearch-regex: parse a regex string into regex-syntax Hir, compile a Thompson NFA, execute a Pike VM that guarantees O(|text| × |NFA|) time for any input.
  • Support: anchors (^ $), character classes ([a-z], \w, .), alternation, concatenation, repetition (* + ? {n,m}), capture groups (for MatchSpan extraction), case-insensitive flag.
  • Used exclusively for candidate verification (Phase 8); must never exhibit catastrophic backtracking.
  • Constraint: no use of the regex crate's backtracking engine for verification; use only the NFA/Pike VM path.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P2 (Thompson NFA linear-time proof), P3 (Pike VM thread-list mechanics), B4 Ch.3.7 (NFA simulation), B5 (entire Cox 2007 article), I4 (linear-time invariant), ADR-08 (custom PikeVm over regex crate), R2 (google/codesearch regexp/nfa.go reference) -->

Module layout (csearch-regex/src/):

File Contents
parse.rs Thin wrapper: parse(pattern: &str, flags: Flags) -> Result<Hir, ParseError> via regex-syntax::Parser
nfa.rs Nfa struct: states: Vec<NfaState>, start: StateId; NfaState enum: Match, ByteRange { lo, hi, next }, Split { a, b }, Capture { slot, next }. compile(hir: &Hir) -> Nfa. Thompson construction: alternation → Split; concatenation → chain; * → Split + back-edge; + → required prefix + Split; ? → Split; {n,m} → unrolled finite repetitions + optional tail.
pikevm.rs PikeVm struct. fn run<'a>(&self, nfa: &Nfa, text: &'a [u8], anchored: bool) -> Option<Vec<Capture>>. State: two Vec<(StateId, Captures)> alternating as current/next sets (dense bitset for visited-this-step to prevent duplicate state visits). Advance one byte at a time; epsilon-close via DFS with visited guard; O(
capture.rs Captures — [Option<usize>; MAX_GROUPS * 2] (start/end pairs); slot indices map to group numbers.
matcher.rs RegexMatcher::new(pattern, flags) -> Result<Self>. fn find_all<'a>(&self, text: &'a [u8]) -> Vec<ByteSpan>. Calls PikeVm::run in a loop advancing past each match; handles zero-length match advance.
line_index.rs LineIndex::new(text: &[u8]): builds Vec<u32> of newline offsets. fn byte_to_line_col(offset: usize) -> (u32, u32) via binary search. Used by csearch-search to compute MatchSpan.line/.col.

NFA compilation invariants:

  • Every Hir::Look (anchor) compiles to a zero-width assertion state; ^ checks offset == 0 || text[offset-1] == b'\n'.
  • Hir::Dot (.) compiles to ByteRange { lo: 0x00, hi: 0xFF, next } with \n exclusion when default dot-not-newline.
  • {n,m} with m > MAX_REPEAT_UNROLL (default 64): unroll n required iterations then m-n optional Splits; if m == unbounded, use back-edge Split.
  • Unicode character classes: expand to byte-range sequences covering each code-point range's UTF-8 encodings; stored as sorted Vec<(u8, u8)> byte ranges.

Pike VM execution model:

for each byte b at offset i:
  for each (state, caps) in current_set (in priority order):
    epsilon_close(state, caps, next_set, visited)
    if state matches ByteRange covering b: add (next_state, caps) to next_set
  swap current_set / next_set; clear next_set; reset visited bitset

5.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p csearch-regex passes all unit and property tests.
  • RegexMatcher correctly finds all non-overlapping matches in a byte slice.
  • LineIndex converts byte offsets to (line, col) accurately.
  • No regex input causes super-linear runtime (demonstrated by benchmark harness).

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Correctness vs reference Differential test vs grep -oP on 10k pattern+input pairs: zero discrepancies
Linear-time guarantee Benchmark: (a+)+ on 1000-byte adversarial input completes in < 1 ms
Capture correctness All capture groups in test suite match regex crate results
Anchor correctness ^foo$ matches only full-line occurrences; no off-by-one
Zero-length match handling /a*/ on "b" returns one zero-length match at offset 0, then advances
Case-insensitive /foo/i matches FOO, Foo, fOo

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
fixtures/regex/anchors.txt ^, $, \b patterns Line-by-line expected match offsets
fixtures/regex/classes.txt [a-z], \w, \d, ., negated classes Match spans
fixtures/regex/alternation.txt foo|bar|baz All occurrences
fixtures/regex/quantifiers.txt *, +, ?, {3,5} Greedy leftmost-longest
fixtures/regex/adversarial.txt (a+)+, (a*)*, (a|a)*b on long inputs Completes < 5 ms per pattern
fixtures/regex/unicode-classes.txt \p{L}, [À-ÿ] UTF-8 correct spans
fixtures/regex/captures.txt Named + numbered capture groups Slot indices match expected
fixtures/regex/empty-input.txt Patterns on zero-byte files find_all returns empty vec

5.6 Pitfalls

  • Epsilon cycle → infinite loop: Thompson NFA can have epsilon cycles via Split; the DFS epsilon-closure MUST track visited states in the same byte-step's bitset, not across bytes. Symptom: CPU spin. Debug: add visited.contains(state) assertion before recursion.
  • Capture slot clobber on split: when a Split spawns two threads, each must get an independent copy of Captures; sharing a Captures by reference causes second-thread writes to overwrite first-thread saves. Always clone on Split.
  • {n,m} NFA blowup: unrolling {1000,2000} produces 1000-2000 NFA states; csearch-trigquery should reject patterns with unrolled count > MAX_UNROLL before reaching matcher. Add a compile-time guard: return Err(PatternTooLarge) if NFA exceeds MAX_NFA_STATES (default 16384).
  • Zero-length match infinite loop: after a zero-length match at offset i, advancing by 0 loops forever. Rule: after a zero-length match, advance by 1 byte unconditionally before the next run call.
  • Byte vs. char boundary in ByteRange: UTF-8 multi-byte sequences span multiple ByteRange states; computing incorrect line/col if you treat byte offset as char offset. LineIndex must use byte offsets throughout.

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Current/next set allocation: allocate both sets once at matcher construction, clear per byte; avoid per-byte Vec::new.
  • Visited bitset: use a fixed-size [u64; NFA_STATES/64] stack array when NFA_STATES <= 1024; heap bitvec otherwise.
  • Capture clone cost: cloning Captures per Split is the dominant cost for patterns with many groups; cap MAX_GROUPS at 32; use Copy array.
  • LineIndex construction: O(|file|) once per candidate file; cache within a single search request if the same file appears in multiple shard results.
  • Branch prediction: the inner byte loop's ByteRange check is highly predictable for simple character classes; the interpreter overhead dominates for very short files (< 64 bytes); acceptable since those are cheap to scan linearly.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • Pattern .*: compiles to a single Split + back-edge; Pike VM matches entire input in one pass; correctly returns a single span covering all bytes.
  • Multi-line input with ^/$: must match at each \n boundary, not only at offset 0; test fixture anchors.txt covers this.
  • Null bytes in file: ByteRange { lo: 0x00, hi: 0x00 } is a valid NFA state; no special-casing.
  • Very long lines (> 1 MB): LineIndex binary search still O(log n_lines); no per-byte scan at lookup time.
  • Pattern compilation error: parse.rs returns ParseError with byte offset of the offending token; csearch-query must propagate this as a user-visible error.
  • Overlapping matches: find_all returns non-overlapping leftmost-longest matches; document this clearly; symbol search may need find_all_overlapping variant.

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
NFA too large (> MAX_NFA_STATES) compile() returns Err(PatternTooLarge) Single query rejected Return 400 to client with error message
Regex parse error parse() returns Err(ParseError) Single query rejected Surface error to user
Pike VM super-linear blowup Not possible by construction; if NFA has back-edges, visited bitset prevents re-entry N/A Verify with adversarial benchmark in CI
Capture slot OOB slot >= MAX_GROUPS * 2 in debug assert Panic (dev), UB (release) Add release-mode bounds check; return Err

5.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: regex_compile_duration_us, regex_match_duration_us (histogram), regex_nfa_states (per-query gauge), regex_match_calls_total.
  • Tracing: tracing::debug! on compile(pattern) with nfa_state_count; tracing::trace! on run(len) with match_count.
  • No persistent state: RegexMatcher is constructed per query from the parsed Hir; no caching needed for correctness (Phase 8 may add a query-level LRU cache as an optimization).
  • Compatibility: csearch-regex has no dependency on the regex crate's Exec engine; only regex-syntax for Hir is imported. This enforces the no-backtracking constraint at the dependency level.
  • Testing in CI: adversarial benchmark (linear-time assertion) runs as a CI step; failure blocks merge.

Phase 6 — Regex → Trigram Query Lowering

6.1 Objectives

  • Implement csearch-trigquery: given a regex Hir, compute a TrigramQuery that is a sound over-approximation of the match set.
  • Soundness (I1): for every doc D, if D matches the regex then D satisfies the TrigramQuery — no true match filtered out.
  • Precision is best-effort; false positives (extra candidates) are acceptable.
  • Handle: literals → AND of trigrams; alternation → OR of sub-queries; concatenation → AND of sub-queries; ./.*/unbounded repetition → All; anchors → transparent; ambiguous classes → All; case-insensitive → expand or All.
  • Output: TrigramQuery enum consumed by csearch-search (Phase 8) for posting-list intersection.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P1 (Cox 2012 — complete lowering algorithm), I1 (soundness: no false negatives), P2 (no false negatives principle), §8.7 (TrigramQuery state machine diagram), P12 (Culpepper SvS — cost ordering of AND operands), ADR-09 (custom HIR visitor over DFA minimization) -->

Module csearch-trigquery/src/lower.rs: pub fn lower(hir: &Hir, case_insensitive: bool) -> TrigramQuery — recursive descent over Hir:

Hir variant Lowering rule
Literal(bytes) extract overlapping trigrams → And(trigrams.map(Trigram))
Concat(children) AND of lowered children; drop All terms (vacuous)
Alternation(children) OR of lowered children; any child All → whole node All
Repetition{min:0} (*, ?) All — zero occurrences guarantees nothing
Repetition{min≥1, inner} fixed-length inner → extract required trigrams; else All
Look (^ $ \b) transparent → All (carries no trigram info)
Dot All
