Class(ranges), single-byte contributes to surrounding-literal trigram extraction
Class(ranges), otherwise All
Capture(inner) lower(inner) — transparent

Trigram extraction: trigrams_of(bytes): len<3 → []; else sliding 3-byte windows packed via Trigram::pack. Result is ANDed — a doc missing any one trigram cannot match the literal.

Case-insensitive expansion: expand each ASCII alpha byte to both cases, Or over the resulting trigram variants; expanded set > MAX_CASE_EXPAND (default 256) → All.

Simplification pass (csearch-trigquery/src/simplify.rs): And([x])→x; And([])→All; Or([x])→x; Or([])→Any (unsatisfiable regex; caller rejects); And drops any All member; Or containing All→All; flatten nested And(And(..))/Or(Or(..)); dedup trigrams within a node.

Soundness obligation (fuzz-enforced, not formally proved): every rule is conservative — default to All when unsure; a non-All result requires the subtree provably contain those trigrams in every matching string. Property: lower(hir) evaluated on doc_trigrams is true whenever hir.matches(doc).

Module csearch-trigquery/src/eval.rs: pub fn eval(query: &TrigramQuery, doc_trigrams: &HashSet<Trigram>) -> bool — used only by fuzz/unit tests to check soundness; never on the hot search path (posting-list intersection is used instead).

6.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p csearch-trigquery passes all unit tests.
  • lower(hir) returns a TrigramQuery for any valid Hir without panicking.
  • Soundness fuzz harness: 10,000 random regex + document pairs; zero false negatives observed.
  • Precision report: on a reference corpus, the average candidate reduction factor is ≥ 10× for literal-containing queries.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Soundness (I1) Fuzz: 0 false negatives over 1M random (regex, document) pairs
Literal query precision lower("foobar") produces And([Trigram("foo"), Trigram("oob"), Trigram("oba"), Trigram("bar")]) — exactly 4 trigrams
Alternation lower("foo|bar") = Or([And([...foo trigrams...]), And([...bar trigrams...])])
Wildcard degrades to All lower(".*") = All; lower("a*b") = All (due to a*)
Short literal (< 3 bytes) lower("ab") = All (no 3-gram extractable)
Case-insensitive literal lower("Foo", case_insensitive=true) returns Or(...) covering all case variants or All
Simplification And([All, T1, T2]) simplifies to And([T1, T2])

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected TrigramQuery
fixtures/trigquery/literal.txt "function" And of 6 trigrams
fixtures/trigquery/alternation.txt "err|error|panic" Or of 3 And nodes
fixtures/trigquery/concat.txt "foo.*bar" And([And(foo-trigrams), All, And(bar-trigrams)]) → simplifies to And([foo-trigrams..., bar-trigrams...])
fixtures/trigquery/short.txt "ab" All
fixtures/trigquery/dot.txt "a.b" All (dot is one wildcard byte, span < 3)
fixtures/trigquery/class-exact.txt "[fF]oo" Or([And(foo-trigrams), And(Foo-trigrams)])
fixtures/trigquery/class-wide.txt "[a-z]oo" All (26 variants exceed threshold or lack fixed prefix)
fixtures/trigquery/anchored.txt "^import " Anchors are transparent; same as lower("import ")
fixtures/trigquery/soundness-fuzz 1M random pairs Zero false negatives

6.6 Pitfalls

  • Concat drops All but Or must not: dropping All from And is safe (vacuous); dropping it from Or over-restricts. Guard: Or containing All → All.
  • Short alternation arm silently widens: lower("foobar|x") = Or([And(6), All]) = All since "x" (<3B) lowers to All. Correct by soundness, surprising for precision — document that any arm shorter than 3 bytes forces All.
  • Unicode case folding: "ß"→"SS" changes byte count; expand via regex-syntax's fold table, never hand-roll.
  • Nested alternation explosion: k arms × n nestings → k^n nodes; cap MAX_OR_NODES (default 64), else All.
  • Fixed-count repetition ≥3 ("foo{3}" = "foofoo"): the Repetition handler must detect min==max and flatten to literal before lowering.
  • Empty regex: lower("") = All; never panic.

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • lower sits on the query hot path: < 100 µs for typical patterns; no heap allocation in the common literal case.
  • Deep And/Or trees allocate many Vec nodes; patterns lowering to All return early, building no tree.
  • Dedup: HashSet<Trigram> is O(n), fine below 1000 trigrams; sort+dedup is cache-friendlier at larger scale.
  • Case-insensitive expansion is O(3^k) in alpha-byte count k; MAX_CASE_EXPAND bounds the blowup.
  • eval is test-only; never called on the production search path.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Regex matching the empty string ("a*", "a?") lowers to All; correct — the empty string trivially satisfies any trigram query.
  • Regex matching no string ("a(?!a)a") may lower to a non-All query that filters every doc — sound, since no true matches exist.
  • Literal > 1 MB: O(n) extraction; dedup keeps the And node bounded; intersection stays fast.
  • Hir variants from future regex-syntax releases: catch-all arm returns All; never panic on unrecognized input.
  • sym: queries bypass trigram lowering entirely; use the symbol posting index directly.

6.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
False negative (I1 violation) Soundness fuzz test fails A true match is not returned — critical correctness bug Treat as P0; review the lowering rule for the offending Hir variant; add fixture; fix and re-fuzz
OR node explosion (> MAX_OR_NODES) lower returns All Reduced precision, more candidates Acceptable by design; log at DEBUG that query degraded to All
lower panics on valid Hir Crash Query fails with 500 Add catch-all arm; add the triggering pattern to fuzz corpus
Case-fold table mismatch vs regex-syntax Differential test fails Case-insensitive search returns incorrect candidates Recompute fold table from regex-syntax at build time; do not hard-code

6.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: trigquery_lower_duration_us (histogram), trigquery_result_type (counter by variant), trigquery_trigram_count (gauge).
  • Tracing: tracing::debug!(pattern, result_type, trigram_count) per lower call.
  • Fuzz corpus fuzz/trigquery/; CI runs 60s/PR on csearch-trigquery; any panic or false-negative blocks merge.
  • lower is pure — no cache, no shared state; safe to call concurrently.
  • Upgrade path: new Hir variant is caught by the catch-all first; precise lowering is added as a follow-up, never a regression.

Phase 7 — Query Language Parser

7.1 Objectives

  • Implement csearch-query crate: tokenizer + recursive-descent parser for the full query surface.
  • Support query forms: bare literal, /regex/, file:RE, lang:go, case:yes|no, sym:Name, repo:RE.
  • Produce QueryAst + Vec<Filter> from a raw string; return structured ParseError for all malformed inputs.
  • No I/O; no index access; pure transformation of string -> AST.
  • Introduce QueryAst, Filter, ParseError as stable public API consumed by csearch-search.

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: B10 Ch.16 (hand-written lexer patterns), §8.6 (QueryAst grammar), ADR-10 (regex-syntax Hir frontend), S7 (gitignore pattern spec, relevant to lang:/file: semantics), §11.5 (QueryAst types) -->

Tokenizer (csearch-query/src/lexer.rs):

Token kind Rule
Slash(str) /…/ — content between unescaped slashes; forbid unclosed
Field(key,val) word:value — key is [a-zA-Z]+, value is bare or quoted
Quoted(str) "…" — C-style escapes \n \t \\ \"
Bare(str) non-whitespace run not matching above
And/Or/Not keywords; lowercase only; if bare-context → literal

Parser (csearch-query/src/parser.rs):

  • Top-level: sequence of terms joined by implicit AND; or keyword separates OR branches; not unary prefix.
  • Precedence (low→high): or < implicit-and < not; parentheses for grouping.
  • Field tokens parsed into Filter variants; remaining terms fold into QueryAst::And / Literal / Regex.
  • Regex field: parse /pattern/ with optional flags suffix (i → case:no override).
  • case: filter normalizes to bool; conflicts last-wins.
  • Emit ParseError { offset, kind, message } with span; never panic on any input.

AST construction (csearch-query/src/ast.rs):

  • QueryAst::Literal(String) — single unanchored string term.
  • QueryAst::Regex(String) — validated regex string (parse to Hir via csearch-regex; reject if parse fails).
  • QueryAst::And(Vec<QueryAst>) / Or(Vec<QueryAst>) / Not(Box<QueryAst>).
  • QueryAst::Field(Filter) — field query folded into the AST for filter dispatch.
  • ParsedQuery { ast: QueryAst, filters: Vec<Filter>, raw: String } — top-level output.

Error messages: include offset, expected token, and a short human-readable suggestion (e.g., "unclosed regex delimiter at offset 12").

7.3 Deliverable

  • csearch-query compiles as standalone crate with no I/O dependencies.
  • ParsedQuery::parse(s: &str) -> Result<ParsedQuery, ParseError> stable public fn.
  • CLI csearch parse "query" prints the AST + filters as JSON for inspection.
  • Property tests: parse(q).raw == q for all valid inputs; error on unclosed /, mismatched parens.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • 100+ unit tests covering literals, regex, all filter types, error paths.
  • Fuzz target fuzz_parse: zero panics after 10M iterations on arbitrary byte strings.
  • parse completes < 100 µs for any query ≤ 4 KB.
  • All ParseError variants include a non-empty message string.
  • Round-trip: ParsedQuery serialises to JSON and deserialises with serde without loss.

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Input Expected AST summary Error?
q_bare_literal.txt foo bar And[Lit("foo"),Lit("bar")] no
q_regex.txt /fn\s+\w+/ Regex("fn\\s+\\w+") no
q_filters.txt foo file:\.go lang:go case:yes Lit + File+Lang+Case filters no
q_sym.txt sym:HashMap Field(Sym("HashMap")) no
q_unclosed_regex.txt /unclosed — ParseError(UnclosedDelimiter)
q_bad_field.txt lang: — ParseError(EmptyFieldValue)
q_nested_parens.txt (a or b) and c And[Or[Lit,Lit],Lit] no
q_quoted.txt "hello world" Lit("hello world") no

7.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Detection Recovery
Slash in literal misread as regex delimiter a/b parsed as /b start Test: parse("a/b") = And[Lit("a"),Lit("b")] Require both open+close slash for regex token
Field value swallows subsequent terms lang:go file:\.rs merges into one field Fixture q_filters.txt Tokenize field as single word:nonwhitespace unit
Unicode grapheme cluster spans token boundary Multi-byte glyph split at byte offset Fuzz with non-ASCII input Operate on &str char boundaries; never byte-slice mid-char
not keyword in identifier context notable parsed as Not Lit("able") Unit test: parse("notable") Require not followed by whitespace or (
Quoted string with lone backslash "foo\" triggers OOB read Fuzz; unit test Check escape at EOF → ParseError(UnterminatedEscape)
case: conflict (two occurrences) Ambiguous bool Fixture with duplicate field Last occurrence wins; emit ParseWarning(DuplicateField)

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • Parser is hot per-request; allocates one Vec<Token> per call — pre-size with query length heuristic.
  • Avoid String::new() inside tight token loop; use &str slices into the original query.
  • Error path: never clone the full query string into the error; store only offset: usize.
  • Serde serialisation of QueryAst is off the hot path; allocations there are acceptable.

7.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty string: returns QueryAst::And([]) — trivially matches everything; search executor interprets as scan.
  • Query of only whitespace: same as empty.
  • Solo or / not: ParseError(UnexpectedKeyword).
  • Deeply nested parens (> 64 levels): return ParseError(MaxDepthExceeded) — prevent stack overflow.
  • NUL bytes in query string: strip or ParseError(InvalidByte).
  • repo: with regex containing : — value is everything after first : until whitespace.

7.9 Failure Analysis

  • Bad parse propagates to search: executor must surface ParseError as HTTP 400 with message; never search on malformed AST.
  • Panic in parser: would crash the server worker thread; fuzz coverage + catch_unwind wrapper in csearch-server route handler.
  • Regex compile failure: /bad[regex/ compiles fine by csearch-regex but [regex is invalid; detect at parse time, not at search time.
  • Blast radius: single request; no index state mutation; recovery is returning error to caller.

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Metric: query_parse_error_total{kind} counter per error variant.
  • Log: structured INFO log per parsed query: {raw, ast_variant, filter_count, parse_µs}.
  • csearch parse subcommand is the primary debugging tool for malformed queries.
  • Version QueryAst with a #[non_exhaustive] enum annotation; adding a new variant is backward-compatible.

Phase 8 — Search Executor

8.1 Objectives

  • Implement csearch-search crate: end-to-end query execution pipeline.
  • Pipeline: ParsedQuery → TrigramQuery (via csearch-trigquery) → posting-list intersection → candidate DocId set → field filter → PikeVm verify → MatchSpan collection → rank → Vec<SearchResult>.
  • Enforce result cap and per-query deadline; short-circuit when either is hit.
  • Emit P10 observability counters at each stage.
  • Consume csearch-shard (mmap reader), csearch-regex (PikeVm), csearch-trigquery (lowering), csearch-rank.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P1 (candidate-then-verify), P2 (no false negatives), P7 (Sanders galloping, P8 Strohman early termination, P12 Culpepper SvS ordering), B2 Ch.1-2 (boolean retrieval AND/OR algebra), I1 (soundness of candidate set), I6 (MatchSpan exactness), P6 (bounded latency deadline), ADR-12 (galloping intersection) -->

Pipeline stages (csearch-search/src/executor.rs):

ParsedQuery
  │ lower_to_trigram_query(ast) → TrigramQuery        [csearch-trigquery]
  │ open_shards(manifest) → Vec<ShardHandle>          [csearch-shard]
  ↓
per-shard fan-out (rayon par_iter or tokio spawn):
  │ intersect_postings(shard, tq) → Vec<DocId>        galloping
  │ apply_field_filters(docs, filters, shard)         path/lang/repo
  │ verify_candidates(docs, pattern, shard) → spans   PikeVm per doc
  ↓
merge spans from all shards → rank → top-K → SearchResult

Posting-list intersection (csearch-search/src/intersect.rs): galloping intersection advances the shorter list via exponential probe then binary search; Or branches merge two sorted iterators; TrigramQuery::All/Any skip intersection (all shard DocIds are candidates); AND operands are sorted by posting-list length ascending before intersecting (cost estimation). Field filter (csearch-search/src/filter.rs): Filter::File(re) matches DocMeta.path via PikeVm (compiled once/query); Filter::Lang(s) compares DocMeta.lang lowercase; Filter::Repo(re) matches DocMeta.repo; Filter::Case(bool) sets the regex compile flag passed to csearch-regex; Filter::Sym(name) routes to the symbol index path (Phase 10) — stubbed in Phase 8. Candidate verification (csearch-search/src/verify.rs): loads doc content from the shard doc-content store (raw bytes at stored offset); compiles Nfa once per query, not per doc; runs PikeVm::find_all(content, nfa) → Vec<(byte_offset, len)>; maps byte offsets to (line, col) by scanning newlines up to each match start; emits MatchSpan per match, capped at spans_per_doc_cap (default 100). Deadline and result cap (csearch-search/src/executor.rs): accepts SearchOptions { deadline: Instant, result_cap: usize, spans_per_doc_cap: usize }; checks the deadline at the top of each shard's work unit, abandoning remaining shards if exceeded; accumulates into a BoundedHeap<SearchResult, K=result_cap> (min-heap on score); returns SearchResponse { results, truncated: bool, stats: QueryStats }. QueryStats (csearch-core/src/types.rs): { shards_touched, candidates_total, candidates_filtered, verified_total, matches_total, truncated, elapsed_µs_by_stage }.

8.3 Deliverable

  • csearch-search compiles; search(manifest, query_str, opts) -> Result<SearchResponse> fn works end-to-end.
  • Integration test: build a small index (Phase 4 fixture), run search, assert correct MatchSpan values.
  • csearch search "pattern" CLI subcommand prints results as JSON.
  • QueryStats printed to stderr in debug mode.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Zero false negatives: for corpus of 10k files, search result set ⊇ grep -rE result set (differential test).
  • False-positive rate (candidates that fail verification) ≤ 30% on a real source corpus.
  • Latency: p99 < 200 ms for literal/simple-regex queries on a 1 M-doc index with 8 shards.
  • Deadline enforcement: query with 50 ms deadline completes ≤ 60 ms wall-clock.
  • Result cap: result_cap=100 returns ≤ 100 results; truncated=true when more exist.
  • Galloping intersection unit tests: correctness on list pairs of length 1, equal, skewed 1000:1.

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Setup Query Expected
exec_literal.json 5 files, known content "hello" exact line/col spans
exec_regex.json same /fn\s+main/ spans in files containing that pattern
exec_filter_lang.json mixed Go+Rust files foo lang:rust only Rust files
exec_filter_file.json same bar file:\.go$ only .go paths
exec_no_match.json same /zzznomatch_xyz/ empty results, no error
exec_deadline.json large synthetic index 10 ms deadline truncated=true, elapsed ≤ 15 ms
exec_result_cap.json 200 matching docs result_cap=10 exactly 10 results, truncated=true

8.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Detection Recovery
NFA compiled per-document 100x slowdown on large candidate sets Benchmark: compile once vs per-doc Compile Nfa once outside verify loop; cache by query
Galloping skips required DocId False negative — doc omitted from candidates Differential test vs brute-force Invariant test: gallop result ⊆ naive intersection
Deadline checked too infrequently Server thread hangs past deadline Latency histogram spike Check deadline every N=256 candidates, not per-doc
TrigramQuery::All on sparse pattern Whole index scanned Monitor candidates_total metric Log warning when candidates > threshold; encourage narrower query
Case-insensitive fold not propagated Wrong matches under case:yes Fixture with mixed-case corpus Pass case flag through to both trigram lowering and NFA compile
Span byte-to-line mapping off-by-one Wrong line numbers in results Unit test: known file, known match Scan newlines inclusive of match start byte

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • Shard fan-out: rayon::par_iter across shards; tune parallelism to shard count, not CPU count.
  • Posting-list decode / candidate set: zero-copy via mmap, stream through PostingList<'a> (no Vec<u32> copy); pre-allocate the candidate Vec<DocId> to the estimated intersection size and reuse it across shards.
  • Content loading: mmap'd; PikeVm reads directly from &[u8] — no heap allocation for content.
  • BoundedHeap: fixed-size min-heap avoids unbounded Vec growth when result_cap is small.
  • Cache thrashing: DocId-ordered access within a shard is cache-friendly; cross-shard access hurts L3 — fan-out amortizes this.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • Query lowers to TrigramQuery::All (e.g., . or .*): executor issues a full scan; must still enforce deadline + cap.
  • Zero-length regex match (e.g., a*): find_all could loop; bound by requiring at least 1 advance per iteration.
  • File with no newlines: line=1, col=byte_offset+1 for all spans.
  • Shard opened during query (hot-reload): use snapshot of manifest at query start; new shards visible only to subsequent queries (I7).
  • All shards return zero candidates: SearchResponse { results:[], truncated:false } — not an error.
  • result_cap=0: treat as unlimited (or configurable max default, e.g. 1000).

8.9 Failure Analysis

  • Shard read error (mmap fault): caught per-shard; shard skipped; stats.shard_errors++; logged at ERROR; other shards continue.
  • PikeVm panic on malformed NFA: impossible if NFA compiled correctly in Phase 5; catch_unwind at verify boundary.
  • Deadline expired mid-shard: partial results returned with truncated=true; not an error from the caller's view.
  • Posting-list CRC mismatch: detected at shard open; shard excluded from fan-out; operator alerted via metric shard_crc_error_total.
  • OOM on large candidate set: bounded by design — posting lists are streamed; only candidate DocId vec materialised.

8.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: search_candidates_total, search_verified_total, search_matches_total, search_latency_µs{p50,p99}, search_truncated_total, shard_errors_total.
  • Trace span per query: stage breakdown (trigram-lower, intersect, filter, verify, rank) in µs.
  • Log at DEBUG: {query_id, tq_variant, shards_touched, candidates, verified, matches, elapsed_µs}.
  • csearch stat --query "foo" prints QueryStats without returning results (dry-run mode).
  • Hot-reload of manifest (Phase 12/13) requires no executor restart; executor snapshots manifest on each call.

Phase 9 — Repository Ingestion

9.1 Objectives

  • Implement csearch-ingest crate: recursive filesystem walk, language detection, binary/large-file filtering, content addressing, per-repo doc enumeration.
  • Produce a stream of (DocMeta, content: Bytes) tuples consumed by csearch-index.
  • Respect ignore rules (.gitignore, .ignore, custom patterns); handle symlinks safely; handle non-UTF-8 content safely.
  • Constraint: ingest must be deterministic given the same filesystem state (same sha → same DocId assignment).

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: I5 (sha256 content addressing), P9 (incremental rebuild), S7 (gitignore pattern spec), P13 (Cavnar N-gram language ID context), ADR-14 (extension+shebang+sniff priority order), R8 (ignore crate from ripgrep) -->

Walker (csearch-ingest/src/walk.rs): ignore crate (BurntSushi/ripgrep/ignore) applies .gitignore/.ignore/global patterns via WalkBuilder; parallel walk (build_parallel) feeds a (path, metadata) channel; symlinks to files are followed, symlinks to directories are not (cycle prevention); max_depth configurable (default 64); hidden directories skipped unless --include-hidden.

Language detection (csearch-ingest/src/lang.rs):

Priority Method Details
1 Extension map .rs→Rust, .go→Go, .py→Python, … (50+ entries); phf::Map<&str,&str> compile-time perfect hash
2 Shebang line #!/usr/bin/env python3 → Python; first line only, #! prefix, interpreter basename
3 Content sniff First 512 bytes: detect XML decl, HTML doctype, JSON brace
4 Unknown lang = "unknown" — still indexed

Binary / filter policy (csearch-ingest/src/filter.rs):

Check Threshold Action
File size > max_file_bytes (default 1 MB) Skip; log WARN
Binary sniff ≥ 1 NUL byte in first 8 KB Skip
Minified JS/CSS line length > 500 chars in first 5 lines Skip; log DEBUG
Zero-byte file size == 0 Skip (no trigrams)
Symlink to dir any Skip (walk policy)

Content addressing (csearch-ingest/src/sha.rs): sha256 over raw file bytes (post-read, pre-transform); DocMeta.sha256: [u8;32] stored verbatim; incremental check — (path, mtime, size) matches prior manifest AND sha matches → unchanged, skip re-index (I5).

DocMeta assembly (csearch-ingest/src/meta.rs): path canonical relative from repo root (UTF-8 only; non-UTF-8 rejected with WARN); repo from the RepoId ingest argument; lang from detection; size: u32 capped at max_file_bytes; sha256 as above; line_count from newline count + 1 during read.

Output stream (csearch-ingest/src/lib.rs): IngestStream::new(repo_root, opts) -> impl Iterator<Item=Result<IngestDoc, IngestError>>; IngestDoc { meta: DocMeta, content: Bytes }; IngestError variants Io, NonUtf8Path, TooLarge, PermissionDenied; errors are per-document — walk continues past per-file errors.

Encoding safety: content bytes stay raw (not UTF-8 decoded); Phase 2 trigram extraction operates on raw bytes; paths must be valid UTF-8 or are rejected.

9.3 Deliverable

  • csearch-ingest compiles; IngestStream produces IngestDoc for a real repo (e.g., the project's own source tree).
  • csearch index --repo . drives IngestStream → csearch-index; emits doc count, size stats.
  • csearch stat --ingest . prints per-language file/byte counts without building an index.
  • Skip stats logged: skipped binary count, oversized count, ignored count.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Deterministic: two runs on the same directory with same mtime produce identical DocMeta sequences (same order, same sha).
  • Binary detection: zero false negatives on a corpus of 100 known binary files (ELF, PNG, ZIP).
  • Language detection accuracy ≥ 95% on a sample of 1000 source files across 10 languages.
  • No symlink cycle: walk on a directory containing a circular symlink terminates.
  • No panic on any path containing non-ASCII characters; IngestError::NonUtf8Path emitted instead.
  • Throughput: ≥ 500 MB/s aggregate (disk-bound); measured on a warm-cache local filesystem.

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Content Expected outcome
ingest_basic/ 3 .rs, 1 .go, 1 .py 5 docs; correct lang per file
ingest_binary/ 1 ELF binary 0 docs; 1 skip-binary log
ingest_oversized/ 1 file of 2 MB (> default 1 MB) 0 docs; 1 skip-size log
ingest_gitignore/ .gitignore excludes target/ files under target/ not in stream
ingest_symlink_cycle/ a/ -> b/, b/ -> a/ walk terminates; no infinite loop
ingest_shebang/ file with #!/usr/bin/env python3, no extension lang=Python
ingest_nonutf8_path/ file with non-UTF-8 byte in path name IngestError::NonUtf8Path; walk continues
ingest_empty/ empty files skipped (zero-byte filter)
ingest_minified/ .js with 800-char lines skipped; debug log

9.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Detection Recovery
Symlink cycle → infinite walk Process hangs; inode table exhausts No progress for > N seconds WalkBuilder::follow_links(false) for dirs; track visited inodes
Reading file after metadata check (TOCTOU) File deleted between stat and read → panic Treat Io(NotFound) as skip Wrap read in match; emit IngestError::Io
Non-deterministic walk order Different DocId assignment across runs Property test: two walks same order Sort entries within directory before yielding
Binary sniff misses sparse binary NUL bytes only after first 8 KB Seed known corpus of sparse binaries Increase sniff window; accept occasional binary in index (not harmful)
.gitignore not parsed in sub-directories Vendor dirs not excluded Fixture with nested .gitignore WalkBuilder handles nested ignore files by default; verify in fixture
mtime granularity collision Two files with same mtime but different content Skip sha check → stale incremental Always compare sha; mtime is hint only
Hardlink overcounting Same inode indexed multiple times Fixture with hardlinks Track visited (dev, ino) set; skip duplicates

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • Parallel walk: WalkBuilder::build_parallel uses a work-stealing thread pool; tune threads = min(CPU_count, 8).
  • Content read + sha: read file once into a Bytes buffer; compute sha and count lines in a single pass.
  • Extension map: phf::Map for O(1) lookup; avoid HashMap with heap-allocated keys.
  • Channel backpressure: bounded channel between walker and index builder (capacity = 1024 docs); prevents unbounded memory if index build is slower than walk.
  • Line counting: single-pass memchr::Memchr::new(b'\n', content) iterator; faster than splitting.
  • Inode dedup set: use FxHashSet<(u64,u64)> for visited inodes; inserted once per file.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • Filesystem with case-insensitive paths (macOS HFS+): normalise path with dunce::canonicalize; warn if two logical paths map to the same inode.
  • File with write lock held by another process: Io(PermissionDenied) → IngestError; skip.
  • File grows during read: read_to_end returns partial content; sha computed on what was read; not an error, just a snapshot.
  • Repo root itself is a symlink to directory: resolve once at ingest start via canonicalize; subsequent walk uses the canonical path.
  • Very deep directory trees (> 64): emit IngestError::TooDeep; configurable with max_depth.
  • Files with only a BOM (UTF-8 EF BB BF): treat as content; not binary.
  • Repo with millions of small files: IngestDoc channel becomes the bottleneck; measure at 1M+ files.

9.9 Failure Analysis

  • Permission denied on entire subtree: log ERROR once per subtree root; remaining tree continues.
  • Disk read error (EIO): IngestError::Io; doc skipped; counter incremented; overall ingest not aborted.
  • OOM during content read: 1 MB cap per file bounds per-doc allocation; aggregate bounded by channel capacity × 1 MB.
  • Walk thread panic: WalkBuilder::build_parallel worker panics → channel closes → builder observes closed channel → clean shutdown with error.
  • Corrupt .gitignore: ignore crate ignores malformed patterns silently; acceptable — only affects which files are included, not correctness of indexed content.
  • Blast radius: per-document failures are isolated; partial ingest is valid (missing docs → missing from index, not wrong results).

9.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: ingest_docs_total{repo}, ingest_skipped_total{reason}, ingest_bytes_total, ingest_duration_µs.
  • Log: INFO at ingest start/end with {repo, doc_count, skip_count, elapsed_s}; WARN per oversized/binary skip if verbose_skip=true.
  • csearch stat --ingest <path> is a dry-run mode: walks, detects, counts — no index written; useful for auditing what would be indexed.
  • --exclude-pattern CLI flag appended to WalkBuilder ignore rules at runtime; documented in csearch index --help.
  • Incremental mode (Phase 12) reuses the same IngestStream; unchanged docs (sha match) yield IngestDoc with unchanged=true flag, allowing the index builder to skip trigram extraction.

Phase 10 — Symbol Index + Symbol Search

10.1 Objectives

  • Implement csearch-symbol: extract named definitions from source files without a full AST parse.
  • Build per-language heuristic/regex extractors for Go, Rust, Python, C/C++, TypeScript, Java, Ruby, Kotlin, Swift.
  • Write SymbolEntry records into a parallel posting index keyed by symbol name trigrams and exact-name lookup.
  • Integrate symbol section into the shard format via symbol_table_off in ShardHeader.
  • Expose sym:Name and sym:/regex/ query paths through csearch-query → csearch-search executor; rank definition hits above reference/context hits, enabling jump-to-definition with precise (ShardId, DocId, line).
  • Constraint: extraction runs in O(n) over file bytes; no subprocess invocations; no network.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: ADR-15 (ctags-style heuristic vs tree-sitter), R9 (universal-ctags tag format), S8 (Universal Ctags tag format spec), §8.12 (symbol index model), §11.3 (SymbolEntry), I5 (content addressing for symbol dedup) -->

Crate structure csearch-symbol/src/: lib.rs (re-exports SymbolExtractor trait, extract(), SymbolEntry) · extractor.rs (trait + Lang-keyed dispatch table) · postings.rs (accumulate SymbolEntry → posting records; trigram-index names) · shard_section.rs (encode/decode symbol-table shard section) · lang/{go,rust,python,c,typescript,java,ruby,fallback}.rs · tests.rs. Core trait: trait SymbolExtractor { fn extract(&self, path: &Path, bytes: &[u8], doc_id: DocId) -> Vec<SymbolEntry>; }.

Per-language extraction strategy:

Lang Definition Triggers Excluded
Go ^func , ^type , ^var , ^const at col 0 imports, comments
Rust ^pub fn, ^fn, ^struct, ^enum, ^trait, ^impl, ^const, ^type use, mod imports
Python ^def , ^class , ^async def decorated lines: capture name from next non-decorator line
C/C++ ^[A-Za-z_].*\( (heuristic prototype) + typedef struct #define, extern decls
TypeScript function , class , interface , const .* = (, export prefix type annotations only
Java `public protected
Ruby ^def , ^class , ^module require lines
Fallback def , func , class anywhere high false-positive; tag kind=Heuristic

Posting accumulation (in csearch-index builder): after the trigram pass, dispatch_extract(lang, path, bytes, doc_id) -> Vec<SymbolEntry> pushes into SymbolAccumulator { entries: Vec<(name_bytes, kind, DocId, line)> }; at shard finalization, sort by name_bytes ascending and write the symbol section after the doc-meta table.

On-shard symbol section binary layout:

[ u32: n_symbols ][ per symbol: u8 name_len, [u8;name_len] name (UTF-8),
  u8 kind (0=Func 1=Class 2=Struct 3=Trait 4=Const 5=Type 6=Module 7=Heuristic), u32 doc_id, u32 line (1-based) ]

Symbol name trigram index (prefix/substring search): overlapping 3-byte windows per name (same algorithm as csearch-trigram) → Set<Trigram>, emitted into a secondary symbol_trigram_posting section as sorted Vec<(Trigram, Vec<symbol_idx>)>, varint-delta encoded, binary-searchable by Trigram.

sym: query executor path in csearch-search:

  1. Parse sym:Name → Filter::Sym(name_str).
  2. name_str.len() >= 3: trigrams → symbol_trigram_posting lookup → candidate symbol_idx list; else linear scan of the symbol name section.
  3. Filter by exact/prefix match per query syntax; collect DocId set from matched SymbolEntry records.
  4. Intersect with any file:/lang:/repo: filter set from QueryAst.
  5. Per matching DocId: PikeVm-verify if a text pattern is also present; else emit MatchSpan { line: symbol.line, col: 0, len: name.len() }.
  6. Tag SearchResult.is_symbol_def = true; csearch-rank applies symbol_def_bonus.

csearch-shard reader additions: symbol_lookup_exact(name: &[u8]) -> Vec<SymbolEntry>, symbol_lookup_prefix(prefix: &[u8]) -> Vec<SymbolEntry>, symbol_lookup_trigram(tq: &TrigramQuery) -> Vec<SymbolEntry> — all zero-allocation iterators over the mmap'd symbol section; names decoded on demand.

10.3 Deliverable

  • csearch-symbol crate compiles; extracts definitions from Go/Rust/Python/C/TypeScript source fixtures.
  • csi index populates symbol section visible via csi stat --sym.
  • csi search sym:MyFunc returns definition spans with is_symbol_def=true; definitions appear before plain occurrences.
  • csearch-shard reader decodes symbol table without allocating beyond result Vec<SymbolEntry>.
  • sym:/regex/ variant lowers regex to TrigramQuery over symbol name bytes; falls back to full scan when query degenerates to TrigramQuery::All.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Symbol extraction recall on 7-language corpus >= 90% of known definitions found
False extraction rate (non-definition lines) < 5%
sym: query latency (10K-doc shard, exact name) < 5 ms p99
sym: with trigram index (substring match) < 15 ms p99
Definition ranked above same-file plain occurrence 100% on fixture suite
symbol_lookup hot path: no heap alloc on miss verified with allocator test harness
symbol section CRC matches header field 100% of freshly written shards
Name roundtrip: extract → write → decode → compare 100% on all fixture files

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
fixtures/sym/go_funcs.go Go func/method/type extraction SymbolEntry list: name + line exact-match grep
fixtures/sym/rust_impls.rs Rust fn/struct/trait extraction no false positives on use statements
fixtures/sym/python_classes.py class + def (including async def) nested methods included; decorators not extracted
fixtures/sym/c_header.h C function declarations vs. definitions declarations tagged kind=Heuristic; definitions kind=Func
fixtures/sym/typescript_exports.ts export function/class/const anonymous arrow const skipped
fixtures/sym/minified.js Minified file (filtered upstream) zero symbols extracted
fixtures/sym/empty.rs Empty file zero symbols, no panic
fixtures/sym/unicode_names.rs Unicode identifiers (e.g. fn héllo) name bytes round-trip without truncation
fixtures/sym/generated.pb.go Dense generated Go code capped at 65535 symbols; symbol_cap_hit metric
fixtures/sym/short_name.py def f(): (1-char name) symbol present; sym:f triggers linear scan

10.6 Pitfalls

  • Regex over-matching in string literals and comments — pattern fires inside "func foo" string content. Mitigation: skip any line where the matched identifier offset falls between paired quote characters; log symbol_in_string_skipped counter per language per run. Debugging: compare extraction vs. grep -n '^func ' file.go.
  • Macro-generated names (Rust derive, Go go:generate) — names exist at source line but represent different types at runtime. Tag as kind=Generated; do not suppress (they are still definitions); lower rank weight by 0.10. Detection: line contains #[derive or //go:generate.
  • Line-offset drift after byte → line conversion — must count \n bytes in original slice, not re-read file from disk. One off-by-one maps to wrong line in jump-to-def result. Validate: fixture asserts SymbolEntry.line == grep -n 'pattern' file | head -1 | cut -d: -f1.
  • Symbol table section exceeds u32 capacity — generated code (.pb.go, _generated.go) can produce hundreds of thousands of definitions per file. Cap at 65535 symbols per shard; log symbol_cap_hit metric; include first-N-by-line-order.
  • sym:/regex/ query degenerates to TrigramQuery::All — pattern . or .* yields no useful trigrams; triggers full symbol-section linear scan. Add short-circuit: if candidate count after linear scan > 10000 and no lang:/file: filter co-present, return error with message "sym: pattern too broad; add a filter".
  • Name collision across languages — init appears in Go, Rust, Python; new in Java, Rust, TypeScript. When lang: filter co-present, intersect DocId candidates with language filter before emitting results. Without lang:, all are returned ranked by path_importance.
  • Symbol section absent from older shards — shard written before Phase 10 has symbol_table_off = 0; reader returns empty on symbol_lookup_*; sym: queries degrade gracefully with sym_section_absent=true metric.

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Symbol extraction is a second pass over file bytes already read for trigram extraction; reuse the same &[u8] slice from the ingest pipeline — do not re-open the file.
  • Builder SymbolAccumulator uses a flat Vec<(name_hash, SymbolEntry)> with a single sort at finalization; avoids per-entry BTreeMap allocation.
  • Symbol section decode: length-prefixed layout allows O(1) skip to the next entry (avoid String::from_utf8_lossy per entry; decode names on demand only when a filter requires comparison); trigram index per name via BTreeSet is O(k log k), k=name_len-2, ≤62 for typical ≤64-byte names.
  • symbol_lookup_exact: binary search over sorted name section → O(log n_symbols); no additional allocation.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • File deleted between trigram extraction and symbol extraction pass: extract() receives bytes from the ingest buffer; no second file read; no error possible from file deletion.
  • Symbol name = empty string from extractor bug: assert !name.is_empty() in SymbolAccumulator::push; drop entry silently; increment symbol_empty_name_dropped counter.
  • Two symbols with identical (name, line) in same file (generated duplication): dedup by (name_bytes, line) before writing; keep first occurrence.
  • sym: query with 1–2 character name: no trigrams available; linear scan of symbol name section; emit sym_linear_scan=true metric; bounded by n_symbols * avg_name_len bytes scanned.
  • Symbol section write interleaved with concurrent shard reads: shards are immutable once written, symbol section appended before rename(2) makes the shard visible — no concurrent read of an incomplete section is possible; deep Unicode names (CJK, 4-byte codepoints) are capped by name_len: u8 at 255 bytes and truncated to the nearest valid codepoint boundary.

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Extractor panic (malformed file) catch_unwind per file; symbol_extractor_panic counter single doc loses symbols; index build continues skip doc symbols; full text search still works
Symbol section CRC mismatch on open reader returns Err(CorruptSymbolSection); log shard path sym: queries on that shard return 0 results rebuild shard; full text search unaffected
sym: returns 0 results on known symbol sym_candidates_from_trigram=0 metric; csi stat --sym Name shows 0 entries user-visible quality degradation csi doctor checks symbol section; trigger re-index
Symbol DocId resolves to wrong path differential test: sym:F DocId path != grep result wrong jump-to-def add roundtrip fixture; re-index
symbol_cap_hit on large generated file metric counter > 0 definitions beyond cap missing from sym: raise cap; or filter generated files at ingest

10.10 Operational Notes

  • Per-query metrics: sym_candidates, sym_defs_found, sym_scan_type (trigram|exact|linear), sym_section_absent. Build-time metrics: symbol_extraction_ms per file; symbol_cap_hit per shard; symbol_empty_name_dropped global.
  • csi stat --sym <Name> prints symbol table entries (shard, DocId, line, kind, path); csi stat --sym-section <shard> prints n_symbols, section byte range, CRC match.
  • csi doctor validates symbol section length vs. symbol_table_off, CRC match, and no DocId out of range.
  • Symbol section version byte in shard header; reader rejects unknown version → shard served for text queries only; sym_section_version_skip counter.
  • Log symbol_extraction_ms > 500 as WARNING with file path (runaway regex on pathological input); cap extractor CPU with a per-file timeout (200 ms) via a thread-local alarm.

Phase 11 — Ranking

11.1 Objectives

  • Implement csearch-rank crate: compute RankScore(f32) per SearchResult from four components — match_density, path_importance, symbol_def_bonus, repo_weight.
  • Top-k selection with a bounded BinaryHeap<OrdByScore>; never materializes more than k entries.
  • Deterministic tie-break: total order by (score desc, path_bytes asc, line asc).
  • Integrate into csearch-search executor: called after PikeVm verification, before result serialization.
  • Constraint: no floating-point non-determinism — accumulate in fixed operand order; NaN must never appear in score.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P9 (Robertson BM25 — match_density analog to TF saturation), R4 (zoekt ranking.go for path/symbol bonus design), I10 (determinism invariant), ADR-17 (metrics for score observability), §8.13 (ranking model), S9 (IEEE 754 float properties, NaN guard) -->

Crate structure csearch-rank/src/: lib.rs (re-exports RankScore, scorer, topk, ctx) · scorer.rs (score() entry, calls component fns) · components.rs (match_density, path_importance, symbol_def_bonus, repo_weight) · topk.rs (TopKCollector, OrdByScore) · ctx.rs (RankCtx: repo weight table, query terms) · tiebreak.rs (OrdByScore total-ordering wrapper).

Score formula (exact accumulation order): score = w_density·match_density(spans,doc) + w_path·path_importance(doc.path) + w_sym·symbol_def_bonus(result) + w_repo·repo_weight(doc.repo,ctx). Default weights:

Weight Value Rationale
w_density 0.50 frequency of match in file
w_path 0.25 file structural importance
w_sym 0.35 definition vs. occurrence
w_repo 0.15 operator-configured repo priority

Scores intentionally may sum > 1.0 (additive, not normalized internally); display layer may normalize.

match_density: clamp(spans.len() as f32 / doc.line_count.max(1) as f32, 0.0, 1.0); dedup (line, col) pairs in spans before counting (handles overlapping PikeVm captures).

path_importance (evaluated in order; first match wins):

Path pattern (byte scan via memchr) Multiplier
path depth ≤ 2 components 1.00
contains vendor/ or third_party/ or extern/ 0.40
contains .pb.go or _gen. or generated/ 0.30
contains test/ or _test or spec/ or testdata/ 0.60
contains examples/ or demo/ 0.70
default 0.80

memchr::memmem::find for substring checks; no compiled regex on the hot path. symbol_def_bonus: if result.is_symbol_def {1.0} else {0.0}, applied once per SearchResult (not per MatchSpan). repo_weight: ctx.repo_weights.get(doc.repo).copied().unwrap_or(1.0) — default 1.0 for unknown repos; 0.0 is a valid operator-suppressed value.

TopKCollector: struct TopKCollector { k: usize, heap: BinaryHeap<OrdByScore> }; push — below k, push; else replace the heap-min if the new score is higher; into_sorted_vec(self) -> Vec<SearchResult> heap-sorts descending.

OrdByScore total order: score DESC (NaN guarded to −inf) → path bytes ASC (lexicographic) → line ASC; Ord/PartialOrd derived from this explicit chain, never f32::partial_cmp.

Integration (csearch-search/src/executor.rs): TopKCollector(k=cfg.result_cap) instantiated per query; after each PikeVm verify, ranker::score(doc, spans, ctx) wraps into a SearchResult and collector.push(result); on completion collector.into_sorted_vec() yields the final order; RankCtx is built once per query from cfg.repo_weights + query trigrams.

--explain (csearch-cli): serializes per-result { density_raw, density_score, path_mult, sym_bonus, repo_weight, total } as JSON when the flag is present.

11.3 Deliverable

  • csearch-rank compiles standalone; unit-tested in isolation without shard dependency.
  • csi search "query" returns results ordered score-desc; definitions appear above plain occurrences in all fixture cases.
  • --explain flag prints score breakdown per result.
  • TopKCollector never holds more than k entries; memory ceiling O(k).
  • Result ordering is byte-for-byte identical across repeated runs on the same index.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Symbol def ranked above same-file occurrence 100% on fixture suite
vendor/ file always below non-vendor (equal density) 100%
*_test.go always below main source (equal density) 100%
Determinism: two runs of same query byte-identical result list
TopKCollector heap size at any point <= k
All scores are finite floats 100%; assert score.is_finite() per result
Tie-break: no equal-rank pairs survive into final list property test over 10K synthetic results
Score values in range [0.0, 2.05] property test with max-weight inputs

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected Behavior
fixtures/rank/def_vs_ref.jsonl is_symbol_def=true result at rank 1
fixtures/rank/vendor_penalty.jsonl vendor/ file always below non-vendor with equal span count
fixtures/rank/test_penalty.jsonl _test.go always below main.go with equal span count
fixtures/rank/tiebreak_stable.jsonl Equal scores: lower path-byte-sort first, lower line first
fixtures/rank/multi_span.jsonl Higher span-count file scores higher when line_count equal
fixtures/rank/empty_spans.jsonl No spans: score = path_importance * w_path (density=0)
fixtures/rank/repo_weight_zero.jsonl Results from repo_weight=0.0 repo appear last
fixtures/rank/generated_penalty.jsonl .pb.go file multiplier=0.30; test verifies rank below normal file

11.6 Pitfalls

  • Floating-point non-determinism across platforms — f32 accumulation in different instruction order may differ by ULP. Fix: always accumulate the four components in the same named-constant order in scorer.rs; document that scores are not portable across architectures.
  • NaN from 0.0 * f32::NAN — if any component function returns NaN (pathological input), the entire score becomes NaN and breaks OrdByScore. Guard: assert!(score.is_finite()) in debug; in release: replace NaN with 0.0, log rank_score_nan counter.
  • Depth miscalculation on non-Unix paths — Path::components().count() may count drive letters on Windows paths; normalize path separators to / at ingest time (done in csearch-ingest); ranking depends on this normalization being applied before path_importance.
  • Heap over-fill with equal scores — if OrdByScore is not a total order (e.g., partial NaN comparison), BinaryHeap may allow more than k entries through equal-push logic. Ensure OrdByScore::cmp is strictly total (never returns Equal for distinct results).
  • symbol_def_bonus double-counted — if csearch-symbol tags a span AND the executor separately detects a definition pattern, bonus could be applied twice. Fix: bonus is read solely from SearchResult.is_symbol_def (one boolean per result), set exclusively by the symbol search path in csearch-search.
  • repo_weight map not populated at query time — new repository not yet in RankCtx.repo_weights defaults to 1.0 via unwrap_or; this is correct behavior; log repo_weight_default_used counter for tuning visibility.

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • TopKCollector::push is O(log k), k=50 default, negligible per-candidate cost; avoid cloning DocMeta per push — store Arc<DocMeta> in SearchResult (clone is a refcount bump).
  • path_importance: one memmem::find call per pattern, evaluated in order, short-circuit on first match; never construct a Regex at query time.
  • Score computation runs per-candidate after PikeVm verification; PikeVm is the dominant cost — ranking adds < 5% overhead.
  • Per-shard fan-out: each shard executor scores independently into a shard-local TopKCollector(k); global merge collects ≤k results per shard into a global TopKCollector(k) at O(n_shards · k · log k), negligible.
  • RankCtx construction: HashMap lookup is O(1) amortized; build once per query, never per candidate.

11.8 Edge Cases

  • doc.line_count = 0 (empty file verified by PikeVm): density = 0.0 (denominator clamped to 1); no divide-by-zero.
  • spans contains duplicate (line, col) entries from overlapping regex captures: dedup before density computation; use BTreeSet<(u32,u32)> locally.
  • Path length > 4096 bytes (adversarial symlink target): path_importance memchr scan terminates at path length; no stack overflow.
  • repo_weight = 0.0: multiplies component to 0.0; valid; result may still appear in top-k if other components are high.
  • All candidates in vendor/: results returned with path_mult=0.40; never suppressed entirely; top-k includes best-scoring vendor results.
  • k = 1: TopKCollector keeps exactly one entry; valid; --explain output has one entry.
  • Query with no verified candidates: into_sorted_vec() returns empty Vec; no panic.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
NaN score (bug in component fn) rank_score_nan counter > 0; --explain shows NaN one result mis-ranked or missing fix component; add regression test
OrdByScore non-total order (partial NaN) sort_unstable_by panics in debug mode server 500 on affected query NaN-guard upstream; enable NaN-assert in component
repo_weight map empty (config not loaded) all repo_weights default to 1.0; repo_weight_default_used counter ranking quality degraded (repo preference lost) populate config; SIGHUP reloads weights
TopKCollector exceeds k (total-order bug) assert!(heap.len() <= k) in test; rank_heap_overflow counter memory growth per query fix OrdByScore total order
Determinism violation (cache warm vs. cold) regression test: two runs diff output user confusion audit for any map-iteration-order dependency in scorer

11.10 Operational Notes

  • Per-query metrics: rank_candidates_scored, rank_top_k_returned, rank_score_max, rank_score_min, rank_score_nan (should be 0), rank_heap_overflow (should be 0). --explain on csi search serializes the score breakdown as a JSON array, one object per result.
  • Repo weight table loaded from [ranking] in index.toml ({ repo_id: weight }); hot-reload via SIGHUP. csi stat --rank-weights prints the current RankCtx table and defaults.
  • Log WARNING when rank_score_nan > 0 in a query, with query string and DocId in the structured log.
  • Score formula and weight defaults are documented in csearch-rank/README.md (operator-facing only, not blueprint source).

Phase 12 — Incremental Indexing + Shard Merge

12.1 Objectives

  • Implement csearch-incremental: detect changed/deleted files per repository using sha256 + mtime cache; re-index only affected files; rebuild changed repos into new shards without touching unchanged repos' shard files.
  • Mark old DocIds as superseded in the manifest (no results until compacted, I9); merge small shards (LSM-style tiered compaction) when shard count exceeds max_shards.
  • Swap the active manifest atomically via rename(2) (I7) so no search sees a partial shard set; enforce I3 (immutability), I5 (content addressing), I7 (manifest atomicity), I9 (deletion via supersession).
  • Constraint: a single file change must not force a global rebuild; incremental latency target < 500 ms for 1 changed file in a 10K-file repo.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: B8 Ch.7 (LSM compaction, tiered vs leveled), I3 (shard immutability), I5 (sha256 change detection), I7 (manifest atomic swap), I9 (deletion via supersession), S4 (POSIX rename(2) atomicity), B3 Ch.3 (concurrent object semantics for ArcSwap), ADR-27 (reproducible builds, determinism across incremental runs) -->

Crate structure csearch-incremental/src/: lib.rs (re-exports) · change_detect.rs (ChangeSet: added/modified/deleted via FileStateCache diff) · file_state.rs (FileStateCache: path -> (mtime_ns, sha256), bincode on disk) · rebuild.rs (rebuild_repo(): ingest+trigram+symbol+shard write for a ChangeSet) · manifest.rs (Manifest, atomic write, ShardEntry, superseded set) · deletion.rs (apply_deletions(): populate superseded BTreeSet from deleted paths) · merge.rs (LSM compaction: tier select + stream-merge posting lists) · compaction_policy.rs (CompactionPolicy: max_shards, size_ratio, tier selection) · gc.rs (deferred shard-file deletion after Arc<ShardSet> refcount drops) · tests.rs.

FileStateCache: key = path (bytes, repo-root-relative); value = (mtime_ns: u64, sha256: [u8;32]); storage = bincode-encoded sorted Vec<(path, mtime_ns, sha256)>, length-prefixed, CRC32 trailer. Fast path: mtime_ns + size unchanged → assume content unchanged (skip sha256 read); slow path: recompute sha256 and compare — a match means no re-index (I5).

ChangeSet: struct ChangeSet { added: Vec<PathBuf>, modified: Vec<PathBuf>, deleted: Vec<PathBuf> }; fn detect(repo_root: &Path, cache: &FileStateCache) -> (ChangeSet, FileStateCache) walks via walkdir (skipping .git, respecting .gitignore), applying the mtime+size fast path per file.

rebuild_repo() pipeline: filter added+modified through csearch-ingest (binary/oversized skip) → run csearch-trigram::extract_trigrams() + csearch-symbol::dispatch_extract() per file → accumulate into csearch-index::ShardBuilder with new contiguous DocIds from 0 → finalize + write to index/shards/shard-{repo_id}-{ts}.bin.tmp → fsync + rename tmp→final → return ShardHandle { shard_id, path, n_docs, byte_size }.

Manifest: struct Manifest { version: u32, shards: Vec<ShardEntry>, superseded: BTreeSet<(ShardId, DocId)> }; struct ShardEntry { shard_id: ShardId, repo_id: RepoId, path: PathBuf, n_docs: u32, byte_size: u64, created_at_ns: u64 }. Serialized as JSON (operator-readable); atomic write: serialize → tmp/manifest.json.{pid}.tmp → fsync → rename(tmp, index/manifest.json) (POSIX atomic) → notify ShardRegistry watcher.

apply_deletions(): for each deleted path, look up (ShardId, DocId) via the FileStateCache path→shard mapping (ShardEntry.doc_path_index) and insert into Manifest.superseded; shard bytes are untouched (I3 preserved).

is_superseded() (in the csearch-shard reader): checked by the csearch-search executor for every candidate DocId before emitting a result; hot-path optimization — if Manifest.superseded.is_empty() (the common case), skip the check via a cached has_any_superseded: bool flag on ShardSet.

LSM-style compaction (merge.rs): select tier = shards with byte_size < tier_threshold (default median(shard_sizes)/size_ratio); for the selected shards, open all mmap readers → merge-sort heap keyed by (Trigram, DocId) skipping superseded DocIds → assign new contiguous DocIds in the merge output → re-encode posting lists (delta+varint) into a new shard → copy DocMeta+symbol sections (superseded filtered) → write, fsync, rename tmp→final → update Manifest (remove source entries + their superseded rows, add the new shard) → atomic swap → schedule old shard files for GC once ShardSet refcount drops.

CompactionPolicy: max_shards=8 (compaction trigger), size_ratio=10.0 (tier boundary), min_merge_size=2 (minimum shards per merge batch).

ShardRegistry hot-reload (csearch-server): inotify (Linux)/kqueue (macOS) watches index/manifest.json; on a write event, re-reads the manifest, builds a new Arc<ShardSet>, and atomically swaps it into the registry via ArcSwap (arc-swap crate; no mutex on the read path); in-flight queries hold a cloned Arc<ShardSet> snapshot, so old shards stay mmap-opened until the last Arc drops.

GC (gc.rs): a PendingGc { shard_path, weak: Weak<ShardSet> } list; a background task checks Weak::upgrade() and deletes the file once it returns None; runs after every manifest swap, logging gc_shard_deleted per file removed.

csi index --incremental CLI flow: load FileStateCache from index/filecache.bin → detect(repo_root, cache) → (ChangeSet, new_cache) (empty set exits 0 with incremental_no_changes) → apply_deletions(manifest, change_set.deleted) → rebuild_repo(repo_id, added+modified, old_manifest) → ShardHandle → add as a new ShardEntry → atomic manifest swap → write new_cache to index/filecache.bin → if manifest.shards.len() > policy.max_shards, run compaction.

Path→shard mapping: ShardEntry carries an auxiliary doc_path_index: BTreeMap<PathBuf, DocId> serialized inside the shard's doc-meta table (not the manifest JSON, to keep it small); loaded lazily by deletion.rs only when a deletion is detected.

12.3 Deliverable

  • csi index --incremental <repo> modifies only changed-repo shards; unchanged repos' shard bytes are identical (verified by sha256sum).
  • csi search never returns results from superseded DocIds; confirmed by integration test.
  • Shard count bounded by max_shards after compaction; verified by csi stat.
  • Server hot-reloads new manifest within 100 ms of rename(2) on the manifest file.
  • csi stat prints: shard list, per-shard doc count, superseded doc count, file-state cache hit rate, last compaction timestamp.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Incremental index time (1 file changed, 10K-file repo) < 500 ms wall time
Superseded doc never returned in search 100% on deletion fixture suite
Shard count post-compaction <= max_shards (default 8)
Manifest atomic: crash during rename → consistent state fault-injection test: old manifest intact
Server manifest hot-reload latency < 100 ms p99
Unchanged files: cache hit rate (mtime fast path) 100% on no-change fixture
Compaction: merged shard contains no superseded docs 100% on compaction fixture
FileStateCache decode error: fallback to full re-scan integration test with corrupted cache file

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
fixtures/incr/add_file/ Add one file to repo new DocId present; old shard bytes identical
fixtures/incr/modify_file/ Modify file content old content absent (superseded); new content present
fixtures/incr/delete_file/ Delete file from repo DocId in manifest.superseded; no search result
fixtures/incr/rename_file/ Rename = delete + add both events handled; no stale result
fixtures/incr/no_change/ Re-index unchanged repo zero new shards written; filecache_hit_rate = 100%
fixtures/incr/compaction_trigger/ Push shard count to max_shards + 1 merge fires; result count unchanged post-merge
fixtures/incr/crash_during_merge/ SIGKILL mid-merge manifest points to pre-merge shards; no corruption
fixtures/incr/schema_upgrade/ Shard version bump in minor release old shards readable; merged shard at new version
fixtures/incr/cross_device_rename/ tmp/ on separate filesystem detects EXDEV; falls back to copy+unlink; manifest swap succeeds
fixtures/incr/filecache_corrupt/ Truncated filecache.bin CRC mismatch detected; cold-start full rescan; filecache_cold_start metric

12.6 Pitfalls

  • mtime resolution on FAT32/HFS+ — 1-second or 2-second resolution causes missed changes when file is modified within the resolution window. Mitigation: whenever mtime_delta < 2s (configurable), always fall back to sha256 comparison regardless of size match. Log mtime_resolution_fallback counter.
  • rename(2) across filesystems (EXDEV) — if tmp/ is on a different filesystem than index/, rename(2) fails with EXDEV. Detection: first swap attempt returns EXDEV; fallback to std::fs::copy + fsync + std::fs::remove_file(old). Log manifest_cross_device_copy metric. Note: copy+remove is not atomic; document that in this fallback case a crash between copy and remove leaves both files present; recovery: re-run csi index.
  • In-flight queries see deleted shard file post-GC — Arc<ShardSet> reference keeps shard mmap open; GC must not delete a shard file while any Arc<ShardSet> holds it. Use Weak<ShardSet> GC gate; gc_deferred_shard_count metric indicates accumulation.
  • Compaction concurrent with search — merge reads from old shards while search also reads them; no lock required (immutable shards); but new merge must re-read manifest.superseded at compaction start and re-apply to filter deleted docs from the merged output.
  • DocId collision in merged shard — merger assigns new contiguous DocIds from 0; all external references use (ShardId, DocId) pairs which are invalidated after merge. Clients (e.g., jump-to-def stored links) must re-resolve; document as a known limitation.
  • FileStateCache write corruption — partial write during csi crash leaves a truncated filecache.bin. CRC32 trailer detects truncation; reader falls back to cold-start full sha256 rescan; logs filecache_cold_start.
  • Shard GC deletes file still in manifest — only delete shard file if it is absent from the current manifest AND Weak::upgrade() returns None. Two-phase check; enforce in gc.rs with a manifest snapshot taken at deletion time.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • FileStateCache load on startup: bincode decode of 100K entries ~50 ms on warm disk; length-prefixed format keeps decode O(n) sequential; avoid random-access deserialization.
  • Compaction I/O: merging N shards reads all posting lists in (Trigram, DocId) order via a k-way merge-sort heap, each shard accessed sequentially through mmap (kernel read-ahead dominates, no random seeks); posting lists are delta re-encoded from scratch per trigram straight to a streaming PostingWriter — no full in-memory sort of all postings.
  • superseded lookup on search hot path: BTreeSet::contains is O(log n_superseded); skipped entirely via a flag when has_any_superseded = false (the common case after compaction).
  • inotify/kqueue watcher: single OS fd per manifest, no polling loop, rename(2)→notification latency typically < 1 ms on Linux; ArcSwap read path (load()) is lock-free with no read-path contention, writes only on manifest change.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • Repo directory deleted entirely: all docs for that RepoId added to superseded; shard retained until compacted; repo_deleted metric emitted.
  • Symlink cycle in repo walk during rebuild: csearch-ingest tracks visited (device, inode) pairs; cycle detection prevents infinite recursion; log symlink_cycle_skipped per cycle.
  • Two concurrent csi index --incremental invocations: advisory flock on index/manifest.lock; second invocation waits (timeout 30s) or errors with ERR_MANIFEST_LOCKED; never two concurrent atomic swaps.
  • Shard merge produces zero-doc shard (all docs superseded): do not write shard file; remove all source entries from manifest without adding a new entry; GC source files.
  • sha256 collision (negligible probability): treated as identical content → no re-index; log sha256_identity_assumed; no correctness impact.
  • Clock stepping backward (NTP step): mtime for modified file < last recorded mtime; change_detect detects this via mtime_ns < cache_mtime_ns - tolerance; falls back to sha256 comparison; log clock_step_backward_detected.
  • Manifest JSON parse failure at startup: server falls back to previous manifest.json.prev (kept for one generation); log manifest_parse_error; emit alert.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Manifest rename fails mid-swap (ENOSPC, EROFS) rename() Err; manifest_swap_failure counter no index update; serve stale index operator alert; fix disk; retry csi index
Compaction crash: merged shard partially written shard CRC fails on open; reader returns Err merged shard rejected on load; old shards still in manifest re-run csi merge --compact; old shards intact
superseded set grows unbounded (no compaction) superseded_count metric rising; csi stat shows count per-candidate BTreeSet::contains cost grows lower max_shards; run csi merge --compact manually
FileStateCache lost (disk wipe or corruption) cold-start CRC fail; filecache_cold_start metric full re-index on next run (~minutes for large repos) acceptable; log prominently; index still correct
GC removes shard still mmap'd SIGBUS on mmap access; server crash query failure; possible restart enforce Weak::upgrade() GC gate; add integration test
Manifest points to missing shard file shard open returns Err(NotFound); shard_open_error counter that shard excluded from query fan-out; partial results csi doctor detects; trigger re-index for missing shard

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Per-run metrics (csi index --incremental): incremental_files_added, incremental_files_modified, incremental_files_deleted, incremental_build_ms, filecache_hit_rate, shards_written, docs_indexed. Per-compaction: compaction_runs, compaction_shards_merged, compaction_docs_merged, compaction_superseded_dropped, compaction_ms.
  • Per-query metrics (from server): superseded_count (in manifest at query time), shard_count, gc_deferred_shard_count.
  • csi stat output: shard list (id, repo, n_docs, byte_size, created_at), superseded doc count, file-state cache hit rate, last compaction timestamp.
  • csi doctor checks: manifest JSON valid; all listed shard files present and CRC-valid; no orphan shard files on disk (present but absent from manifest); shard version compatibility.
  • Manifest versioning: version field incremented on schema change; reader rejects unknown version with actionable error "re-run csi index to rebuild index" — no silent data loss.
  • Rollback: manifest.json.prev kept for one generation; csi rollback performs rename(manifest.json.prev, manifest.json) + manifest swap signal. Compaction can also run manually via csi merge --compact [--max-shards N], useful after a bulk import creates many shards.
  • SIGHUP on csearch-server: re-reads index.toml (repo weights, compaction policy); does NOT restart; manifest watcher continues uninterrupted.

Phase 13 — Search Server

13.1 Objectives

  • Expose HTTP/JSON API over all search and index-management operations via csearch-server.
  • Support streaming result delivery, per-query deadline enforcement, and result cap.
  • Implement concurrent fan-out across all loaded shards; aggregate ranked results.
  • Hot-reload manifest on atomic shard swap without restarting the server process.
  • Emit structured metrics per query: candidate count, verified count, shards touched, latency stages.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P6 (bounded latency, deadline propagation), P10 (observability by default), I7 (manifest atomic swap, hot-reload), B3 Ch.18 (lock-free registry swap), B9 Ch.7 (mmap under memory pressure, MADV hints), ADR-03 (Tokio fan-out), ADR-11 (axum HTTP server), S3 (HTTP/1.1 chunked streaming) -->

API surface — csearch-server/src/api.rs

Endpoint Method Input Output
/search GET/POST {q, filters, limit, offset, deadline_ms} {results: [SearchResult], stats: QueryStats}
/search/stream GET same NDJSON: one SearchResult per line, flush on each
/index/reload POST {manifest_path} {ok, shard_count}
/health GET — {status, shard_count, uptime_s}
/metrics GET — Prometheus text exposition

Shard registry (csearch-server/src/registry.rs): ShardRegistry = Arc<RwLock<Vec<Arc<csearch_shard::ShardReader>>>>; hot-reload reads the new manifest path, opens a new ShardReader set, takes the write lock, swaps the Vec, and drops old readers (mmap unmaps at zero Arc refcount); reload triggers via inotify/kqueue on the manifest directory or an explicit API call; active queries hold Arc<ShardReader> snapshots, so a reload never invalidates in-flight work.

Fan-out executor (csearch-server/src/fanout.rs): one tokio::task per shard per query runs csearch_search::execute_shard(shard, query, deadline, per_shard_cap); results aggregate via FuturesUnordered into a bounded mpsc channel (capacity = result cap); select! on the channel receiver + deadline timer cancels remaining tasks via CancellationToken once the cap is met or the deadline fires; final merge collects all partial Vec<SearchResult>, sorts by RankScore descending, truncates to limit, applies offset.

Streaming (csearch-server/src/stream.rs): axum Body::from_stream backed by a tokio::sync::mpsc::Receiver<Bytes>; each SearchResult serializes as NDJSON and flushes immediately for incremental delivery; deadline expiry mid-stream emits a terminal {"error":"deadline_exceeded","partial":true} line and closes.

Metrics (csearch-server/src/metrics.rs): prometheus crate registered at process start; counters/histograms query_total, query_duration_seconds (per-percentile buckets), candidates_total, verified_total, shards_touched, false_positive_ratio (verified/candidates); gauges shard_count, total_docs, total_trigrams.

Config (csearch-server/src/config.rs): ServerConfig — bind address, default deadline ms, default result cap, max concurrent queries, shard directory, manifest path, reload watch interval; loaded from TOML + env var overrides, validated at startup (missing shard dir is fatal).

13.3 Deliverable

  • csearch-server binary starts, loads shards from manifest, binds HTTP port.
  • /search returns ranked JSON results; /search/stream delivers NDJSON incrementally.
  • /index/reload swaps shard set live; in-flight queries complete against old set.
  • /metrics returns Prometheus exposition; /health returns live shard count.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Threshold
100 concurrent queries on 10-shard corpus p95 latency < 200 ms
Reload under load: zero dropped queries all in-flight queries complete
Result cap=10, corpus 50k files response time ≤ deadline, exactly ≤10 results
Stream: 100-result query, first result delivered within 50 ms
/metrics after N queries query_total == N, candidates_total >= verified_total
Deadline 10 ms on slow corpus response contains "partial":true or empty results, no hang

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose
fixtures/server/basic_search.json request + expected result set for known corpus
fixtures/server/stream_output.ndjson expected NDJSON lines for streaming query
fixtures/server/reload_sequence/ manifest v1, v2; verify results switch correctly
fixtures/server/deadline_exceeded.json deadline=1ms; expect partial/empty with error field
fixtures/server/metrics_snapshot.txt known Prometheus output after seeded queries
fixtures/server/concurrent_load.sh 50-client wrk/hey script; parse p99 from output

13.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
Arc<ShardReader> leak during reload memory grows monotonically query holds shard arc past completion track Arc::strong_count in metrics
Thundering herd on hot reload latency spike at reload instant all tasks re-acquire RwLock stagger reload behind a Semaphore(1)
NDJSON frame split across TCP packet client parse error no explicit flush after each frame use axum chunked; verify with nc
Deadline fires before any shard returns empty results silently returned CancellationToken races with first result emit partial:true flag unconditionally when deadline fires
Unbounded task spawn on large shard count OOM / thread exhaustion per-shard tasks without concurrency cap semaphore on fan-out width; default=32
Manifest parse error on partial write registry holds stale shards filesystem not atomic write manifest to .tmp, rename atomically

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • RwLock on registry: read path holds lock only to clone Arc<Vec>, then releases; contention is negligible.
  • Fan-out task count = shard count per query; bound with semaphore to avoid tokio scheduler pressure.
  • NDJSON serialization: zero-copy serde_json::to_writer into BytesMut; avoid intermediate String allocation.
  • Result merge across shards: heap-merge by RankScore; O(N log S) where N=results, S=shards.
  • mmap eviction under memory pressure: OS page cache reclaim; mitigate with madvise(MADV_SEQUENTIAL) during fan-out.
  • HTTP keep-alive: reuse connections across queries; axum defaults are correct.
  • tokio worker threads: set to num_cpus; avoid blocking syscalls on async threads (use spawn_blocking for any sync I/O).

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero shards loaded at startup: /health returns degraded; /search returns {"results":[],"stats":{"shards":0}}.
  • Query with all-.* regex (TrigramQuery::All): skip posting intersection, fan all docs directly to verify; enforce result cap early.
  • Concurrent reload + query: query snapshot taken before reload completes sees old shard set; correctness preserved by I7.
  • Client disconnects mid-stream: detect via channel send error; cancel remaining fan-out tasks.
  • Malformed JSON request body: return HTTP 400 with error detail; do not panic.
  • offset beyond total results: return {"results":[]}, not an error.
  • Very large limit (e.g., 100000): clamp to server max (default 1000); log warning.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Shard mmap fails (ENOMEM) ShardReader::open returns Err; logged at ERROR that shard excluded from query reduce ulimit -v; reduce shard count via merge
Tokio runtime panic in fan-out task JoinError caught in aggregator single query returns error; server continues log stack trace; increment query_error_total counter
Manifest file deleted inotify/kqueue event; reload returns Err registry unchanged (stale but valid) operator restores manifest; trigger reload
Port bind failure at startup TcpListener::bind error; process exits 1 server never starts check ss -tlnp; free port or change config
Runaway query (no deadline set) query runs until OOM or result cap single goroutine monopolizes shard enforce mandatory server-side deadline ceiling (e.g., 30 s)

13.10 Operational Notes

  • Structured JSON logging via tracing-subscriber; log level from RUST_LOG env.
  • Per-query trace span: query_id (UUID), q_text, shard_count, candidate_count, verified_count, duration_ms.
  • Rolling log files: delegate to tracing-appender or external log rotation (logrotate).
  • Deployment: single binary; shard directory on fast local NVMe or tmpfs for hot data.
  • Rollback: restore previous manifest path; trigger /index/reload; old shards still on disk.
  • Graceful shutdown: drain in-flight queries (up to 5 s), then exit; signal handler on SIGTERM.
  • Config hot-reload for non-structural fields (log level, result cap) via SIGHUP + re-read config file.

Phase 14 — Hardening + Correctness Conformance + Perf at Scale + Packing

14.1 Objectives

  • Achieve differential correctness against grep -rE on a real corpus (zero false negatives, bounded false positives).
  • Fuzz-prove I1 (trigram-query soundness): for random regex, candidate set is a strict superset of true matches.
  • Fuzz shard reader for memory safety under malformed bytes.
  • Establish latency and throughput benchmarks at scale (10M+ files, multi-shard).
  • Finalize on-disk varint+delta packing; verify pack/unpack roundtrip correctness and size regression tests.

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: I1 (soundness obligation, fuzz verification), P2 (no false negatives), ADR-19 (cargo-fuzz libFuzzer), ADR-21 (grep differential), P5 (Lemire varint — performance benchmark baseline), P15 (KMP baseline for literal correctness), P1 (Cox 2012 soundness proof validated by fuzz) -->

Differential test harness (csearch-harness/src/diff_grep.rs): corpus = real OSS repos (Linux kernel, CPython, LLVM; ~50 GB, script-seeded); per sampled query, run grep -rP (PCRE) on raw files and csearch search, compare match sets by (file_path, line, col, text) — any grep match absent from csearch output is a false negative (I1+I6 violation, test failure); false positives (extra csearch lines) are recorded as a metric, not a failure, when bounded. Runs as a CI job on a reduced 1 GB sample, full 50 GB nightly; comparison normalizes line endings, strips BOM, sorts both sets before diffing.

Trigram-query soundness fuzzer (csearch-harness/src/soundness_fuzz.rs): cargo-fuzz/libFuzzer target fuzz_soundness — arbitrary bytes parsed as a regex string (skip on parse failure), lowered to TrigramQuery via csearch_trigquery::lower, evaluated against 100 random documents (0–4096 random bytes each); asserts regex-matches-doc ⟹ TrigramQuery satisfied by the doc's trigrams (soundness). Corpus seeds: alternation-heavy regexes, .{0,5}, anchored patterns, multi-byte UTF-8.

Shard-reader fuzzer (csearch-harness/src/shard_fuzz.rs): target fuzz_shard_reader — arbitrary bytes as a shard file through ShardReader::open → posting_list(trigram) → PostingList::iter().collect(); must never panic, access out-of-bounds, or infinite-loop (UBSAN+ASAN enabled); crash corpus saved to fuzz/artifacts/shard_fuzz/.

Latency benchmark suite (csearch-bench/src/latency.rs): criterion benchmarks — index build time, single-shard query, multi-shard fan-out, regex vs. literal — at 10k/100k/1M-file corpus sizes, measuring wall time + allocated bytes; regression gate: < 5% slowdown vs. the baseline commit on any benchmark.

Benchmark Target
Index build 100k files (10 MB avg) < 60 s on 8-core
Single literal search, 1M-file index < 20 ms p99
Regex search .*(foo|bar).* 1M files < 80 ms p99
Fan-out 16 shards, regex < 150 ms p99
Shard open + mmap (cold) < 100 ms per shard

Pack/unpack correctness (csearch-core/tests/pack_roundtrip.rs): property test (proptest) — arbitrary sorted, dedup Vec<DocId> → PostingWriter encode → PostingReader decode → assert bit-for-bit equal; varint boundaries at DocIds 0, 127, 128, 16383, 16384, u32::MAX; delta overflow (consecutive DocIds with delta > u32::MAX/2) must not occur (enforced by sort invariant I2); size regression: 1M DocIds encode to < 4 MB (~4 bytes/delta average).

Correctness conformance integration (csearch-harness/src/conformance.rs): loads the canonical fixture corpus (fixtures/conformance/, sha256-pinned, immutable), runs every query in queries.jsonl against expected.jsonl — any deviation fails CI. Covers literal match, regex match, file:/lang:/sym: filters, case-insensitive, empty result.

14.3 Deliverable

  • cargo fuzz run soundness_fuzz runs 1M iterations without a soundness violation.
  • cargo fuzz run shard_fuzz runs 10M iterations without a memory-safety issue.
  • Differential grep test passes on 1 GB corpus sample with zero false negatives.
  • All criterion benchmarks pass regression gate.
  • Pack/unpack property tests pass across all varint boundary cases.
  • Conformance suite (all fixture queries) passes with exact match.

14.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Pass condition
False-negative rate vs grep 0 (zero tolerated)
False-positive rate vs grep < 5% of result count
Soundness fuzz: 1M iters no assertion failure
Shard fuzz: 10M iters, ASAN no crash, no OOM
Literal search p99 / 1M files < 20 ms
Regex search p99 / 1M files < 80 ms
Index build / 100k files < 60 s
Pack roundtrip property test 10k random inputs, 0 failures
Conformance fixture suite 100% queries match expected

14.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose
fixtures/conformance/corpus/ sha256-pinned OSS file set (~500 files, known content)
fixtures/conformance/queries.jsonl 200 queries: literals, regexes, filters, edge cases
fixtures/conformance/expected.jsonl pre-computed ground-truth results per query
fixtures/soundness/seed_regexes.txt 500 hand-crafted regexes targeting lowering edge cases
fixtures/pack/boundary_cases.bin encoded posting lists at varint boundaries
fuzz/corpus/soundness_fuzz/ libFuzzer seed corpus for soundness target
fuzz/corpus/shard_fuzz/ libFuzzer seed corpus for shard reader target
fixtures/bench/corpus_10k/ stable 10k-file bench corpus (sha256-pinned)

14.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug
Grep vs csearch encoding mismatch false negatives in diff test grep operates on UTF-8 decoded; csearch on raw bytes normalize: index raw bytes; run grep with LC_ALL=C
