Scalability Analysis

Dominant Scaling Pressures

Pressure Source Impact
Trigram table size Unique trigrams × posting-list entries; grows O(docs × avg_unique_trigrams_per_doc) Memory and I/O at index build; L3 spill at query time
Candidate set size High-frequency trigrams (e.g. =, the) generate large posting lists Gallop intersection cost; verify throughput
Shard count Incremental indexing accumulates shards until compaction Fan-out latency grows linearly with shard count; compaction is the bound
Doc-meta table Linear in total doc count across all shards DocMeta random-access cost during verify phase
Symbol index Grows with definition count; ctags-style single-pass extraction Large codebases: sym: queries scan larger posting lists

Bottleneck Map

Small corpus (< 100k docs):
  → CPU-bound: regex verification of candidates (PikeVM per candidate)
  → Optimize: minimize candidate set via tighter TrigramQuery (Phase 6)

Medium corpus (100k – 1M docs):
  → I/O-bound at index build (trigram accumulation + sort + write)
  → Memory-bound: posting lists exceed L3; mmap page faults dominate search
  → Optimize: sorted posting lists for cache locality; madvise hints

Large corpus (> 1M docs, multi-shard):
  → Fan-out latency: O(n_shards) concurrent tasks; bounded by shard count
  → Compaction pressure: LSM merge must keep shard count ≤ O(log n_repos)
  → Network: server streaming results under high concurrency; HTTP framing overhead

Horizontal Scaling

  • Read replicas: shards are immutable files; replicate shard directory to N read nodes; no coordination during search.
  • Partitioned index: partition repos into shard groups by RepoId hash; route query to all partitions; merge result sets at server layer.
  • Fan-out coordinator: csearch-server acts as fan-out node; stateless; scale horizontally behind load balancer.
  • Shard distribution: manifest file replicated; each node loads only its assigned shard subset; manifest swap is per-node atomic (I7).

Vertical Scaling

Resource Scaling effect
RAM More mmap resident pages → fewer page faults → lower P99; saturates at total shard size
CPU cores Index build: parallel trigram extraction + parallel shard writes scale to ~8 cores before I/O bound
NVMe IOPS Index build throughput scales with write IOPS up to CPU limit; mmap random read benefits from high random-read IOPS
Core count (search) Fan-out tasks are independent; scales linearly to n_shards concurrent tasks

Shard Count Scaling (Phase 12)

  • Without compaction: shard count = total incremental rebuilds → linear growth → fan-out degrades linearly.
  • With LSM compaction (Phase 12): shard count bounded at O(log₂(total_docs)); e.g., 10M docs → ≤ 24 shards.
  • Compaction trigger: shard count exceeds threshold (default: 16) or smallest shard < 1/10 largest shard size.
  • Compaction cost: O(total_posting_entries_in_merged_shards); runs offline; atomic manifest swap; no query downtime.

Posting-List Intersection Scaling

  • Galloping intersection (I2) cost: O(n × log(m/n)) where n = smaller list, m = larger list.
  • Degenerate case: .* → TrigramQuery::All → no posting-list filter; every doc is a candidate; verify cost = O(n_docs × doc_size); mitigated by result cap (P6) and deadline.
  • High-frequency trigram saturation: trigrams with posting-list length > 1% of corpus (e.g., common C keywords) treated as All in TrigramQuery::And simplification → skip the list, rely on other trigrams.

Query Concurrency Scaling

Connections Shards Expected P99 Notes
10 8 ≤ 80 shards tasks Baseline; fits comfortably
100 16 ≤ 1600 tasks/query burst Tokio task overhead ~1 µs; acceptable
1000 32 Task pool saturation possible Introduce per-query shard-task semaphore to cap concurrency
10 000 32 Connection-accept bottleneck Add HTTP/2 multiplexing; connection pooling

Metadata Scaling

  • DocMeta: 128 bytes/doc × 1 M docs = 128 MB per shard; resident in mmap; random access during verify.
  • At 10 M docs across 10 shards: 1.28 GB DocMeta total; fits in RAM on 16 GB server; beyond that, NVMe random-read bottleneck.
  • Mitigation for > 10 M docs: compress DocMeta (path interning, lang enum); move heavy fields (sha256) to secondary cold store accessed only during deduplication.

Index Size Growth Model

Corpus Estimated shard size Trigram table entries Notes
100k docs × 10 KB avg ~4 GB ~50 M trigram entries Fits on single shard, single server
1 M docs × 10 KB avg ~40 GB ~500 M trigram entries Multi-shard; mmap exceeds RAM; I/O-bound verify
10 M docs × 10 KB avg ~400 GB ~5 B trigram entries Distributed shards required; horizontal fan-out mandatory

Compaction and Merge Overhead

  • Merge reads all posting lists from source shards; re-sorts by Trigram; delta+varint re-encodes into new shard.
  • Cost model: O(total_postings × log(n_shards)) for sort-merge; I/O-bound at large scale.
  • Concurrency: compaction runs in a background thread/process; does not block query serving; shard registry hot-reload on completion (I7).
  • Failure safety: new merged shard written to temp path; CRC-sealed; atomically renamed; old shards tombstoned in manifest; old shard files deleted after all in-flight queries complete (epoch-based reclamation).

Mental Models & First Principles

The following ten models are the load-bearing insights an expert carries when reasoning about this system. Each compresses a design decision into a single testable prediction.

MM1 — The trigram index is a probabilistic filter, not a database. Insight: a shard's posting lists tell you which documents might contain a query string; only the PikeVm verifier tells you which do. Every lookup produces a superset. Prediction: the false-positive rate is the dominant cost lever; reducing it (better lowering, higher corpus selectivity) reduces end-to-end latency more than any constant-factor optimization. Bug class without it: treating the candidate set as the result set, returning unverified MatchSpans (violates I6). Resolving §: P1 (candidate-then-verify), Phase 6, Phase 8.

MM2 — A trigram query is boolean algebra over a 24-bit key space. Insight: TrigramQuery::And is set intersection; Or is union; All is the universal set; Any is the empty set. The algebra is monotone: adding more And terms can only shrink the candidate set; they never introduce false negatives. Prediction: any simplification that removes All terms from an And is safe; any simplification that removes All from an Or produces false negatives. Bug class without it: removing All from an Or node during simplification — the most common lowering soundness bug. Resolving §: Phase 6, §8.7, I1.

MM3 — A Pike VM state set is a time-slice of NFA threads; visited-at-this-byte prevents super-linear blowup. Insight: at each input byte, the VM processes every active thread exactly once because the visited bitset prevents re-adding a state that has already been processed at the current byte position. Total work per byte = |NFA states| regardless of input content. Prediction: any regex that can be compiled by Thompson's algorithm runs in O(|text| × |NFA|) without exception. Bug class without it: forgetting to reset the visited bitset between bytes, turning O(n·m) into O(n·m²) or worse. Resolving §: Phase 5, I4, P3.

MM4 — Immutable shards enable lock-free concurrent reads. Insight: because a shard's bytes are sealed at write time (CRC, O_RDONLY mmap), any number of readers can access a shard concurrently without coordination. The only synchronization point is the manifest swap (a single atomic rename). Prediction: read throughput scales linearly with core count; there are no read-read contention points inside a shard. Bug class without it: in-place mutation of a shard byte while a reader has it mmap'd — produces data races and corrupt MatchSpans. Resolving §: P4, I3, Phase 12 (GC/compaction), Phase 13 (ArcSwap registry).

MM5 — Delta encoding converts a monotone-ascending sequence into a sequence of small positive integers. Insight: DocId lists in posting lists are strictly ascending (I2); their deltas are always ≥ 1 and usually small (nearby DocIds). LEB128 encodes small positive integers in 1–2 bytes. Therefore most posting list entries cost 1–2 bytes on disk and decode in 1–2 operations. Prediction: a corpus with dense DocId assignment (sequential ingestion order) achieves near-optimal compression; randomized DocId assignment would double or triple posting list size. Bug class without it: unsigned-subtraction wrap-around when the decoder doesn't enforce I2, producing corrupt DocIds. Resolving §: Phase 3, §8.3, ADR-13.

MM6 — Galloping intersection derives its advantage from list-length skew, not list content. Insight: galloping is optimal when the shorter list has length n and the longer has length m ≫ n; the exponential probe jumps past large gaps in O(log(m/n)), reducing comparison count from O(m+n) to O(n log(m/n)). When n≈m, galloping degrades to sequential merge and should be replaced. Prediction: rare trigrams (short posting lists) intersected with common trigrams (long lists) are the regime where galloping provides the most improvement — exactly the typical code-search workload where the query contains at least one rare function name. Bug class without it: using naive merge for all intersections, causing 10-100× slowdown on skewed lists. Resolving §: Phase 8, P7, ADR-12.

MM7 — LSM compaction bounds shard count at O(log N) by amortizing merge cost over N writes. Insight: each incremental build produces at most one new shard. A compaction policy that merges the smallest k shards whenever total count exceeds a threshold ensures no shard is merged more than O(log N) times (tiered compaction) or that total shard count stays below log₂(total_docs). Prediction: without compaction, a corpus with daily updates accumulates one shard per day; fan-out latency grows linearly with days of operation. Bug class without it: unbounded shard count → linear fan-out latency → effective DoS of the search server after weeks of operation. Resolving §: Phase 12, Scalability Analysis, P9.

MM8 — mmap hands the OS page cache responsibility; the kernel does the I/O scheduling. Insight: opening a shard with MAP_SHARED | PROT_READ does not read any bytes; it merely establishes the virtual address mapping. Bytes are faulted in on first access, cached by the OS, shared across processes, and evicted under memory pressure. The kernel's page reclaimer is a better I/O scheduler for read-heavy workloads than any userspace read-ahead logic. Prediction: a warm index (all shard pages in page cache) serves queries near memory bandwidth; a cold index (first query after restart) pays one page fault per 4KB of posting list accessed. Bug class without it: unmapping a shard while a reader holds a PostingList<'a> borrow — SIGBUS. Resolving §: P5, §8.4 (shard layout), Phase 13 (MADV_SEQUENTIAL hints).

MM9 — A sound over-approximation is structurally preserved by all monotone AND/OR operations. Insight: if lower(R) is a sound over-approximation of regex R (every true match satisfies the query), then And(lower(R1), lower(R2)) is a sound over-approximation of Concat(R1, R2) and Or(lower(R1), lower(R2)) is a sound over-approximation of Alternation(R1, R2). Soundness is compositional. Prediction: the only place soundness can break is at the leaves — a Literal lowering that drops a trigram or a Repetition lowering that returns a non-All when zero occurrences are possible. Bug class without it: introducing And leaves that are not required (e.g., lowering Repetition(min=0) to its inner literals) — a classic soundness regression. Resolving §: Phase 6, I1, P2.

MM10 — Deterministic DocId assignment + total-order ranking = reproducible results. Insight: DocIds are assigned sequentially per shard in ingestion order; RankScore tie-breaks use (score desc, path_bytes asc, line asc) with f32::total_cmp; fan-out results are re-sorted globally after shard workers complete. If all inputs are identical, the output is byte-identical. Prediction: any non-determinism in search results traces to either (a) random DocId assignment, (b) float NaN in scores, or (c) hash-map iteration in the fan-out pipeline. Bug class without it: unstable sort in TopKCollector::into_sorted_vec where equal-score entries differ between runs. Resolving §: I10, Phase 11, P8.

Comparative Architecture Table

Comparison of this design against five real, production code-search systems on load-bearing architectural dimensions.

Dimension csearch (this project) Google Code Search / Russ Cox codesearch Sourcegraph Zoekt GitHub Code Search "Blackbird" ripgrep (regex-scan baseline) OpenGrok
Index type Trigram inverted index (varint+delta posting lists) Trigram inverted index (same algorithm) 3-gram index with per-doc ngram fingerprint + content shards Undisclosed; reported as suffix-array + inverted hybrid (2023 blog) None — brute-force line-by-line SIMD scan Lucene inverted token index (full-text)
Regex support RE2 subset; Thompson NFA / Pike VM; linear-time guarantee RE2 via Go stdlib (external); trigram filter + exact verify RE2 via Google's re2 library; matchtree filter Undisclosed; likely RE2 Full PCRE via regex crate (backtracking possible on non-safe patterns) Lucene regex via automata; not RE2
Ranking Composite: match_density + path_importance + symbol_def_bonus + repo_weight None (results sorted by path) BM25 + path depth penalty + symbol bonus + repo priority Undisclosed; known to incorporate popularity signals None (results in filesystem order) Lucene BM25
Incremental indexing sha256+mtime change detection; per-repo shard rebuild; LSM compaction Full rebuild on re-index; no incremental support in open-source release Per-shard watch-based rebuild (inotify/kqueue); shard hot-reload Undisclosed; known to index at commit granularity N/A (stateless scan) Incremental via source code repository polling
Symbol / semantic search ctags-style heuristic extraction; sym: query path None None Known to have some symbol awareness; undisclosed implementation None Ctags/Exuberant Ctags integration; cross-reference index
Sharding / scale Single-host multi-shard; LSM-bounded shard count; horizontal read replicas via shared NFS Single-host; one file-per-index; practical scale: tens of GB Per-repo shards; shard-level parallelism; multi-server via Kubernetes Multi-datacenter distributed; petabyte-scale Single process; scale by parallel invocation Single JVM server; scales via NFS
Shard format Custom binary: magic+version+CRC + sorted trigram table + varint posting lists + doc-meta CBOR Custom binary; 8-byte block alignment; no per-shard CRC Custom binary with per-doc ngram fingerprint sections Undisclosed N/A Lucene segment format
mmap / zero-copy mmap read-only; zero-copy PostingList<'a>; Arc-ref-counted unmap mmap in Go; GC manages lifetime mmap in Go; GC manages lifetime Undisclosed mmap line scanning via memmap2 JVM heap-based
Open source Yes (Apache-2.0 / MIT, this project) Yes (BSD-3, google/codesearch) Yes (Apache-2.0, sourcegraph/zoekt) No (GitHub internal) Yes (MIT/Unlicense, BurntSushi/ripgrep) Yes (CDDL, oracle/opengrok)

Google Code Search (codesearch): Cox's reference implementation directly inspired this design. Its key differentiating decision is treating the trigram index purely as a filter with zero ranking — results are returned sorted by filesystem path. This wins for internal developer tools where ranking is unnecessary and simplicity is paramount; the sparse codebase (~2k lines of Go) makes it the easiest starting point for study. The lack of incremental indexing is the main limitation.

Sourcegraph Zoekt: Zoekt's distinguishing decision is the per-doc ngram fingerprint — a compact bitmask recording which 3-grams are present in each document, stored before the full posting list blob. This enables rapid per-document candidate rejection without reading the full posting list, at the cost of extra per-doc storage (~16KB bitmask per doc). Zoekt wins for Sourcegraph's multi-tenant hosted scale (millions of repos) where per-shard hot shards consume too many page-cache bytes without the fingerprint short-circuit.

GitHub Code Search ("Blackbird"): GitHub's 2023 engineering blog describes a hybrid suffix-array + inverted-index approach that enables exact substring search (not trigram-approximate). Exact substring search eliminates false positives entirely at the cost of a larger index (suffix arrays are O(N log N) space vs O(N) for trigram). Blackbird wins at petabyte scale where false-positive verification load (PikeVm over millions of candidates) is too expensive and the larger index can be distributed across many nodes.

ripgrep (regex-scan baseline): ripgrep uses no index; it scans files with SIMD-accelerated line iteration and compiled regex. Its key decision is to trade precomputation (index build) for search simplicity. ripgrep wins for single-directory ad-hoc searches where the corpus fits in page cache and query frequency is low — it has zero index maintenance cost. It loses when the corpus is large and queries repeat, where index lookup amortizes the query cost.

OpenGrok: OpenGrok uses Lucene's full-text inverted index, which tokenizes source code at the lexical level (identifiers, keywords) rather than raw bytes. This enables token-level BM25 ranking and cross-reference queries but loses the ability to find arbitrary byte patterns (e.g., multi-line strings, raw hex literals) that trigram search handles naturally. OpenGrok wins for Java/enterprise environments already running JVM infrastructure and requiring rich cross-reference and historical navigation features.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

All numbers derived step-by-step from the System Constraints table (§4.1). Formulas shown.

Corpus baseline: 1 M documents × 10 KB average file size = 10 GB source bytes.

1. Raw trigram extraction volume. Each byte contributes one overlapping 3-gram (except the last 2 bytes per file). trigrams_per_byte ≈ 1.0; total_trigram_events = 10 GB × 1.0 = 10 B trigram events (with duplicates). After per-document dedup: average unique trigrams per 10 KB file ≈ 8,000 (empirical; typical code files cover ~50% of the 16 M possible trigrams up to frequency saturation). total_unique_postings = 1 M docs × 8,000 = 8 B postings.

2. Posting list on-disk size. Each posting = one delta-encoded DocId. Average DocId gap for a trigram appearing in 0.1% of 1M docs = gap ≈ 1,000; LEB128(1000) = 2 bytes. posting_list_bytes = 8 B postings × 2 bytes = 16 GB. The constraint §4.1 states index ≤ 30% of source; 16 GB / 10 GB = 160% — this exceeds the target. Resolution: high-frequency trigrams (appearing in >1% of docs, i.e., >10k posting entries) are capped or omitted (their posting list degrades to TrigramQuery::All). Empirically, ~5% of the 16M trigrams cover 60% of posting entries. Excluding them: effective_posting_bytes ≈ 16 GB × 0.40 = 6.4 GB. Plus doc-meta: 128 bytes/doc × 1M = 128 MB. Total index ≈ 6.5 GB / 10 GB source ≈ 65% — still above the 30% target. This motivates the configurable frequency cap and the empirical observation that real code corpora are denser than random text.

3. RAM required for cold index (mmap working set). A typical regex query touches 3–6 trigrams. Per trigram: one trigram table lookup (8 bytes) + posting list scan. For a rare trigram (posting length 100 docs): list_bytes = 100 × 1.5 bytes avg = 150 bytes. pages_per_trigram_lookup = ceil(150 / 4096) = 1 page = 4 KB. For 6 trigrams × 8 shards = 48 page faults per cold query. At 1ms per cold page fault: cold_query_overhead = 48 ms. This matches the p99 latency target of 200ms with room for verification. Warm index (all pages in cache): zero page faults; posting decode is pure memory bandwidth.

4. Trigram table RAM footprint. n_distinct_trigrams per shard: assume 5M (out of 16.7M possible, typical for a code-heavy corpus). trigram_table_size = 5M × 12 bytes per TrigramEntry = 60 MB per shard. For 16 shards: total_trigram_table_ram = 16 × 60 MB = 960 MB. Binary search: O(log 5M) = 23 comparisons; all 23 cache-line accesses within the 60 MB working set.

5. Query fan-out concurrency budget. tasks_per_query = n_shards = 16. concurrent_queries = 20 (from §4.1 throughput target). peak_tokio_tasks = 20 × 16 = 320 tasks. Each task stack ~8 KB: stack_RAM = 320 × 8 KB = 2.5 MB. Negligible.

6. Candidate set size per query (posting list intersection output). A query with 4 required trigrams, each with posting list length 10k (0.1% of corpus): naive_intersection_output ≈ N × p^k = 1M × (0.001)^4 = 1 doc (for independent trigrams). In practice, trigrams in code are correlated; realistic candidate set: 50–500 docs per query. candidate_docid_vec_bytes = 500 × 4 bytes = 2 KB — trivially small.

7. PikeVm verification cost per query. 500 candidates × 10 KB avg file size × |NFA| = 50 states. verify_ops = 500 × 10,000 bytes × 50 states = 250 M state-byte ops. At 1 ns per state-byte op (modern CPU): verify_time = 250 ms — this is the dominant cost at high candidate counts, confirming why candidate reduction (MM1) is the primary lever.

8. Index build throughput model. Source ingestion rate §4.1: ≥ 50 MB/s. For 1 M docs × 10 KB = 10 GB: build_time ≥ 10 GB / 50 MB/s = 200 s ≈ 3.3 min. Trigram extraction: 10 B events × 50 ns/event = 500 s bottleneck without parallelism. With 8-core parallel extraction: trigram_extract_time ≈ 62 s. Sort+merge of 8B postings: O(N log N) at 100 M ops/s = 80 s. Total estimated build: 3–5 min on 8-core NVMe — consistent with §4.1 targets.

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Each question requires synthesis of at least two sections of this blueprint. Answering them correctly demonstrates principal-engineer command.

Q1. A user queries /im(port|plement)/ and reports results are extremely slow (p99 = 2 s). Walk through the complete execution path and identify which metrics you would inspect first, what root cause is most likely, and what change to the query or system would fix it. (Resolving §: Phase 6 lowering, Phase 8 executor, §4.1 performance targets, P2, MM2)

Q2. After a rolling upgrade, you discover that csearch-server returns different results for the same query on two replicas. One replica is running the old binary, one the new. The new binary introduced a change to csearch-trigquery::lower() for the Repetition(min=1) case. Prove whether this change can cause false negatives, false positives, or both, and design a CI test that would have caught it before deploy. (Resolving §: I1, Phase 6, Phase 14 differential test, Testing & Verification Strategy)

Q3. The shard count is growing without bound despite the compaction policy. csearch-cli stat shows 64 shards, max_shards=8. Trace the exact code path that should have triggered compaction and identify three distinct failure modes that could cause compaction to silently not fire. (Resolving §: Phase 12, 12.6 pitfalls, Scalability Analysis, I7)

Q4. You are migrating from shard format version 1 to version 2, which changes the DocMetaTable encoding from CBOR to a fixed-binary layout. Design the migration procedure such that: (a) old binaries can still read old shards, (b) the new binary can read both old and new shards, (c) the migration is atomic with no search downtime. (Resolving §: Compatibility & Versioning, I3, I7, Build & Release Engineering)

Q5. A corpus contains a file generated.pb.go with 200,000 function definitions. A sym: query for a name that appears in that file returns zero results. Enumerate all the distinct code paths that could produce this behavior, ordered by decreasing likelihood, and the metric that would distinguish each. (Resolving §: Phase 10, §10.6 pitfalls, Failure Modes & Recovery F8, §8.12)

Q6. Design a test that proves the incremental indexer satisfies I5 (content addressing) and I9 (deleted docs produce no results) simultaneously in the presence of a file that is (a) renamed, (b) content-identical to another file in a different repo, and (c) deleted from one repo but retained in another. (Resolving §: Phase 12, I5, I9, Testing & Verification Strategy integration tests)

Q7. Explain why TrigramQuery::All lowers to a full-corpus scan rather than an error, and derive the worst-case latency for a . query on the 1M-doc index from the Back-of-Envelope numbers. Is it within the deadline budget? What mechanism prevents this from being a DoS vector? (Resolving §: P2, §8.7 edge case, Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model #7, P6, Threat Model T9)

Q8. A new team member proposes replacing the galloping intersection with a Roaring Bitmap intersection, citing a 5× speedup on synthetic benchmarks. Construct the argument for and against the change using the ADR table, the Comparative Architecture Table, and the Back-of-Envelope numbers. What corpus property would make Roaring Bitmaps strictly better, and does the real-world code corpus have that property? (Resolving §: ADR-12, Comparative Architecture Table, Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model #3, Phase 8, MM6)

Q9. The csearch-regex crate must guarantee O(|text|×|NFA|) runtime. Describe a code path that could silently violate this guarantee without a test failure, and design the property-based test that would catch it within one hour of fuzzing. (Resolving §: Phase 5, I4, Phase 14 fuzz, §5.9 failure analysis, MM3)

Q10. You have received a bug report: a sym:Parser query returns a result from a shard that was supposed to be superseded after a csearch index --incremental run. Trace through the manifest lifecycle and identify at least three distinct races or ordering bugs that could produce this behavior. (Resolving §: I9, Phase 12, 12.6 pitfalls, Failure Modes & Recovery F8, I7)

Q11. Explain why the PostingList<'a> lifetime design requires Arc<MmapShard> rather than a simpler &'shard [u8] borrow, and prove that the current design is sound by tracing the lifetime from ShardReader::posting_list() through a fan-out shard task to the final SearchResult returned to the HTTP client. (Resolving §: P5, §11.3, Phase 13 shard registry, §11.12 trait obligations)

Q12. The differential test shows 3 false negatives on the following three query types: (a) /\bfoo\b/ with word boundary, (b) /foo(?i)BAR/ with inline case flag, (c) /[^\n]{200}foo/ with a wide negated class before the literal. For each, identify exactly which HIR node in the lowering function produces All when it should produce trigrams, and state whether fixing it would be sound or require the All fallback. (Resolving §: Phase 6, P2 lowering table, I1, Phase 14 differential test)

Q13. Draw the complete state machine for a shard file from creation to GC deletion, labeling every transition with the crate and function responsible, and every state with the set of invariants that hold in that state. (Resolving §: I3, I7, I9, §8.10 incremental state transitions, Phase 12, Failure Modes & Recovery F1, F3)

Q14. A deployment has n_shards = 32 and is receiving 100 concurrent queries. What is the peak number of active tokio tasks? If the Tokio worker thread pool has 8 threads, what is the minimum number of task context switches per query and what is the expected P99 scheduling overhead? (Resolving §: Scalability Analysis concurrency table, Phase 13, Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model #5)

Q15. Compare the soundness obligations of TrigramQuery::And vs TrigramQuery::Or. Show by counterexample that swapping them (using And where Or is required) produces false negatives. Construct the minimal regex and corpus that would trigger the bug and be caught by the soundness fuzz target. (Resolving §: I1, Phase 6, §8.7, Phase 14 fuzz, MM2, MM9)

Q16. You are asked to add support for NOT in TrigramQuery (i.e., return only documents that do NOT contain a given trigram). Prove that this is architecturally sound or unsound with respect to I1, and if unsound, specify the exact constraint that must be relaxed. (Resolving §: I1, P2, §11.6 TrigramQuery semantics, Phase 6 lowering)

Q17. The manifest atomic swap uses rename(2) which is documented as failing with EXDEV when source and destination are on different filesystems. Enumerate the production deployment configurations where this failure is likely, and detail the fallback path implemented in Phase 12 including its atomicity properties. (Resolving §: I7, 12.6 pitfalls EXDEV, Deployment Topology, Operational Readiness runbooks)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Four fundamentally different approaches to code search, each with an ASCII sketch, the regime where it wins, the real system that embodies it, and why this blueprint rejected it.

Alt-1: Trigram Index + Regex Filter (Russ Cox / Zoekt — what we built, for contrast)

 [Source files]
      |
      v  sliding 3-byte window
 [Trigram posting lists]
      |  boolean AND/OR over trigrams
      v
 [Candidate DocId set]  (superset of true matches)
      |  PikeVm verification per candidate
      v
 [Exact MatchSpans]

This IS the chosen architecture. Included for contrast only. Wins when: the corpus is source code (high trigram selectivity for identifiers); regex queries are the primary workload; index size must be < 30% of source. Embodied by: google/codesearch, Sourcegraph Zoekt, Hound. Why chosen: optimal balance of index size, query latency, and query expressiveness for the stated workload.

Alt-2: Suffix Array + Substring Index

 [Source files, concatenated]
      |
      v  O(N log N) or O(N) SA construction
 [Suffix Array: sorted offsets into concatenated bytes]
      |  binary search for any substring
      v
 [Exact suffix positions]  (zero false positives)
      |  line/doc lookup via auxiliary offset table
      v
 [Exact MatchSpans, no verification step]

Wins when: the query workload is dominated by exact substring searches (no regex); false positives from trigram over-approximation impose unacceptable verification cost (very dense corpora where most files match short trigrams); index rebuild is rare (O(N log N) construction). The space overhead is O(N log N) bits for the SA vs O(N) for the trigram index on typical code corpora. Embodied by: GitHub Code Search "Blackbird" (reported suffix-array hybrid, 2023 blog). Why rejected: regex support requires full RE2 integration on top of the SA (SA only handles exact substring; regex still needs NFA verification), negating the false-positive advantage. SA construction is more complex to implement incrementally (Phase 12 would require rebuilding the global SA on any file change). SA index size is typically 4–8× source, exceeding the 30% target of §4.1.

Alt-3: Inverted Token Index (Lucene-style)

 [Source files]
      |  language-aware tokenization (identifiers, strings, numbers)
      v
 [Token → posting list]  (like a search engine, not a byte scanner)
      |  token-level boolean query
      v
 [DocId set]
      |  BM25 scoring, no PikeVm verification (token-level exact)
      v
 [Ranked results, no regex verification step]

Wins when: the primary query type is identifier search (find all uses of HashMap); ranking by term frequency is more important than exact byte-level pattern matching; the deployment already runs Elasticsearch or Solr. Token-level indexing is smaller than trigram indexing for the same recall on identifier queries because identifiers are whole tokens (fewer false positives). Embodied by: OpenGrok (Lucene), Elasticsearch-backed code search (many enterprise deployments). Why rejected: cannot handle arbitrary regex patterns (e.g., /\bfoo[0-9]+\b/), raw byte patterns (binary sequences), or structural patterns that span token boundaries. The tokenization step requires language-specific grammars (higher maintenance than trigrams). The lang:, file: filters are easier to implement in the trigram model because filtering is post-candidate. Token indexing also breaks the zero-false-negative invariant if tokenization is incorrect (missed tokens → missed results).

Alt-4: Brute-Force Distributed Grep (ripgrep Fan-Out)

 [Query]
      |  broadcast to N worker nodes
      v  [Worker 1: ripgrep --glob '*.rs'] ... [Worker N: ripgrep ...]
      |  SIMD line scan, no index
      v
 [Per-node match streams]
      |  merge, deduplicate, rank
      v
 [Ranked results]

Wins when: the corpus is small enough to scan in < 1 s per worker (< 1 GB per node), queries are infrequent (< 1 QPS), and the operational cost of index maintenance exceeds the latency benefit. Also wins when the query workload is entirely ad-hoc with no repeated patterns, negating the amortization benefit of the index. Embodied by: ripgrep itself (single-machine version), Hound's early design, some internal Sourcegraph fallback paths. Why rejected: at 10 GB+ corpus size, even parallel grep across 16 cores takes 5–20 seconds per query — far outside the 200 ms p99 target of §4.1. The approach scales only horizontally (more machines), adding operational complexity and cost. For code search where the same codebase is queried thousands of times per day, the index amortizes its build cost within minutes of operation.

Deferred Features Register

The following features were explicitly scoped out during design. Each entry records where it was deferred, the phase that owns the prerequisite work, and the anchor to the PhaseDeferred(...) call in §1.9.

# Feature Deferred In Prerequisite Phase PhaseDeferred Anchor Unlock Condition
D1 Multi-node distributed index (cross-host shard distribution) §1.9, §1.3 Non-Goals Phase 13 (server fan-out must work single-host first) PhaseDeferred("Phase 13") — multi-node Single-host fan-out proven correct under load; shard format stable (v1+); networking layer designed
D2 mTLS / JWT authentication on HTTP API §1.9, §1.3 Non-Goals Phase 13 (HTTP server must exist) PhaseDeferred("Phase 13") — mTLS / JWT auth HTTP server shipped; security threat model updated with auth requirements
D3 Full LSP symbol protocol (beyond ctags-style) §1.9, §1.3 Non-Goals Phase 10 (ctags-style symbols must prove value first) PhaseDeferred("Phase 10") — full LSP symbol protocol tree-sitter grammars stabilized; Phase 10 recall measured to be insufficient for target use case
D4 SIMD varint decode optimization §1.9 Phase 14 (benchmarks must show decode is the bottleneck) PhaseDeferred("Phase 14") — SIMD varint decode Phase 14 benchmark proves posting decode > 15% of query latency; P5 (Lemire) paper implementation validated
D5 Persistent query cache / result TTL cache §1.9 Phase 13 (server must be running; cache coherence with incremental index is non-trivial) PhaseDeferred("Phase 14") — persistent query cache Phase 12 incremental indexing stable; cache invalidation strategy defined; cache hit rate measured on real query log

Glossary

All definitions are implementation-relevant for this codebase. Terms appear in alphabetical order. Where a term maps directly to a named type or crate, that binding is noted.

Term Definition
backtracking Execution strategy in which a regex engine speculatively tries alternatives and undoes choices on failure; worst-case time is exponential in input length. Forbidden in csearch-regex; replaced by Thompson NFA / Pike VM (P3, I4).
BM25 Okapi BM25 probabilistic term-frequency ranking function. Adapted in csearch-rank as a reference model for match-density scoring; IDF is replaced by a path-importance signal because code files have no document-frequency distribution analogous to natural-language corpora.
candidate set The set of DocIds that survive posting-list intersection for a given TrigramQuery. A strict superset of the true match set by I1 (no false negatives). Candidates are forwarded to PikeVm for verification; false positives are discarded there.
catastrophic backtracking Pathological super-linear (often exponential) blowup in backtracking-based regex engines triggered by adversarial input strings. Prevented by the Thompson NFA design (I4, P3); any input must run in O(
content addressing Using the sha256 digest as a document's stable identity (I5, DocMeta.sha256). Enables: deduplication across repos; idempotent re-index of unchanged files; change detection without mtime trust.
ctags Tool convention (universal-ctags format) for extracting source-code symbol definitions from files using per-language heuristic regex patterns. csearch-symbol follows ctags-style patterns to produce SymbolEntry records; does not depend on the ctags binary.
delta encoding Encoding a posting list as successive differences d[i] = DocId[i] − DocId[i−1] instead of absolute values. Always non-negative because posting lists are strictly ascending (I2). Delta values are then varint-encoded, typically fitting in 1–2 bytes for dense lists.
DocId DocId(u32) — dense integer assigned to each document within one shard during build (Phase 4). Globally unique only when paired with ShardId. Posting lists are sorted ascending by DocId, enabling galloping intersection (I2).
DocMeta Per-document record in the shard's doc-meta table: { path, repo: RepoId, lang, size, sha256, line_count }. Retrieved zero-copy from the mmap shard by DocId during verification and result assembly.
false negative A document that truly matches a query but is absent from the candidate set or result set. Forbidden by the soundness invariant (I1, P2). The trigram lowering is designed so false negatives are structurally impossible; this is the primary correctness obligation.
false positive A candidate DocId that satisfies the TrigramQuery but does not match the regex when verified by PikeVm. Increases verification work and latency but does not affect correctness. Minimising false positives is a precision optimisation, not a correctness requirement.
fan-out Concurrent dispatch of one query to multiple shards in csearch-server. Results from each shard are merged under a shared per-query deadline and global result cap (P6). Implemented in csearch-server/src/fanout.rs.
galloping intersection Posting-list intersection algorithm where the pointer in the smaller list advances by successive doubling in the larger list, then binary search to the exact position. Complexity O(n log(m/n)) vs O(m+n) for sequential merge; critical when one list is much shorter. Implemented in csearch-search/src/intersect.rs.
HIR (High-level IR) The regex-syntax crate's high-level intermediate representation for a parsed regex pattern. Represents alternation, concatenation, repetition, character classes, anchors, etc. Input to both csearch-trigquery (lowering to TrigramQuery) and csearch-regex (NFA compilation).
hot reload csearch-server detects a manifest swap (I7) through inotify/kqueue or periodic stat and re-opens the new shard set without restarting. In-flight queries drain against the old shard set; new queries see the updated set. Implemented in csearch-server/src/shard_registry.rs.
immutable shard A shard whose bytes are sealed with a CRC at write time and never subsequently modified (P4, I3). Supersession is recorded in the manifest by marking a ShardId inactive; the shard file remains until it is compacted away.
incremental indexing Re-indexing only files whose sha256 or mtime has changed since the last build, rather than rebuilding the entire index (P9, Phase 12). Implemented in csearch-incremental; guarantees correctness via I7 manifest atomicity and I9 deletion-via-supersession.
inverted index Data structure mapping each Trigram to the sorted list of DocIds whose content contains that trigram. The primary data structure inside a shard. Each shard is one partition of a global inverted index over all indexed documents.
language detection Classification of a file's programming language by: extension lookup table (primary), shebang line parse (secondary), content sniff for ambiguous cases (tertiary). Implemented in csearch-ingest/src/lang_detect.rs. Drives lang: filter and per-language symbol extractor dispatch.
LSM compaction Log-Structured Merge-Tree style compaction: accumulate small shards from incremental builds, then merge them into a larger shard, delete the inputs, and update the manifest. Bounds shard count at O(log(N_files)). Implemented in csearch-incremental/src/compact.rs.
manifest A file or atomic record listing the active ShardIds and their on-disk paths, plus per-repo last-indexed state (sha256, mtime). Swapped atomically on any shard change (I7). Read at server startup and on hot-reload. Managed by csearch-incremental/src/manifest.rs.
match density The fraction of a document's content lines that contain regex matches. Primary RankScore signal: a document where matches are clustered in few lines typically represents a more focused result than one where a single match appears in a 10 000-line file.
MatchSpan { doc: (ShardId, DocId), line: u32, col: u32, len: u32, line_text_ref } — the exact location of one regex match within a document (I6). Produced by PikeVm verification; the atom of a SearchResult.
mmap Memory-mapped file I/O (memmap2 crate). The shard reader accesses posting-list bytes and doc-meta bytes directly through OS page mappings with no user-space copy (P5). The OS page cache serves as the shard read cache at no extra cost.
NFA (Thompson NFA) Non-deterministic finite automaton constructed by Thompson's algorithm from a regex HIR. All active states are tracked simultaneously in a bitset; at each input byte the set is advanced. Guarantees linear time O(
n-gram / trigram An n-gram of length 3 bytes extracted from a file's raw bytes. Trigram(u32) packs three bytes big-endian into the low 24 bits: `b0<<16
over-approximation A TrigramQuery Q over-approximates a regex R if: for every document D, if R matches D then D satisfies Q. Q may admit documents not matched by R (false positives) but structurally cannot exclude true matches (P2, I1). The proof obligation lives in docs/trigram-query-soundness.md.
path importance A RankScore component that rewards files at shallower directory depth and penalises test/, vendor/, third_party/, _test.go-style paths. Implemented as a weighted negative depth + path-prefix penalty in csearch-rank/src/score.rs.
Pike VM Submatch-capable NFA simulation algorithm by Rob Pike. Tracks which NFA states are active and which capture groups they carry simultaneously, advancing one byte at a time. Linear time O(
posting list Sorted ascending list of DocIds sharing a given Trigram; stored in the shard as a contiguous run of delta+varint bytes after the trigram table entry. Decoded on demand by PostingReader without decoding the full list into a heap vector.
PostingList PostingList<'a> — a zero-copy iterator view over the encoded posting-list bytes for one Trigram in a mmap'd shard (P5). The lifetime 'a is bound to the shard mmap.
PostingWriter / PostingReader PostingWriter — sequential append encoder: accepts ascending DocIds, writes delta+varint to an output buffer. PostingReader — mmap-backed seekable decoder keyed by Trigram; locates the list via the trigram table binary search.
query deadline A per-query monotonic deadline (Duration from query arrival). All fan-out shard workers check the deadline before beginning verification of each candidate; the search executor returns partial results rather than timing out hard (P6).
RankScore RankScore(f32) — composite relevance score: w1*match_density + w2*path_importance + w3*symbol_def_bonus + w4*repo_weight. Weights are compile-time constants tunable per deployment. Used in top-k selection with deterministic tie-break (I10).
regex lowering The transformation in csearch-trigquery from a regex HIR to a TrigramQuery. The result is a sound over-approximation used for shard lookup before PikeVm verification. Core algorithm: concat → AND of required literal trigrams; alternation → OR; unbounded repetition / . → All.
RepoId / ShardId RepoId(u32) — stable integer identity for one indexed repository. ShardId(u32) — identity for one on-disk shard file. Multiple shards may cover the same repo (after incremental updates); the manifest tracks their relationship.
result cap Maximum number of SearchResults returned per query (configurable, default 100). Enforced in csearch-search/src/executor.rs and in the HTTP handler. Bounds both per-query latency and response payload size (P6).
shard An immutable on-disk unit containing: ShardHeader, a sorted trigram table, concatenated delta+varint posting lists, and a doc-meta table. Written once by csearch-index, read many times by csearch-shard. The atom of indexing and compaction.
ShardHeader First bytes of every shard file: { magic: [u8;4], version: u16, n_docs: u32, n_trigrams: u32, trigram_table_off: u64, doc_table_off: u64, crc: u32 }. Validated on open; CRC covers header+data.
soundness The property that the trigram query lowering is a sound over-approximation of the regex (I1, P2). Proven by structural induction on the regex HIR: each HIR constructor case either produces a TrigramQuery that contains all trigrams required by strings matching that case, or conservatively produces All. Tested by the csearch-trigquery soundness fuzz target.
symbol index A secondary inverted index in csearch-symbol keyed by symbol name strings rather than trigrams. Posting lists contain (ShardId, DocId, line) tuples for definitions and references. Serves sym: queries and contributes the symbol_def_bonus to RankScore.
Trigram Trigram(u32) — 24-bit key: bytes [b0, b1, b2] → `b0<<16
TrigramQuery enum { All, Any, And(Vec<TrigramQuery>), Or(Vec<TrigramQuery>), Trigram(Trigram) } — a boolean formula over Trigrams derived from a regex or literal query. Evaluated against posting lists to produce the candidate DocId set. All is the trivially true query (any doc is a candidate); Any is trivially false (no candidate).
varint Variable-length integer encoding (LEB128): small values (0–127) encode in 1 byte; each additional 7 bits of value requires one additional byte. Used for delta-encoded DocId gaps in posting lists. Reduces storage without loss (P7).
verification The phase in csearch-search where PikeVm runs the original regex over each candidate document's bytes, producing exact MatchSpans. Verification ensures that the final result set contains no false positives and no false negatives (P1, I6).
zero-copy The search read path accesses posting-list bytes and DocMeta records directly from mmap pages; no heap allocation or memcpy occurs between the shard bytes and the byte slice passed to the decoder. Allocations occur only for the candidate DocId set and the result Vec (P5).

Appendix A — File Inventory

All paths are relative to the workspace root. Leaf files only. Layout is consistent with the repository tree in section 10.

Path Purpose
Cargo.toml Workspace manifest; member crate list; shared dependency versions and feature flags.
Cargo.lock Reproducible dependency lock for the full workspace.
README.md Project overview; quick-start index and search commands; pointer to docs/.
docs/architecture.md Canonical architecture narrative; hosts the canonical ASCII architecture diagram.
docs/shard-format.md Byte-level shard layout specification: ShardHeader field offsets, trigram table entry format, posting-list encoding, doc-meta table encoding.
docs/trigram-query-soundness.md Soundness proof sketch for csearch-trigquery: structural induction over HIR cases; the normative reference for I1 and P2.
docs/query-language.md User-facing grammar reference for the csearch-query DSL; all filter operators, quoting rules, and error message catalogue.
docs/incremental-indexing.md Design rationale and operational guide for csearch-incremental: change detection algorithm, compaction policy, manifest lifecycle.
testdata/repos/small/ Tiny synthetic repository (~50 files) used for unit tests and fast CI integration tests.
testdata/repos/medium/ Mid-size corpus (~10 000 files, multiple languages) used for differential tests against grep -rE.
testdata/corpora/trigram-fuzz/ Seed corpus for the csearch-trigquery soundness fuzz target (Phase 14).

testdata/fixtures/symbols/go_fixture.go Go source file with known symbol definitions; used by csearch-symbol unit tests.
testdata/fixtures/symbols/python_fixture.py Python source file with known symbol definitions; used by csearch-symbol unit tests.
csearch-core/Cargo.toml Crate manifest for shared types; zero I/O dependencies.
csearch-core/src/lib.rs Re-exports all public types from sub-modules.
csearch-core/src/types.rs Newtype definitions: DocId(u32), RepoId(u32), ShardId(u32), Trigram(u32), RankScore(f32), Posting.
csearch-core/src/query_ast.rs QueryAst and Filter enum definitions; no parsing logic.
csearch-core/src/trigram_query.rs TrigramQuery enum; And/Or normalisation and simplification helpers (no lowering).
csearch-core/src/match_span.rs MatchSpan, SearchResult, SymbolEntry, SymbolKind definitions.
csearch-core/src/doc_meta.rs DocMeta struct; Lang enum with all supported language tags.
csearch-core/src/error.rs CsearchError unified error enum; thiserror-derived; used by all crates.
csearch-core/tests/types_roundtrip.rs Property tests: Trigram pack/unpack bijection (I8); DocId ordering; MatchSpan field ranges.
csearch-trigram/Cargo.toml Trigram extraction crate manifest.
csearch-trigram/src/lib.rs Public API: extract(bytes: &[u8]) -> Vec<Trigram>, extract_dedup, frequency_map.
csearch-trigram/src/extract.rs Overlapping 3-byte window iteration; newline-handling policy; empty/short-file guard (<3 bytes).
csearch-trigram/src/fold.rs Case-fold trigram expansion: for each trigram, emit the ASCII-lowercased variant when case-insensitive indexing is active; deduplicates with the original.
csearch-trigram/tests/extract_tests.rs Unit tests: known byte sequences, short files, all-same-byte files, non-ASCII bytes, case-fold output verification.
csearch-regex/Cargo.toml Linear-time regex crate manifest; depends on regex-syntax for HIR.
csearch-regex/src/lib.rs Public API: compile(pattern: &str) -> Result<Regex>; Regex::find_all(text: &[u8]) -> Vec<MatchSpan>.
csearch-regex/src/nfa.rs Thompson NFA construction from HIR: state graph, epsilon-closure table, character-class expansion.
csearch-regex/src/pike_vm.rs Pike VM execution: active-state bitset advance per input byte; capture-group slot tracking; O(n×m) guarantee.
csearch-regex/src/hir_util.rs HIR traversal helpers shared by NFA compilation and trigram lowering; character class to byte-range conversion.
csearch-regex/tests/linear_time.rs Property tests confirming O(n) scaling: measure wall time vs input length for known ReDoS patterns; must be linear.
csearch-regex/tests/correctness.rs Differential tests: csearch-regex output matches the reference regex crate on random (pattern, input) pairs.
csearch-trigquery/Cargo.toml Regex-to-TrigramQuery lowering crate manifest.
csearch-trigquery/src/lib.rs Public API: lower(hir: &Hir) -> TrigramQuery; soundness contract stated in doc comment.
csearch-trigquery/src/lower.rs Structural recursion over HIR constructors: concat → AND of required-literal trigrams; alternation → OR; unbounded repetition / dot → All.
csearch-trigquery/src/required.rs required_literals(hir: &Hir) -> Vec<String> — extracts shortest required substrings from a regex for AND factoring.
csearch-trigquery/src/simplify.rs Boolean simplification: absorb All into Or, absorb Any into And, flatten nested And/And, deduplicate Trigram leaves.
csearch-trigquery/fuzz/fuzz_targets/soundness.rs Fuzz target: random regex + random document bytes; assert that candidate-set membership holds (I1).
csearch-trigquery/tests/lowering_tests.rs Known-answer tests for specific regex patterns; All/Any boundary cases; alternation depth.
csearch-index/Cargo.toml Index builder crate manifest.
csearch-index/src/lib.rs Public API: IndexBuilder::new(opts), add_doc(meta, bytes), finish() -> ShardFile.
csearch-index/src/accumulator.rs In-memory HashMap<Trigram, Vec<DocId>> accumulation; spill to a temp segment when memory threshold exceeded.
csearch-index/src/shard_writer.rs Serialise accumulated trigram→DocId data into on-disk shard: header write, trigram table sort+write, delta+varint posting-list serialisation, doc-meta table, CRC finalise.
csearch-index/src/merge.rs N-way merge of sorted posting-list streams from multiple spill segments; produces a single merged sorted stream.
csearch-index/src/doc_store.rs DocMeta accumulator; assigns dense DocIds in insertion order; serialises to the doc-meta table.
csearch-index/src/varint.rs LEB128 encode/decode; shared with csearch-shard; exposed as a public submodule.
csearch-index/tests/build_tests.rs Build a shard from synthetic docs; re-open with csearch-shard; verify every posting list is correct and CRC validates.
csearch-shard/Cargo.toml Shard reader crate manifest; depends on memmap2, csearch-core.
csearch-shard/src/lib.rs Public API: Shard::open(path) -> Result<Shard>, posting_list(t: Trigram) -> PostingList<'_>, doc_meta(id: DocId) -> Result<DocMeta>, all_trigrams() -> impl Iterator<Item=Trigram>.
csearch-shard/src/mmap_shard.rs mmap lifecycle (open, map, pin advisory); bounds checks; header magic and version validation; CRC verification on open.
csearch-shard/src/trigram_table.rs Binary search over the sorted trigram table to locate the byte offset and length of a posting list for a given Trigram.
csearch-shard/src/posting_reader.rs PostingReader: stateful delta+varint decode iterator over a raw byte slice; zero-copy (P5).
csearch-shard/src/doc_table.rs DocMeta deserialisation from the doc-meta table section given a DocId index.
csearch-shard/fuzz/fuzz_targets/reader_fuzz.rs Fuzz target: arbitrary bytes presented as a shard file; must return structured errors, never panic.
csearch-shard/tests/roundtrip.rs Write a shard with csearch-index; read back with csearch-shard; assert all posting lists, all DocMeta fields, CRC validity.
csearch-ingest/Cargo.toml Ingestion crate manifest; depends on ignore, infer, sha2, encoding_rs.
csearch-ingest/src/lib.rs Public API: ingest_repo(root: &Path, repo_id: RepoId) -> impl Iterator<Item=Result<DocPayload>>.
csearch-ingest/src/walk.rs Recursive directory walk using the ignore crate; honours .gitignore, .ignore, custom patterns; symlink loop detection.
csearch-ingest/src/lang_detect.rs Language detection: extension map lookup, shebang parse (#!/usr/bin/env X), content sniff for HTML/XML/binary ambiguity.
csearch-ingest/src/filter.rs Binary-file detection (null-byte scan, non-UTF-8 ratio threshold), minified-file heuristic (max line length), configurable file-size cap.
csearch-ingest/src/hash.rs sha256 digest computation over file bytes; returns [u8; 32]; used in DocMeta and change detection (I5).
csearch-ingest/tests/walk_tests.rs Integration tests against testdata/repos/small/: expected doc count, language assignments, correct filtering of binaries and oversized files.
csearch-symbol/Cargo.toml Symbol extraction crate manifest.
csearch-symbol/src/lib.rs Public API: extract_symbols(lang: Lang, bytes: &[u8]) -> Vec<SymbolEntry>; build_symbol_index(entries) -> SymbolIndex.
csearch-symbol/src/extractors/mod.rs Dispatcher: routes to per-language extractor by Lang tag; falls back to generic extractor for unknown languages.
csearch-symbol/src/extractors/rust.rs Heuristic extractor for Rust: line-level regex patterns for fn, struct, enum, trait, impl, const, static definitions.
csearch-symbol/src/extractors/go.rs Heuristic extractor for Go: func, type, var, const definition patterns.
csearch-symbol/src/extractors/python.rs Heuristic extractor for Python: def, class, async def definition patterns.
csearch-symbol/src/extractors/generic.rs Fallback extractor covering common C/C++/Java/JavaScript definition patterns.
csearch-symbol/src/symbol_index.rs Accumulates SymbolEntry records; builds HashMap<String, Vec<(ShardId,DocId,u32)>> for name → location posting-list; serialised alongside doc-meta in the shard.
csearch-symbol/tests/extract_tests.rs Known-symbol tests: fixture files for each language, assert exact SymbolEntry name/kind/line lists.
csearch-query/Cargo.toml Query-language parser crate manifest; depends on csearch-core, csearch-regex.
csearch-query/src/lib.rs Public API: parse(input: &str) -> Result<(QueryAst, Vec<Filter>)>.
csearch-query/src/parser.rs Recursive-descent parser: bare literal, /regex/ delimiters, field:value tokens, implicit AND of terms, quoting.
csearch-query/src/grammar.ebnf Non-executable EBNF grammar; authoritative syntax reference for the query DSL.
csearch-query/tests/parse_tests.rs Round-trip parse tests; precedence tests; error-message regression tests for malformed input (unclosed regex, unknown field names).
csearch-search/Cargo.toml Search executor crate manifest; depends on all core and index crates.
csearch-search/src/lib.rs Public API: execute(query: QueryAst, shards: &[Shard], opts: SearchOpts) -> Vec<SearchResult>.
csearch-search/src/executor.rs Orchestrates: lower QueryAst → TrigramQuery; intersect/union posting lists; apply Filters; verify candidates; collect MatchSpans; call ranker.
csearch-search/src/intersect.rs Galloping intersection and sorted union of PostingList iterators; ascending-order assumption (I2).
csearch-search/src/verify.rs Per-candidate PikeVm execution; byte-offset to (line, col) conversion; MatchSpan collection; result-cap early-exit; I6 exactness.
csearch-search/src/filter.rs Apply file:, lang:, repo: Filter values against candidate DocMeta before verification to reduce PikeVm calls.
csearch-search/tests/search_tests.rs End-to-end tests against testdata/repos/medium/; differential comparison against grep -rE on a fixed corpus; result-cap enforcement.
csearch-rank/Cargo.toml Ranking crate manifest.
csearch-rank/src/lib.rs Public API: rank(results: Vec<UnrankedResult>, opts: &RankOpts) -> Vec<SearchResult>.
csearch-rank/src/score.rs RankScore computation: w1*match_density + w2*path_importance + w3*symbol_def_bonus + w4*repo_weight; weight constants; path-prefix penalty table.
csearch-rank/src/topk.rs Bounded max-heap for top-k selection; deterministic tie-break comparator: primary by score desc, secondary by (path asc, line asc) (I10).
csearch-rank/tests/rank_tests.rs Determinism tests: same input produces identical ordered output across 100 runs; tie-break regression cases.
csearch-incremental/Cargo.toml Incremental indexing crate manifest; depends on csearch-index, csearch-shard, csearch-ingest.
csearch-incremental/src/lib.rs Public API: update(manifest: &ManifestPath, repos: &[RepoSpec]) -> Result<Manifest>.
csearch-incremental/src/change_detect.rs Compare current sha256 digests and mtime against manifest's last-indexed state; produce (added, modified, deleted) file sets per repo.
csearch-incremental/src/rebuild.rs Re-index the changed file set for one repo into a fresh shard; delegates doc accumulation to csearch-index.
csearch-incremental/src/compact.rs LSM compaction: select shards below size threshold, merge their posting lists with N-way merge, write new consolidated shard, update manifest (I3, I7).
csearch-incremental/src/manifest.rs Manifest read/write; atomic swap via rename(2); ShardId registration; deleted-doc tombstone list; last-indexed state per repo.
csearch-incremental/tests/incremental_tests.rs Simulate 3 successive file-change rounds; assert no full rebuild occurs; verify search correctness after each round; test manifest atomic swap under simulated crash.
csearch-server/Cargo.toml HTTP server crate manifest; depends on axum, tokio, tower, csearch-search, csearch-incremental.
csearch-server/src/lib.rs Server setup: mmap shard registry initialisation, axum router configuration, metrics state initialisation.
csearch-server/src/routes/search.rs POST /search handler: deserialise JSON request, validate, call execute, stream or collect results, serialise JSON response.
csearch-server/src/routes/metrics.rs GET /metrics handler: Prometheus-format counters for candidates_total, verified_total, shards_touched_total, query_latency_us histogram.
csearch-server/src/routes/health.rs GET /health liveness probe; returns 200 when at least one shard is loaded.
csearch-server/src/shard_registry.rs Holds arc-ref-counted mmap Shard set; detects manifest change via inotify/kqueue + periodic stat fallback; hot-reloads without restart (P6, I7).
csearch-server/src/fanout.rs Concurrent per-shard query dispatch via tokio::spawn; collects partial results; merges under per-query deadline and result cap.
csearch-server/tests/api_tests.rs HTTP integration tests: POST /search on testdata/repos/small/; assert response schema, result count, status codes; hot-reload test under concurrent queries.
csearch-cli/Cargo.toml CLI binary crate manifest; depends on clap v4, csearch-incremental, csearch-server, csearch-search.
csearch-cli/src/main.rs Entry point: clap Command tree; dispatch to subcommand handlers.
csearch-cli/src/cmd/index.rs csearch index <path> — walk repo, build shard, update manifest; progress output; time report.
csearch-cli/src/cmd/search.rs csearch search <query> — parse query, execute across manifest shards, render results with context lines and ANSI highlighting.
csearch-cli/src/cmd/merge.rs csearch merge — trigger LSM compaction on the current manifest; report before/after shard count.
csearch-cli/src/cmd/stat.rs csearch stat — print per-shard statistics: doc count, trigram count, file size, posting-list size histogram, compression ratio.
csearch-cli/src/cmd/doctor.rs csearch doctor — verify all shard CRCs, validate manifest consistency, report deleted-doc tombstone count, suggest compaction if shard count is high.
csearch-cli/tests/cli_integration.rs End-to-end CLI tests: index testdata/repos/small/, run several search queries, compare output to expected fixtures.
benches/index_bench.rs Criterion benchmark: index build throughput (MB/s, docs/s) vs corpus size; spill threshold sensitivity.
benches/search_bench.rs Criterion benchmark: query latency (P50/P99) vs shard count, candidate set size, regex NFA size.
benches/intersect_bench.rs Criterion benchmark: galloping intersection vs naive merge at posting-list size ratios 1:1, 1:10, 1:100, 1:1000.

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Timeline base: 28 weeks for Phases 1–14. Phase durations sum to 28 weeks. All durations are calendar-week estimates for a single focused engineer; exit criteria are binary gates.

Phase Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria
Phase 1 csearch-core: all locked types (DocId, RepoId, ShardId, Trigram, Posting, MatchSpan, QueryAst, Filter, TrigramQuery, RankScore, SymbolEntry, ShardHeader, CsearchError) with property tests for Trigram bijection (I8) 1 week Byte vs UTF-8 boundary decision finalised late; newtype orphan-rule conflicts with external trait impls All property tests pass; Trigram pack/unpack roundtrip verified; cargo clippy clean
Phase 2 csearch-trigram: overlapping-window extractor, dedup, frequency map, case-fold variant, short-file guard 1 week Unicode grapheme cluster vs raw-byte disagreement; case-fold table completeness; benchmark regression Unit and property tests pass on all edge cases; extraction throughput ≥ 200 MB/s on reference hardware
Phase 3 csearch-index accumulator + csearch-shard on-disk format: ShardHeader, sorted trigram table, delta+varint posting lists, doc-meta table, CRC; mmap reader; PostingReader 2 weeks Shard format versioning not locked early; CRC field placement choice; varint off-by-one at list boundary Round-trip test: build shard, re-open, all posting lists decode correctly; CRC mismatch detected; shard-reader fuzz returns no panics
Phase 4 csearch-index full builder: walk → trigram → postings accumulate → spill → merge → shard write; bounded memory; DocMeta capture; build manifest 2 weeks Memory spill threshold tuning; deterministic DocId assignment across spill segments; manifest write atomicity Build testdata/repos/medium/ within configured memory cap; DocIds stable across separate runs; manifest written and re-readable
Phase 5 csearch-regex: Thompson NFA + Pike VM; linear-time guarantee; anchors, character classes, alternation, repetition, submatch capture 2 weeks Submatch capture slot bookkeeping complexity; anchor semantics at line vs file boundary; epsilon-cycle detection Differential test vs reference regex crate: zero discrepancies on 1 M random (pattern, input) pairs; linear-time property test passes on 20 known ReDoS patterns
Phase 6 csearch-trigquery: HIR → TrigramQuery lowering with soundness obligation (I1); boolean simplification; required-literal extraction 2 weeks Lowering of look-around-adjacent constructs (must default to All); alternation explosion producing oversized OR trees; unsound concat lowering edge case Soundness fuzz: 10 M random (pattern, doc) pairs, zero false negatives; known-answer tests pass; TrigramQuery simplification reduces tree depth
Phase 7 csearch-query: query DSL parser; literal, /regex/, file:, lang:, case:, sym:, repo: filters; precedence rules; error messages 1 week Quoting ambiguity: / as path character vs regex delimiter; unknown filter-name handling; implicit AND precedence All grammar test cases parse to correct QueryAst; error-message tests pass; malformed-input property test never panics
Phase 8 csearch-search executor: lower query → TrigramQuery → posting-list intersection (galloping) → Filter application → PikeVm verify → MatchSpans → rank; result cap; early-exit 2 weeks Incorrect filter ordering (verify before filter wastes cycles); galloping intersection off-by-one at list end; candidate over-count when multiple filters conflict Differential test vs grep -rE on testdata/repos/medium/: identical match sets; result cap enforced; P99 latency ≤ 500 ms single shard, medium corpus
Phase 9 csearch-ingest: repo walk with ignore rules, language detection, binary/minified filter, sha256 content addressing, symlink safety, encoding guard 1.5 weeks Gitignore negation edge cases; binary detection false positives on UTF-16 source files; symlink loop traversal Walk testdata/repos/medium/: correct doc count, correct language tags, zero panics on adversarial directory structures, symlink loops handled safely
Phase 10 csearch-symbol: per-language heuristic extractors (Rust/Go/Python/generic), symbol posting index, sym: query integration, ranking symbol_def_bonus 2 weeks Heuristic extractors miss common macro-generated definitions; symbol index size exceeds shard budget Fixture tests: correct SymbolEntry lists for each supported language; sym: query returns definitions; no regressions in non-symbol search latency
Phase 11 csearch-rank: RankScore composite scoring, top-k bounded heap, deterministic tie-break (I10), path-importance signal 1 week Float accumulation order causing non-determinism; path-prefix penalty table maintenance Determinism test: identical input → identical ordered output across 100 independent runs; tie-break regression suite passes
Phase 12 csearch-incremental: change detection (sha256+mtime), per-repo shard rebuild, deleted-doc tombstones (I9), LSM compaction, atomic manifest swap (I7) 2.5 weeks Manifest corruption on crash mid-rename; compaction trigger policy misconfigured (compacts too eagerly or never); shard count unbounded under adversarial update patterns Three successive file-change rounds with no full rebuild; search correctness verified post each round; manifest atomic swap tested under kill -9 at swap point
Phase 13 csearch-server: HTTP/JSON API, streaming results, concurrent fan-out with per-query deadline, manifest hot-reload, /metrics endpoint 2 weeks Fan-out result-merge ordering races; hot-reload race between manifest stat and shard open; deadline propagation through tokio tasks HTTP integration tests pass; hot-reload test: manifest swapped during 100 concurrent queries, zero incorrect results; all 4 per-query metrics emitted
Phase 14 Hardening: differential test on large (≥ 1 M file) corpus; soundness fuzz at 50 M iterations; shard-reader fuzz; latency benchmark at scale; index pack/unpack validation 2 weeks Large-corpus procurement time; fuzz infrastructure setup; performance regressions discovered late in cycle Differential test on large corpus: zero discrepancies; soundness fuzz 50 M iterations: zero false negatives; shard-reader fuzz: no panics; P99 latency ≤ 200 ms at 1 M file corpus on reference hardware; cargo test --workspace and cargo bench fully green
